Reworded **C-5** in `docs/security/calls.md` (key is now per-`(leaf, media-type)`) + a CLOSED **G-31** in known-gaps. Residual: the same G-26 live deployed-SFU RTP interop proof that governs every SFrame stack |
| [0035](docs/adr/0035-sframe-per-stream-kid-key.md) | **Per-(leaf, stream) SFrame key via the header KID — close the cross-stream AEAD nonce reuse for ANY number of streams** (Phase 3 calls, security fix). **Implemented (2026-07); cross-stack-verified on all six SFrame stacks.** **Supersedes the discriminator of [0034](docs/adr/0034-sframe-per-media-stream-key.md).** The finding: 0034's per-media-TYPE (Audio/Video) tag maps a participant's **two video streams** (camera + screen-share) to the SAME `(key, nonce_salt)` space (both are `Video` → tag `0x02`), so their independent per-stream counters re-collide the exact `(key, nonce)` reuse 0034 set out to fix — HIGH for an SFU group video call (the zero-trust SFU relays both ciphertext streams, invariant #2). A local media-type/track-kind **cannot** distinguish two same-kind streams, and a transceiver **MID differs across the SFU** (sender-side and subscriber-side MIDs are renegotiated) — so neither is a discriminator both endpoints share. **The only per-stream signal both endpoints agree on through the SFU is what rides inside the RFC 9605 header** (the SFU forwards it verbatim to route by KID without decrypting), and that field is the **KID**. **Decision:** make the KID a per-`(sender_leaf, stream)` identifier — `KID = (sender_leaf << 8) | stream_index` (`stream_index` a `u8`, audio 0 / camera 1 / screen-share 2 / …, the sender assigns it at arm time) — and derive the participant key from the FULL KID: `CallKey::participant_key(kid)` folds `kid_be(8)` into **both** HKDF `info` strings, so distinct KID ⇒ disjoint `(key, nonce_salt)` for **any** stream count per leaf. The **receiver** reads the KID off each frame's header and derives `participant_key(KID)` with **no** local media/kind signal; roster consumers recover `sender_leaf = KID >> 8` via `SframeHeader::leaf_of`/`stream_of`. `MediaType` is **removed everywhere**. The `SframeHeader` codec, the `header_decode` fuzz target, and the ADR-0013 AAD are **unchanged** (RFC 9605 §4.3 already encodes an arbitrary `u64` KID in an extended form — a leaf-3 stream-0 frame now carries the 2-byte KID `0x0300`). **Alternatives rejected:** keep the media-type tag (the finding), a second local per-track tag (not shared through the SFU, same failure as the MID), a standalone wire stream-id field (needless — the KID is already a full `u64` routing/key identifier). Flag-day wire + golden change, acceptable pre-GA for a closed ecosystem ([0011](docs/adr/0011-rust-sfu.md), no external SFrame peer). **Landed across all six stacks** — core `lakko-calls` (`kid_for`/`leaf_of`/`stream_of` + `StreamIndexOverflow` fail-closed on `stream > 255`; `participant_key(kid)` + `participant_key_for(leaf, stream)`; three-stream property test proving audio + 2 video derive pairwise-distinct keys/salts/ciphertext — the camera-vs-screen pair 0034 missed; threaded through `CallKeyRing`/`GroupCallSession`), uniffi + wasm FFI (`participantKey(sender_leaf, stream_index)` seal + `openFramedWithOverlap(kid)` open; `FfiMediaType`/`media_type` removed), native C ABI + C++ bridge (encryptor takes `stream_index`, decryptor drops `cricket::MediaType` and reads the KID from the header; `BadMediaType`/`LAKKO_SFRAME_MEDIA_TYPE_*` removed), web Encoded-Transform worker (`streamIndexOfKind` on seal, whole-KID off the wire on open), Android JNI (`SFrameMediaType.kt` deleted, `.so` rebuilt) + iOS Obj-C++ shim (`streamIndex` on attach) — all producing the **same** canonical golden wire vector `910300fe2c2af65799594a4c693ff4c9e0d49b0565756ddd7299b0945fc8adb1c9c12fced57c6937f372e6b94bdae40a` (seed `0x42`×32, leaf 3, stream_index 0 ⇒ KID `0x0300`). ADR-0034's LOW screen-share residual is **RESOLVED BY DESIGN** (screen-share = `stream_index 2`, its own key space); the only care item is the sender's stable-distinct-`stream_index`-per-stream discipline (engineering invariant, not a crypto gap). Invariants #2 (SFU cannot recover plaintext from any number of a leaf's concurrent streams; header forwarded verbatim, SFU never derives a key), #3 (SFrame keying is MLS-exporter-adjacent — ADR + property test), #4 (fail-closed `StreamIndexOverflow`, no non-E2EE fallback). Reworded **C-5** in `docs/security/calls.md` (per-`(leaf, stream)` via the KID) + **G-31** re-marked CLOSED by this ADR (fully covers the multi-video case 0034 did not). Residual: the same G-26 live deployed-SFU RTP interop proof that governs every SFrame stack |
| [0036](docs/adr/0036-auth-attested-genesis.md) | **Auth-attested KT-log genesis — PREVENT first-entry squatting** (G-30 finding-2 residual, Phase 2 key transparency). **Implemented (2026-07); all 7 sprints landed + verified this session.** ADR-0019's genesis self-signature only DETECTS a fabricated first binding — an attacker holding the transparency append secret (or a malicious discovery tier) can write a version-1 leaf binding a *victim's never-used* `account_key` to the *attacker's own* key and self-sign it with that key (a perfectly self-consistent self-sig the append gate + operator accept); the residual is caught only via CONIKS self-monitoring + the ADR-0016 fail-closed peer gate. ADR-0036 PREVENTS it. **Load-bearing decision:** auth never learns the identity pubkey at signup (the `accounts` table is auth-state-only; the Ed25519 identity key is client-generated, invariant #2), so the attestation is a **binding of the OPAQUE-authenticated account to a client-ASSERTED key** — stated plainly, NOT an independent proof the key is "right" — which is exactly sufficient because a squatter cannot OPAQUE-authenticate as the victim's `account_id`, hence cannot obtain auth's signature over the victim's fields; only the account holder can mint a genesis attestation for their own account. Additive to (never replacing) the ADR-0019 self-sig, which STAYS as belt-and-suspenders for the append-secret-NOT-leaked case. **`GenesisAttestation`** (`crates/lakko-attest/src/genesis.rs`) — a 153-byte fixed-layout Ed25519 blob `version(1) ‖ account_id(16) ‖ username_hash(32) ‖ pubkey(32) ‖ expires_at(8 BE) ‖ sig(64)`, domain `lakko/genesis-attest/v1` (distinct from `lakko/device-attest/v1` so a device-removal attestation can never be replayed as a genesis one even under the SHARED signing key — proven by a domain-crossing test), with a load-bearing **freshness split**: `verify_genesis_binding` (client, all-four-field binding, **no** freshness — the attestation is a permanent historical artifact) vs `verify_genesis_fresh` (transparency gate, `username_hash`+`pubkey` binding — it never learns `account_id` — PLUS freshness bounding the mint→append replay window). The bridge is the derivation `username_hash = BLAKE3("lakko/transparency/account/v1" ‖ account_id)` — **promoted** from discovery into `lakko_transparency::account_username_hash` (drift hazard: a one-byte-different domain would silently fail-close the gate) and pinned by a golden drift test (`a5c9754b…`). **7-sprint landing:** S1 primitive + fuzz `genesis_attestation_open`; S2 auth `POST /auth/genesis/attest` (bearer-authed via the `AuthenticatedAccount` extractor, stateless — no DB row, reuses the device-attest signing key + JWKS, TTL 3600 s) + the shared `account_username_hash` golden pin; S3 FFI `verify_genesis_attestation`/`verifyGenesisAttestation` + `account_username_hash`/`accountUsernameHash` (uniffi + wasm); S4 transparency migration `0004` opaque sidecar + the fail-closed append gate (`verify_genesis_fresh`) behind default-OFF `LAKKO_TRANSPARENCY_REQUIRE_GENESIS_ATTEST` + pinned `LAKKO_TRANSPARENCY_GENESIS_ATTEST_KEY` (loud startup diagnostics per flag×key combo); S5 discovery migration `0016` opaque sidecar, `COALESCE`-preserve threaded upload→append (invariant #2); S6 all 3 clients mint on genesis publish (soft-fail) + verify a served attestation on first observation beside the self-sig (fail-closed mismatch), reusing the pinned DeviceAttestKey. **Flag-gated rollout: default-OFF** (additive, non-breaking for existing genesis leaves); flipping it on is a deploy decision sequenced AFTER the clients mint. Invariants #2 (public key-signed artifact, no key material; transparency still never learns `account_id`), #4 (client verify fail-closed / `mismatch`, append gate fail-closed under the flag, mint failure fails the publish closed), #6 (the first binding is now account-authenticated, closing the one identity-lifecycle point the supersession chain does not cover). Reuses the audited ADR-0015 device-attestation template (primitive / signing key / JWKS / FFI verifier / pinned-key / opaque sidecar) end-to-end. Extends **C-56** in the invariant index + transitions **G-30 finding-2** from "detected" to "prevented (flag-gated)". Residual: the deploy rollout (flip the flag once clients mint) + a live cross-service e2e; operator+auth collusion out of scope (a compromised attest signing key already co-signs the root — same trust tier). Flag through `lakko-security` before flipping the gate (touches auth + transparency, invariants #2/#4/#6) |
| [0037](docs/adr/0037-backup-head-rollback-commitment.md) | **Auth-signed backup-head rollback commitment — close fresh-install backup rollback** (G-11, Phase 2 backup/recovery). **Accepted; implemented S1–S6 (2026-07).** A backup is one opaque phrase-AEAD blob per account on `services/storage` (`PUT`/`GET`, no version, no history), so an attacker controlling the storage tier can hand a restoring client an *older* blob — it opens cleanly under the same recovery phrase and silently downgrades state (resurrects revoked devices, forgets recently-accepted contacts). Nothing a client ran authenticated "this blob is the current head". **Load-bearing framing (stated plainly, neither mechanism alone suffices):** a signature proves a head **authentic**, not **current**, so the head combines two — (a) an **auth-assigned, per-account, strictly-monotone `backup_version`** (never client-supplied → a regression is structurally impossible) that a **returning client** refuses to go below via a device-key-encrypted `last_known_version` floor (HARD, no window); (b) a signed **`signed_at`** mint timestamp a **true zero-anchor fresh install** (no floor) bounds with a client-side staleness window (default 30 days). The residual is confined + stated: a within-window replay of a genuine old head is uncaught **for a zero-anchor fresh install only**; any client that ever persisted a floor is hard-protected. The prior docs claim that the "client-with-state case" was CLOSED via the v2 chain (`lakko-backup::chain`) is **corrected** — that chain is dormant (wired to no FFI/client), so it closed nothing in practice; ADR-0037 closes both cases via ONE head artifact and the chain stays out of scope (its sequence-audit role is separate). **`BackupHead`** (`crates/lakko-attest/src/backup_head.rs`) — a 129-byte fixed-layout Ed25519 blob `format_version(1) ‖ account_id(16) ‖ backup_version(8 BE u64) ‖ envelope_hash(32) ‖ signed_at_unix_millis(8 BE i64) ‖ sig(64)`, domain `lakko/backup-head/v1` (distinct from `device-attest/v1` + `genesis-attest/v1` — three-way domain-crossing test, non-interchangeable under the SHARED attest signing key it reuses; timestamps in **milliseconds**, unlike the genesis template's seconds). Two verify entry points: `verify_backup_head` (FULL: sig + `account_id` + `envelope_hash` + freshness, returns the head for the caller's floor check) + `verify_backup_head_binding` (binding only). Binds `account_id` directly (not `username_hash` — the head never touches the KT log; client/storage/auth all speak `account_id`). **6-sprint landing:** S1 primitive + fuzz `backup_head_open`; S2 auth `POST /auth/backup/head` (bearer-authed via `AuthenticatedAccount`, **STATEFUL** — the sole deviation from the ADR-0036 stateless mint: a per-account monotone counter `backup_head_versions`, migration `0015`, read-bumped in one transaction via the MariaDB `LAST_INSERT_ID` idiom; reuses the device-attest signing key + JWKS) → `{ backup_head_b64, backup_version }`; S3 FFI `verifyBackupHead` / `verifyBackupHeadBinding` / `backupEnvelopeHash` + a discriminable `BackupHeadStale` vs `Backup` error (wasm `stale:` / `invalid:` coded messages), uniffi + wasm; S4 `crates/lakko-backup/src/head_frame.rs` — a `0xB1` container-tag framing (`frame_backup_blob` / `parse_backup_blob` → `BackupBlob::{Framed, Legacy}`, byte-0 discriminator: `0xB1`+len → `Framed`, `0xB1`+short → hard `Truncated`, else → fail-open `Legacy`) embedding the head in the ONE storage blob so head + envelope swap atomically in a single `PUT` — `services/storage` **UNTOUCHED** (blob stays opaque, invariant #2, no migration) + fuzz `backup_blob_parse`; S5 web + iOS + Android mint-on-upload (soft-fail to legacy) + verify-on-restore via a pure `restoreVerdict` classifier ported identically to all three (web `head-gate.ts` / iOS `BackupHeadGate.swift` / Android `HeadGate.kt`, 12 gate tests each) — absent → **fail-open**, invalid/truncated → **hard fail-closed**, stale → **user-acknowledged override** — plus a device-key-encrypted monotone version floor; S6 this docs/security reconcile (corrects G-11, adds C-58). Invariants #1 (the head + counter table carry only `account_id` + a `BLAKE3` of ciphertext + a monotone integer + a timestamp — no PII), #2 (storage + auth opaque to key material — auth persists only a counter), #4 (fail-closed, scoped: the absent-head fail-open is a rollback-detection availability choice mirroring ADR-0033 — the envelope stays phrase-AEAD sealed regardless of the head, so no silent downgrade to non-E2EE). Reuses the audited ADR-0015 / ADR-0036 `lakko-attest` template (fixed-layout Ed25519, shared signing key + JWKS, FFI verifier, pinned `DeviceAttestKey`) end-to-end. Adds **C-58** in the invariant index + transitions **G-11** to closed-in-practice. Residual: the deferred auth-served current-version marker (the tightening path that would close the fresh-install window, declined for single-fetch simplicity) + a live recovery e2e (mint → framed PUT → restore gate) on a deployed env. Flag through `lakko-security` before merge (touches auth: a new stateful endpoint + migration + monotonic counter; the storage-blob framing; the client restore gate; invariants #1/#2/#4). lakko-security APPROVE-WITH-NOTES (no CRITICAL/HIGH, no invariant-#4 break on the live path); F1-F5b hardenings landed — F4 absent-head floor-check (returning clients hard-protected against head-stripping, residual scoped to a true zero-anchor fresh install), F5a corrupt-floor -> stale-override, F2 mandatory gate, F1 unpinned-attest-key warning, F3 (Android) Throwable-catch, F5b default-off per-account mint rate-limit (migration 0016). |
| [0038](docs/adr/0038-update-available-notice.md) | **Signed "update available" notice + store deep-link** (GA — client release / update ops). **Accepted; implemented S1–S6 (2026-07), freshness DISABLED in v1.** The store-app slice of auto-update: a store-delivered client **cannot self-swap its binary** (the store owns the update channel), so the only actionable thing it can do about a newer version is TELL the user + link to the store. (`crates/lakko-tuf` — the full binary-artifact TUF verifier — stays the right tool for the sideloaded/desktop self-swap flow, deferred + un-wired; this ADR does NOT use it.) **Not full TUF — a signed monotone-integer NOTICE + a store link.** The client fetches a small **global** (no `account_id`) `ReleaseManifest`, verifies it against a build-time-pinned **dedicated publisher** key, compares its own build-time `CURRENT_RELEASE` u64, and — if behind — shows a dismissible banner. **`ReleaseManifest`** (`crates/lakko-attest/src/release_manifest.rs`) — the FOURTH fixed-layout Ed25519 kind, an 89-byte blob `format_version(1) ‖ latest_release(8 BE u64) ‖ min_supported_release(8 BE u64) ‖ signed_at_unix_millis(8 BE i64) ‖ sig(64)`, domain `lakko/release-manifest/v1` (distinct from device-attest + genesis + backup-head — a four-way `domains_do_not_cross` test), reusing the audited `lakko-attest` template but the odd one out: **GLOBAL** (no per-user field — the simplest primitive in the crate: no auth endpoint, no state, no migration) + signed by a dedicated **OFFLINE publisher** release key (NOT the shared auth attest key — the deliberate inverse of ADR-0037 §3, signing-role isolation), only ever **client-verified**. `verify_release_manifest` (sig + caller-controlled freshness; `i64::MAX` DISABLES the staleness gate) + the pure `release_status`/`release_status_for` → `UpdateStatus { UpToDate, UpdateAvailable, UpdateRequired }` (`min_supported` takes precedence). Fuzz `release_manifest_open`. **6-sprint landing:** S1 primitive + fuzz; S2 FFI `verifyReleaseManifest` → `VerifiedReleaseManifest { latestRelease, minSupportedRelease, signedAtMillis }` + `releaseUpdateStatus` + `UpdateStatus` (uniffi + wasm; **verify-only — NO sign on the FFI**; `FfiError::Release` / wasm `release:`-coded, no stale-vs-invalid split — fail toward UpToDate); S3 `tools/release-manifest` (new workspace-member bin `lakko-release-manifest`, crate `lakko-release-manifest-tool` — `keygen` writes a `0600` seed + prints the base64url pubkey, `mint` writes the RAW 89-byte blob + self-verifies) + justfile `gen-release-key` / `mint-release-manifest`; served as the RAW blob at `/.well-known/lakko-release` (`no-store`, nginx `alias` to a **SEPARATE** root `/var/www/lakko-release/` so `deploy-web`'s `rsync --delete` cannot wipe it) + `infra/README.md` runbook; S4 iOS (`apps/ios/Lakko/Updates/`) + Android (`.../updates/` + `.../ui/updates/`) — build-time pinned `LakkoReleaseKey` / `LAKKO_RELEASE_KEY` + manifest URL + a **single default-empty store URL** (`LakkoAppStoreUrl` / `LAKKO_PLAY_STORE_URL`) + `CURRENT_RELEASE` (default 1) → a throttled launch fetch (via the ADR-0026 anti-censorship transport when armed) → verify (freshness disabled) → `releaseUpdateStatus` → a dismissible informational banner cloning `IdentitySecurityBanner` with a store deep-link, + a pure `UpdateBannerDecision` cadence policy; S5 web (`apps/web/src/update/release-check.ts`) — the shared wasm verify core, **DORMANT** (web keeps `SwUpdateBanner`, no new banner — no store to link to); S6 this docs reconcile. **DEFAULT-EMPTY → DORMANT** everywhere (no pinned key → no fetch/verify/banner; no store URL → the Update button is hidden). **Fail-toward-UpToDate on ANY error** (unpinned / fetch / non-2xx / verify / stale) — an **availability / anti-phishing** control (the signature stops a FORGED nudge; a suppress/replay only hides an unobtrusive nag — LOW + accepted, `docs/security/known-gaps.md` G-32), **NOT** an E2EE control: its "fail-safe" is *fail toward no nag*, distinct from invariant #4's fail-closed-refuse. `UpdateRequired` is carried in the wire (`min_supported_release`) but **no blocking UI is built in v1** (renders the same dismissible banner). Invariants #1 (global — no PII), #2 (a public Ed25519-signed static artifact, no key material; nginx parses nothing), #4 (adjacent, not touched — no E2EE path). Residuals (honest): no production store ids exist yet (every id is `me.lakko.dev`) → the store button stays dormant until minted; the Android `.so` needs a cargo-ndk rebuild for the release FFI symbols at CI/deploy. `lakko-security` pass optional (a static signed integer + a pinned verifier, no message-path / key-material change). Status Accepted. |
| [0039](docs/adr/0039-kt-log-witness-quorum.md) | **KT-log witness quorum — K-of-N co-signing** (G-30 finding-1, the operator+witness collusion residual). **Accepted; implemented S1–S5 (2026-07-21).** **Extends [ADR-0020](docs/adr/0020-witness-cosigning.md)** (does NOT supersede — the single-witness mechanism, the Merkle-consistency defence, the 105-byte cosig blob, the domain separator `lakko/transparency/witness/v1`, the co-sign-on-read model, and the fail-closed client gate all carry through verbatim); realises the "K-of-N a config slot for later" ADR-0020 explicitly parked. ADR-0020 closed split-view for the SINGLE-witness model, but an operator that also controls (or compromises) its one pinned witness can still fork — it signs two divergent valid roots AND gets both co-signed, and every client's local checks pass on each fork. **Decision:** require **≥ K valid co-signatures from DISTINCT keys in a build-time-pinned witness key SET of size N**; enforcement stays client-side + fail-closed, the server fans out best-effort. **S1 primitive** — `crates/lakko-transparency/src/witness.rs` `verify_witness_quorum(cosigs, pinned_keys, threshold, tree_root, log_size)`: a thin loop over `verify_witness_cosig` counting **DISTINCT pinned keys satisfied** (never cosigs — the load-bearing dedup-by-key crux: the cosig list is attacker-influenceable, so a replayed / duplicated cosig satisfies at most the one key it is signed by and contributes exactly one, and a duplicate pinned-key entry counts once, so a single compromised witness can never flood the list to fake a quorum), fail-closed on `threshold == 0` (`WitnessQuorumBadThreshold` — a 0-of-N quorum would trust an unwitnessed root) + `WitnessQuorumNotMet { achieved, required }` below K; **N=1/K=1 is byte-for-byte today's single-witness gate**. 12 property tests (K-valid → Ok; K−1 → refuse; replayed/duplicate cosig + duplicate pinned key counted once; wrong `(tree_root, log_size)` / non-pinned key never counts; `threshold==0` / `threshold>N` refused; 1-of-1 ≡ `verify_witness_cosig`) + fuzz `verify_witness_quorum`. **S2 FFI** — `verifyWitnessQuorum` verify-only on UniFFI (`Vec<Vec<u8>>` cosigs + pinned keys) + wasm (flat-concatenated 105-byte cosigs / 32-byte keys); no sign on the FFI. **S3+S3b transparency N-witness fan-out** — `witness_client.rs` `list_from_env` reads a LIST (`LAKKO_TRANSPARENCY_WITNESS_URLS` + `_COSIGN_SECRETS`, comma-separated, 1:1 positional, empty-entry fail-closed) with a backward-compat singular `LAKKO_TRANSPARENCY_WITNESS_URL`/`_SECRET` fallback (nothing set → empty list → dormant); `root_with_cosig` best-effort collects a BUNDLE (a down / refusing witness is simply absent, never blocking, never a stale per-witness cosig); `RootRes.witness_cosigs_b64: Vec<String>` (clean REPLACE of the singular field — licensed by the greenfield state: no witness is deployed in prod, so no client pins a single-witness key to stay compatible with); migration `0005` widens the single-row `witnessed_root.cosig` → a concatenated `cosigs VARBINARY(8192)` bundle (chunked back on load, every entry exactly 105 bytes) AND adds a per-witness `witness_high_water` table (S3b **anti-stranding**: each witness i is handed a consistency proof from ITS OWN last-co-signed position, advanced only on its own success, so a witness that was down during a partial-success growth round auto-rejoins on the next growth instead of being permanently stranded below the shared base). **`services/witness` UNTOUCHED** — N witnesses = N independent instances of the existing binary; K-of-N needs no witness-service code change. **S4 clients** — pin a key SET + threshold (`VITE_LAKKO_TRANSPARENCY_WITNESS_KEYS` + `_THRESHOLD` / Info.plist `LakkoTransparencyWitnessKeys` + `…Threshold` / `BuildConfig.LAKKO_TRANSPARENCY_WITNESS_KEYS` + `_THRESHOLD`), decode the cosig list, run the quorum gate (`witnessGateRefuses`: empty pinned set → gate OFF operator-only posture; else `verifyWitnessQuorum` → throw → fail-closed `mismatch`) in both `verifyIdentityInLog` + `verifyRotationChain`, with a backward-compat single-key fallback. **Off-by-default** everywhere (unpinned → operator-only; a pinned client fails closed until the server serves ≥ K). **S5** — this docs reconcile + a 2-of-3 witness-quorum e2e in `ci/recovery-e2e` (built; CI/Docker-gated). **The load-bearing HONEST caveat:** this builds the K-of-N MECHANISM; the collusion-resistance it buys is only real once the N witnesses are **independently operated in distinct trust domains** — a DEPLOY / OPS property, not code. A single operator running all N witnesses gains nothing against operator+witness collusion (it IS every witness). So the deliverable is bounded: the threshold is ready in code + proven by the 2-of-3 e2e; collusion-resistance materialises when independent witness operators exist. Refines **C-57** (single-witness → K-of-N — a strengthening of the existing indexed invariant, NOT a new C-number) + transitions **G-30 finding-1** to K-of-N-implemented, the residual now operator + K COLLUDING witnesses (closed in practice only under independent operation). Invariants #2 (the cosig bundle + consistency proofs are public artefacts over public roots, no key material) / #4 (root gate now needs operator AND ≥ K distinct witnesses; sub-threshold / absent → fail-closed; `threshold==0` / `>N` fail-closed) / #5 (each witness persists only `(tree_root, log_size)` high-water, never accounts) / #6 (rotation anchored to a root non-forkable without K-of-N collusion). Residual: independent-operator recruitment (ops) + the deferred client-gossip / cross-check layer (the witness-trust-free split-view defence, a separate future ADR). Status Accepted. |
| [0040](docs/adr/0040-durable-pending-commit-outbox.md) | **Durable pending-commit outbox for crash-safe MLS commits** (G-30-adjacent client-durability class, **G-35**). **Accepted; implemented S1–S5 (2026-07) end-to-end on web + iOS + Android; G-35 CLOSED.** A crypto-misuse audit found the four MLS group-membership **commit** paths (add / remove / role-change / external-join approve) share the same publish-before-persist ordering the G-34 T2 fix closed for the **send** path — but with a **worse, deterministic** failure mode. Each path's `lakko-mls` create-commit **eagerly merges** epoch N → N+1 into the **in-memory** OpenMLS provider *inside* the create call, then the client publishes the commit (+ a `WelcomeRendezvous` for add / approve) to the DS, then persists the N+1 snapshot **best-effort**. A crash **or** a silent persist failure **between publish and persist** makes the commit durable on the DS (peers advance to N+1) while the local snapshot rolls back to N; on restart the lost in-memory own-echo-suppression set lets the poll loop re-fetch the device's own commit, which OpenMLS **unconditionally rejects** (`own_leaf == sender_leaf` → `CannotDecryptOwnMessage`, `openmls-0.8.1/src/framing/validation.rs:111`) — a **silent, deterministic, single-device membership brick**, recoverable only by another admin re-adding the device (+ a residual 2^-32 handshake-ratchet nonce-reuse if the bricked device issues a second N-commit). **The load-bearing point:** the naive persist-before-publish reorder that fixed G-34 T2 makes this **worse** — a commit is a group-wide N→N+1 barrier that **cannot be skipped**, so a bare reorder flips the desync to device-**AHEAD** (durable N+1, commit never on the wire, bytes unrecoverable from the merged snapshot) → strands the **whole group**. The fix needs **durability of the pending commit**, not an ordering change. **Decision (Option A, client-only, no `lakko-mls` core / FFI change):** keep the eager merge; persist a per-`(account, group)` **outbox entry** holding the commit wire-bytes **and** the Welcome / rendezvous wire-bytes (two-artifact coverage), un-acked, **together with** the N+1 snapshot **before any publish**, **fail-closed** (a failed write aborts with no publish → clean rollback to N); publish; mark acked; on restart **re-publish** un-acked entries **idempotently** (peers at N+1 drop the wrong-epoch duplicate; the committer re-arms its echo filter), so every crash-window outcome is either "clean N, no op" or "N+1 with a re-publishable commit". **Open Question 1 (step-2 atomicity) resolved per-platform:** web = one atomic IndexedDB `transaction([snapshots, commit-outbox])` (so "N+1 snapshot with no entry" is impossible, no torn-write reconciliation needed); iOS / Android have no cross-store transaction, so **entry-first-then-snapshot** + the restart sweep **discards** any un-acked entry whose durably-restored `Group.epoch()` did not reach the entry's `target_epoch` (a torn write whose commit was never published → drop the orphan, stay at N clean). **Open Question 2 (orphan `messageId`) resolved** via the already-present receive-loop benign-drop of a re-fetched own commit (`CannotDecryptOwnMessage`: web catch-and-continue, iOS `ChatClient.swift:247` catch-all, Android `ChatClient.kt:277` `FfiException` catch) rather than recording the id in the durable write. **Landed:** web `apps/web/src/chat/commit-outbox.ts` + `mls-store.ts` `saveSnapshotWithCommitEntry` — reached through `ChatContext.tsx`'s `persistSnapshotWithCommit`, which the commit paths in `contacts/client.ts` call as the injected `onPersistWithCommit` / `persistCommitOrThrow`, so the route layer owns the transaction and the client owns the ordering — plus `sweepPendingCommits` on the `Chat.tsx` restart seam. **Post-ADR-0045-S7 there are THREE such paths on web, not four** (add / remove / role-change): an external-join approve is now a proposal, so it advances no epoch and has nothing to record; iOS `apps/ios/Lakko/Chat/CommitOutbox.swift` (one JSON file per commit under Application Support, backup-excluded) + `ChatRepository` reorder + `planSweep` + once-per-session sweep at `pollGroup`; Android `apps/android/.../chat/CommitOutbox.kt` (file-per-entry under `noBackupFilesDir`) + `runCrashSafeCommit` + `reconcileVerdict` + `sweepPendingCommits` at group-open. The two-artifact split: add-path Welcome → recipient **mailbox** (no echo re-arm), approve-path rendezvous → **group journal** (echo re-arm). Purge the outbox on recover / forgetDevice. **Alternatives rejected:** Option B (defer the eager merge in the MLS core until publish is confirmed — cleaner but touches the sacrosanct MLS core + FFI, deferred as a future refinement); the bare reorder (whole-group brick); do-nothing (silent brick). Invariants #1 / #2 (the outbox stores opaque commit + Welcome public artefacts — no plaintext / PII / key material), #3 (no `lakko-mls` state-machine change — orchestration over the audited create-commit + publish paths), #4 (strengthened — no group-epoch advance published without durable local recording of the commit; fail-closed abort). Verified firsthand: web `tsc` + vitest 338 (15 new) + vite build; iOS `xcodebuild` build + test 12/12; Android `:app:compileDebugKotlin` + `CommitOutboxTest` 10/10. Residual: a live crash-injection / power-loss multi-device e2e (a runtime gate, deferred like G-26). **Follow-on finding (2026-08, minted as G-38):** the outbox closes the crash window but NOT a second route to the same device-ahead end state — two membership commits **in flight to the DS at once**. The hazard is not "two commits on the same epoch" (no create region has a suspension point, so the second correctly reads N+1); it is that the DS orders the journal by **arrival**, so an overlap can land commit(N+1 → N+2) first, peers drop it as wrong-epoch and settle at N+1 while the committer sits at N+2 — and the outbox cannot repair it, because both publishes succeeded, both entries are acked and the sweep has nothing to re-publish. Closed by a per-group **serializing** (never coalescing) lock spanning create-through-publish on all three clients — Android's two-tier lock, iOS `ChatRepository.withCommitLock` (a stored task chain modelled on `PushGroupRouting.routingChain`, since `@MainActor` releases isolation at the `await`), and on web what was `withGroupCommitLock` in `contacts/client.ts` is now **`withGroupMlsLock` in `apps/web/src/mls/group-lock.ts`** — still module-level for the same reason (per-route `ContactsClient` instances share one canonical wasm `Identity`), but widened past commits to cover the send path and the polled-commit apply as well, because ADR-0045 made an epoch straddle during a send a certainty rather than a race. **The residual this row recorded as "unfixed in G-38" is closed:** a poll applying a **remote** commit while a local one is mid-publish needed "a DS-side epoch gate or proposal-based commits", and [ADR-0045](docs/adr/0045-remote-commit-epoch-fork.md) built the second (the first is argued against there and deliberately not built). Status Accepted. |
| [0041](docs/adr/0041-ios-silent-push-nse-decrypt.md) | **iOS silent-push background decrypt in the NotificationServiceExtension** (silent-push arc — hard-platform slice). **Accepted; Phases 0-3 landed end-to-end on iOS — now at parity with web + Android; `lakko-security` APPROVE-WITH-NOTES on Phase 1 (LOW-1 fixed) + Phase 3 + the web sibling.** Web (commit `148753b`) + Android (`ae9a087`) already show the real decrypted message preview on a push wake via a **read-only throwaway MLS decrypt** (restore a temp `Group` from the last-persisted snapshot → read-only DS fetch after the app cursor → decrypt via the audited `processIncomingVerifyingRoles`/`parseBodyEnvelope` → show preview → **DISCARD**, never persist / never advance the cursor, so the foreground app's ratchet is never desynced — the G-34 class is avoided; `lakko-security` **APPROVE-WITH-NOTES**, the discipline is airtight: in-memory provider with no persistent handle + zero write transactions + never calls `snapshot_storage`). iOS is the hard platform: the NSE runs in a **separate ~30 MB-budget process**, all five decrypt inputs were app-private, and `lakko-ffi-uniffi` is a 333 MB monolith with no feature seam. **Phase 0 (done):** the server-side APNs `apns-push-type: background`→`alert` fix (`services/push-gw/src/providers/apns.rs` — the body was already the mutable-alert NSE shape; the mismatch likely broke even the shipped `threadIdentifier` grouping). **Phase 1 (done): App Group storage migration** — a shared Keychain access group on both app + NSE targets + the five inputs (AccountIdentity seed, MLS snapshot + wrap key, message-log cursor + wrap key, known-groups) relocated into `group.me.lakko.dev.shared` via a **copy-not-move, never-delete-old, idempotent, read-new-fallback-to-old** migration (`apps/ios/Lakko/Shared/{SharedStorage,KeychainWrapKey,SharedStorageMigration}.swift`); the seed write falls back to the app-private item so it is never left unpersisted, and the wrap key re-mints only when absent from BOTH groups (existing envelopes stay decryptable). **The load-bearing decision:** the AccountIdentity seed (the `Identity.fromSeed` root, the highest-sensitivity on-device secret) must become NSE-readable — on-device MLS decrypt is impossible without it; mitigations: it stays hardware-backed + `AfterFirstUnlockThisDeviceOnly` + no iCloud + team-scoped access group (only the first-party NSE), so invariant **#7**'s non-extractable/hardware-backed property holds and only the process read-scope widens app → {app, NSE}. **Phase 2 (`5ea5c1a`): slim decrypt-only `crates/lakko-ffi-extension` → `LakkoCoreExtension.xcframework`** ({lakko-mls, lakko-attachments} only — `cargo tree` confirms tokio / transport / calls / channels / sfu-proto / call-jwt all absent, 193 crates vs the full surface's 356); the go/no-go measurement resolved cleanly — the dead-stripped linked decrypt footprint is **~1.6 MB** (~1.34 MB mapped) vs the 333 MB monolith, an order of magnitude under the NSE ceiling, so the NSE links the slim framework, not `LakkoCore`. **Phase 3 (`714d019`): the NSE read-only throwaway decrypt handler** (`apps/ios/LakkoNotificationService/{NsePushDecrypt,NseDecryptDeps}.swift` + the extended `NotificationService.swift`) mirroring web/Android — a fresh throwaway `Identity.fromSeed` → `restoreStorage` → `loadGroup`, a read-only `URLSession` DS fetch after the cursor, `processIncomingVerifyingRoles`/`parseBodyEnvelope` for the newest preview, then DISCARD; the no-write property is enforced **structurally in three layers** (the slim FFI has no persist method · the `NsePushDecryptDeps`/`NseThrowawayGroup` protocols expose no write path · `close()` is a no-op · the app cursor is never advanced), so the foreground app reprocesses from its own append-only cursor idempotently (the G-34-class desync is avoided). **Two Phase-3 findings resolved:** Finding 1 — an anti-censorship-**armed** user's NSE short-circuits to the generic banner rather than making a direct un-tunneled DS connection (no open residual gap); Finding 2 — the read-only fetch start is floored at the app's persisted per-group cursor. Landed invariant-index entry **C-59** (the NSE seed-sharing boundary + the three-layer no-write guarantee). Invariants #2 (server sees only ciphertext + pseudonym — the NSE only reads the DS + decrypts locally) / #3 (no MLS state-machine change — the slim crate re-exports the audited path, does not fork it) / #4 (fail-closed to the generic "New message") preserved; **#7** letter preserved (seed read-scope widens app → {app, first-party NSE}; non-extractable / hardware-backed + `AfterFirstUnlockThisDeviceOnly` + no-iCloud hold; only the derived 64-byte seed shared, not the recovery phrase, zeroed after use; reviewed via `lakko-security`). Alternatives rejected: a true silent `content-available` background push (unreliable / throttled app background execution), full-framework-in-NSE (busts the budget), not-sharing-the-seed (the null alternative — no on-device decrypt possible on iOS). Residual: the live APNs → NSE → decrypted-notification round-trip on a provisioned device is deploy-gated. |
| [0042](docs/adr/0042-push-opaque-group-routing-id.md) | **Opaque per-group push routing id — close the push social-graph leak** (Phase 3, invariant #5). **Accepted; implemented end-to-end on the core + FFI + web + iOS + Android (`0d349f6` core+FFI, `024b3c9` web, `ea2c513` iOS+Android, `981bc91` reverse-map, `7da84ee` tap deep-link, `21554f2` + `62f23ad` security fixes); `lakko-security` APPROVE-WITH-NOTES, no CRITICAL/HIGH. Landed C-60 + minted G-36.** The push subsystem routed group wakes by a 32-byte id **identical to the DS journal key**, materialising "these devices are one group" inside `services/push-gw` (the subscription row + the notify JOIN) and — worse — **in cleartext at Apple / Google** (the wake payload `g` + the APNs `thread-id`). **Decision:** give each group a stable **`push_routing_secret`** (32 CSPRNG bytes) minted **once** by the ADR-0022 **owner** and distributed in-group over an E2EE MLS application message, and route subscribe / notify / the delivered payload under **`push_gid = keyed-BLAKE3(secret, "lakko/push/group-routing-id/v1")`** instead. Because the secret is independent random input, `push_gid` is **algebraically independent** of `group_id`, so a push-gw-vs-DS cross-correlation is impossible without it. **`services/push-gw` is byte-identical UNCHANGED** (verified: `git diff 51fe05d..HEAD -- services/push-gw` is empty) — it always treated the value as an opaque `BINARY(32)` lookup key, so only the value's *meaning* shifted from "DS journal key" to "opaque routing id" (invariant #2). Core: `crates/lakko-contacts/src/push_routing.rs` (`derive_push_gid`, golden-pinned; the fixed **62-byte** `domain(29) || version || secret(32)` envelope with a bounded fail-closed codec; the `IncomingEnvelope::PushRoutingSecret` dispatch variant; the `push_routing_secret_decode` fuzz target) + uniffi/wasm FFI. **Load-bearing client rules:** a member without the secret **defers** (never falls back to the leaky `group_id` — invariant #4, no downgrade path); the **receive path is authority-gated** by a pure, host-testable decision function fed by the MLS sender leaf resolved via `members()` against the ADR-0022 role table, ranked **owner > admin > member** (identical secret accepted from anyone; owner may always rotate, evaluated before reading what is stored so priority is arrival-order-independent; admin may **seed** a *provably*-absent secret but never overwrite; plain member / unreadable role table / unresolvable sender all refused fail-closed) — this was `lakko-security` **MEDIUM-1** (the receive path originally accepted a secret from ANY member, letting one non-owner unilaterally rotate the routing id), fixed in `21554f2` then widened in `62f23ad` to fix a real delivery regression (ADR-0022 lets an admin add members, so an admin's redistribute is an admin-added member's ONLY source of the secret). **MEDIUM-3** — four source files carried a literal NUL as the storage-key separator, so git saw them as binary and `grep -r` skipped them silently; replaced with source-level escapes, byte-identical at runtime. The ADR-0041 / C-59 background decrypt (web SW / iOS NSE / Android FMS) + the Android notification tap reverse-map `push_gid -> (account, group_id)` through a plaintext **local** index; the DS fetch keeps the **real** `group_id`, so the read-only-throwaway discipline is unchanged. **Honest scope (G-36):** this removes the linkage to the DS group identity, **NOT** the clustering — push-gw still stores device-to-routing-id rows and the providers still get a **stable** per-group pseudonym in cleartext, and since `push_gid` never rotates a provider can track that cluster + its churn for the group's lifetime. Three further residuals: no rotation on membership change (a removed member keeps a valid `push_gid`), compounded because **per-group unsubscribe is dead code on all three clients** so an ex-member's subscription keeps receiving wakes; a malicious **admin can seed a bogus secret** to a member holding none, denying its wakes until the owner rotates (bounded — foreground polling still delivers); and **legacy / no-role-table groups get no push at all** (no owner ⇒ nobody mints ⇒ everyone defers) — the privacy-preferring choice, but a silent functional regression. Also found: nothing in `apps/web/src` reads the `?wake=` deep-link param the SW writes, so a **web** notification tap has never opened the chat (the SW comment claiming an SPA reader is stale; Android's tap deep-link does work). Invariants #2 (server opaque) / #3 (untouched — no `lakko-mls` change) / #4 (defer, never downgrade) / #5 (**improved but not fully satisfied**) |
| [0043](docs/adr/0043-push-routing-secret-rotation-on-removal.md) | **Rotate the push routing secret on member removal + make per-group unsubscribe live** (G-36 residual 2). **Accepted; implemented end-to-end on web + iOS + Android (`935250a` web reference, `a6ed544` the two native mirrors); landed C-61 + refined C-60.** **Extends [ADR-0042](docs/adr/0042-push-opaque-group-routing-id.md)** — the `push_gid` derivation, the 62-byte envelope, the defer-never-downgrade posture and the byte-identical `services/push-gw` all carry through; it revisits exactly one property ADR-0042 ratified ("the secret does not rotate on member removal in V1"). **Client-only: `git diff 5665018..a6ed544 -- crates/ services/ schemas/` is empty**, so `lakko-contacts` is untouched. Before this, a removed member kept a valid `push_gid` and — because nothing tore its subscription down — **kept being woken for a group it had been evicted from**, learning "this group was active at time T" indefinitely; compounded because per-group `unsubscribeGroup` was **dead code on all three clients**. **Maintainer-ratified option (b): the owner OR an ADMIN may rotate** (the ADR itself recommended the narrower (a)). The gate's admin row flipped from a refusal to an acceptance — an admin may now **OVERWRITE** a stored secret, not only seed a provable hole (web renamed the verdict `reject-admin-overwrite` -> **`accept-admin-rotate`**; both natives keep a binary accept/reject and just flip the row). Everything else about the ADR-0042 gate is unchanged and still pinned in code: identical bytes accepted from **any** sender *before any authority is consulted*, **owner evaluated first** so it wins regardless of arrival order, plain member refused, no-role-table group **accepted** (the legacy/open case — distinct from the *rotation* decision, which refuses it), unreadable role table / unresolvable sender refused fail-closed. **The bound that keeps (b) safe: rotation NEVER MINTS** — a device holding no secret is a no-op, so mint authority stays **owner-only** and the `push_gid` cannot diverge into two; (b) widened *overwrite* authority only. **Android additionally DELETED the strip-admins-on-unreadable-store rule** from the ADR-0042 MEDIUM-1 hardening — it narrowed the admin set on a store-read failure so a null read could not pass as a fillable hole, but under (b) an admin may overwrite whatever is stored, so keeping it would refuse the very admin rotation (b) exists to permit, on nothing more than a transient store error; **a deliberate narrowing-removal of a prior security hardening**, recorded as such (who may write is still role-table-decided and still fail-closed). **Trigger:** a membership **SHRINK** detected as a **set difference over member Ed25519 signature keys** — not counts, not leaf indices, because OpenMLS reuses a vacated leaf for the next Add, so remove+add in one poll batch leaves both unchanged. Four decision outcomes (+ a fifth IO-only `skip-no-secret`); a torn `members()` read or an empty baseline is `skip-unknown-membership`, **never** "everyone left"; a no-role-table group never rotates; growth never fires. Two entry points per client (the device's own remove path, and someone else's removal observed via a before/after diff around the poll batch). **Two load-bearing orderings:** the rotation broadcast strictly **FOLLOWS** the Remove commit (so the new secret is sealed at the post-remove epoch and RFC 9420 forward secrecy denies it to the evicted leaf — pinned as **C-61**), and the **OLD secret is read BEFORE** the in-place overwrite so the teardown can still name the retiring `push_gid`. **Receive-side teardown** (what keeps rotation from being a net loss): a non-rotating member accepting a **changed** secret retires its own old gid (unsubscribe + delete the route row) — without it every member would leave one live push-gw row per rotation, i.e. a live cluster **plus an archive of every previous one**, which the ADR calls strictly worse than not rotating. Gives `unsubscribeGroup` its **first real caller** on all three, adds the per-gid route delete none of the three stores had, and a per-group in-flight rotation lock. **Honest scope (G-36):** the evicted member's push-gw row is **never deleted** — nobody else can delete it and no client has "you were removed" handling — it goes **INERT** (nothing ever notifies the old gid again); that inertness is the win, not cleanup. The **cluster residual survives**: rotation *renames* the cluster, but a provider re-links across the boundary by token-set intersection and the set *difference* even **names the evicted device** — arguably more legible than the stable id was; still needs per-device-blinded ids. Five further residuals: the admin DoS **widens** from seed to churn (an admin can churn the whole group's routing id, and can keep re-establishing a shared id after each owner rotation); legacy / no-role-table groups still get no push and now also never rotate; the old gid can be **orphaned** by a transient store-read failure or a rotation/receive interleaving (not only a crash) — accepted, inert, the prescribed durable pending-rotation record deliberately **NOT** built (its premise went stale: the `distributed` flag + any-holder re-broadcast from `5729aa0`/`9cb8f06`/`7e7d143` already covers the broadcast half); and concurrent rotators can strand a device on a superseded secret (under (b) one removal yields N+1 rotations), self-healing only at the next rotation; and — the one whose consequence is **confidentiality** rather than wake delivery — a stale `pending` re-broadcast silently and **permanently** undoing a rotation (re-review **F-A**, G-36 residual 9). **`lakko-security` (mandatory for option (b)): DONE — APPROVE-WITH-NOTES, no CRITICAL/HIGH, no invariant #2/#3/#4 break on the live path.** Six findings: **F1** (MEDIUM, blocking) — the coalescing rotation lock on web + iOS folded a **second, distinct** removal into an in-flight rotation, handing the second evictee the new secret (Android's serializing `Mutex` was already correct) — **fixed** on both by adopting Android's lock; **F2** (MEDIUM) — concurrent rotators can strand a device on a superseded secret (under (b) one removal yields N+1 rotations; a rotator applies its own mint at *local* time, not at its journal position) — recorded, web-only stale-role sub-case fixed; **F3** (LOW) — the C-61 ordering has **no test on any client** (structural enforcement only), contrary to the ADR's own "must be asserted in a test"; **F4** (LOW, web) — the receive-side teardown can precede the new subscription; **F5** (LOW) — a raw NUL committed in `apps/web/src/push/wake-target.test.ts` (a recurrence of the ADR-0042 MEDIUM-3 class), being fixed; **F6** — these doc corrections. **Pre-merge RE-REVIEW: APPROVE WITH NOTES / merge-ready**, four further non-blocking findings: **F-A** (the substantive one — **a stale `pending` re-broadcast can silently UNDO a rotation, permanently**, with no malice and no crash: a device whose post-add redistribute broadcast failed holds the pre-rotation secret in a `pending` row, another admin rotates to `S1`, and the first device re-broadcasts the **old** secret before applying `S1` — every member accepts it and **retires `gid(S1)`**, migrating the group back onto the id the evicted member still derives, and with no further shrink there is no further rotation, so it is **permanent**. **Root cause: the receive gate has no recency or monotonicity notion at all** — `routingSecretVerdict` and its twins ask only *who sent it*, never *is it newer*, so a retry of the current secret and a replay of a superseded one are byte-indistinguishable. Web narrows the window by awaiting `refresh()` before provisioning, though `refresh()` swallows its own errors; iOS + Android provision on independent tasks, so it is **more** reachable there. **Not blocking — the worst case is *exactly* the pre-ADR-0043 status quo plus inert orphan rows; it fails TO the old behaviour, never below it.** Fix direction, unbuilt: carry a **monotone counter / minting epoch in the 62-byte envelope** and refuse a non-increasing value — a `crates/lakko-contacts` wire change — or, narrowly, clear the `pending` re-broadcast once a *different* secret has been accepted; recorded as **G-36 residual 9**); **F-D** (LOW, availability — the stale-role window is **narrowed, not eliminated**, on all three: the role is captured *before* the rotation queues behind a predecessor, so a demotion landing during the queue wait rotates on a stale role, peers refuse, and the rotator strands itself; self-heals at the next authorised envelope); **F-E** (LOW — rotation and receive are not mutually excluded, so a peer envelope interleaving a local rotation can leave an intermediate orphan gid; same class as the existing orphan residual, and never an id an evicted member knows since every candidate is post-removal); **F-G** (the ADR's and G-36's residual numbering had diverged at 6/7 — now aligned). Two explicit review judgements: accepting the orphan-gid residual is defensible and **the durable record should NOT be built**; and `/push/group/{subscribe,unsubscribe}` carry **no membership check / no ownership proof**, so "no remaining member can tear down the ex-member's row" rests on the device pseudonym never being published, not on server-side authorization. Invariants #2 (server opaque, push-gw byte-identical) / #3 (untouched) / #4 (defer, never downgrade; gate fail-closed) / #5 (**further improved, still not fully satisfied**) |
| [0044](docs/adr/0044-push-routing-secret-recency.md) | **Mint-epoch recency ordering for the push routing secret — close the stale-replay rotation undo** (G-36 residual 9). **Status: ACCEPTED.** Built end-to-end on the core + FFI + web + iOS + Android (`936d849` core+FFI+web reference, `014e84e` the two native mirrors, `9a0f539` the serializing lock, plus the two review follow-ups) and the flag day is over. **The path was not straight and the record is kept, because the lesson is.** The `lakko-security` review of the FIRST landing returned **REQUEST CHANGES on a blocking HIGH and reproduced the defect by execution** on web — two envelopes driven concurrently the way `Chat.tsx` actually drives them gave verdicts `['accept-admin-rotate', 'accept-identical']` and a final stored secret of `0xEE` **at epoch 10**, the **stale** one. The ordering primitive was correct and the gate was armed; the **receive path was an unserialized read-modify-write dispatched fire-and-forget per envelope** on every client, so two envelopes from one poll batch both decided against the same **pre-batch snapshot** and the gate was **bypassed, not defeated** (**G-36 residual 10**). **`9a0f539` closed it**: ONE per-`(account, group)` **serializing** (never coalescing) lock spanning the whole read-decide-write, **shared** across FOUR entry points — receive, rotate, ensure, **redistribute** (included beyond the brief because it is the same read-then-write and unserialized it puts a PRE-rotation secret back on the wire mid-rotation; sharing one lock rather than several also closes the recorded **F-E** residual). Verified by **execution** on web + Android (both reproduced the defect pre-fix, signature `expected 'accept-identical' to be 'reject-stale'`; Android's concurrency is genuinely PARALLEL — `pushScope` is `Dispatchers.IO` — so a confined dispatcher would have MASKED it, and Android **proved the lock must be SHARED** by probing with receive on its own lock: F-A and N-rotation passed while F-E still failed). **On iOS the batch race was REFUTED, not fixed** — the span from the `getRecord` read to the persist has **no suspension point** (both `await`s sit after the write) and `PushRoutingStore` has **no `async` at all**, so `@MainActor` already made it mutually exclusive; the lock was added anyway because that safety was **incidental** and one added `await` would have reopened it silently. iOS was NOT defect-free: its rotation parks in its broadcast, so the rotate-vs-receive interleave (**F-E**) was real there. **`lakko-security` on the fix: APPROVE WITH NOTES**, no CRITICAL/HIGH, and both actionable findings are closed — **MEDIUM (web): the lock was per-JS-realm, not cross-tab** (IndexedDB is shared across tabs, so two tabs on one account were unserialized and in a narrow interleaving reach the F-A confidentiality shape; now a **Web Lock** (`navigator.locks`) of the same per-row key **nests inside** the in-process queue, which is deliberately **KEPT** because `navigator.locks` is secure-context-only — its absence is a real deployment state, so a single mechanism would degrade to a **silent unserialized run**, and the queue is also what captures ordering **synchronously** so a burst in one turn runs in DS journal order); **LOW (all three): the lock was held across unbounded network I/O** (now every call taken under it is individually bounded — web 30 s, natives 15 s; Android additionally bounds `freshMintStamp()` at **both** sites that await it under the lock, `rotate` and `ensure`'s MINT branch, for the **G-37** reason below). **The availability decision resolves in TWO directions at once:** the **operation fails CLOSED** (a timed-out broadcast is never flagged distributed, a timed-out unsubscribe never claims the id was retired; nothing about *who may write* or *which write survives* is relaxed — both gates still run in full inside the lock against a fresh read) while the **row fails OPEN** (the lock is released and the queue proceeds, because the work queued behind a wedged row is precisely the peer rotation that retires an evicted member's id). **This is NOT ADR-0033's fail-open**, which relaxes an anti-abuse *gate*; here no gate is relaxed. **Correction to the ADR's own earlier framing:** a wedged row is not merely an availability problem — the row that never unwedges is the row that never applies a peer's rotation, so the device goes on notifying an id an evicted member still derives, which is the F-A consequence reached through availability and makes bounding it the safe side on **both** axes. **Landed C-62 with a fifth sub-invariant — the gate must run against CURRENT state** (a total order is only meaningful if the gate consuming it reads current state; the single most important lesson of this arc, pinned rather than left in prose), **amended C-60 + C-61**, **closed G-36 residuals 6 (outright), 9 and 7's concurrent-rotator core (single-context case) and 5's rotate-vs-receive F-E path**, **RE-SCOPED residual 10** (not deleted — a browser without `navigator.locks` degrades to same-tab-only serialization, and the native multi-process boundary, iOS NSE / Android background decrypt versus the app, was **NOT examined** by this arc), and **minted G-37** (Android `ChatRepository.groupsLock` held across unbounded DS I/O; the push path was insulated by `boundedOrNull`, but every other `groupsLock` caller inherited the hazard — a chat-path item that merely surfaced here). **G-37 has since been CLOSED on Android (2026-08), and its framing here was corrected in the process:** the defect was not a hang hazard but the **steady state** — `groupsLock` was one mutex per *repository* and `pollGroup` awaited a deliberate 30 s long poll inside it, so an open chat screen stalled every send / commit on **every** group. Fixed by restructuring the poll (network half / non-`suspend` MLS half; a moved cursor drops the batch rather than winding backward), bounding DS I/O at the **transport** rather than with `withTimeout` (whose `CancellationException` would be swallowed by the persist-catch inside `runCrashSafeCommit` and silently become an ADR-0040 commit rollback), and narrowing to **two tiers** — per-group mutex + a global `providerLock`, because a straight per-group swap would have broken ADR-0040 (one OpenMLS provider serves every group; `snapshotStorage` can capture another group torn and `restoreStorage` rolls back **every** group). Residual: cross-group ops still serialise on the provider region. Two smaller web items **noted rather than fixed**: `clearAllGroupRouting()` (`AuthContext.tsx`, on `forgetDevice`) writes the store outside the lock — safe because it is a whole-store teardown, not a per-row read-modify-write, and leaves the state the entry points already handle defensively; and the service workers touch the same database but only the `routes` store, only in `readonly` transactions, so they need no lock. **Extends [ADR-0042](docs/adr/0042-push-opaque-group-routing-id.md) AND [ADR-0043](docs/adr/0043-push-routing-secret-rotation-on-removal.md) — supersedes neither.** The `push_gid` derivation and its golden, the 29-byte domain separator, the E2EE MLS application-message carrier, the defer-never-downgrade posture, the **owner-only mint** rule, the authority gate's rank (owner > admin > member) and its whole verdict matrix, and the byte-identical `services/push-gw` all carry through **verbatim** (verified: `git diff 820230d..HEAD -- services/push-gw` is empty; `crates/lakko-mls` untouched, invariant #3). It revisits exactly one property both ADRs ratified **implicitly, by omission**: that the receive gate has **no recency notion at all**. **The finding targeted — `lakko-security` F-A, G-36 residual 9** (now closed for the single process context), the one residual on that list whose consequence is **confidentiality rather than wake delivery**, needing **no malice and no crash**: the gate asked only *who sent* a secret, never *is it newer*, so a **retry** of the current secret and a **replay** of a superseded one were byte-indistinguishable. A device whose post-add redistribute broadcast failed holds the pre-rotation secret in a `pending` row; another admin rotates to `S1`; the first device's any-holder re-broadcast re-sends the OLD secret; every member's gate sees an authorised sender, accepts, and **retires `gid(S1)`** — migrating the group **back** onto the routing id the evicted member still derives. With no further shrink there is no further rotation, so it was **permanent**. **Decision: carry a `mint_epoch` in the envelope and order envelopes by the pair `(mint_epoch, secret_bytes)`, refusing anything not strictly greater.** **Wire v2, a HARD FLAG DAY** — `domain(29, unchanged) || version(1)=0x02 || mint_epoch(8 BE u64) || secret(32)`, **70 bytes** (was 62); the 29 domain bytes are the `incoming_envelope` dispatch prefix and are **frozen forever** (`domain_separator_bytes_survived_the_v2_bump`), so the in-payload version byte is the knob that moves. **No dual-accept** (`decode_rejects_v1_envelope_as_unknown_version`; the decoder checks **version before length** so a retired v1 reports `UnknownVersion`, not a truncation-looking `BadLength`): a v1 envelope carries no `mint_epoch`, hence has **no position in the order**, so admitting one would re-open F-A for exactly the envelopes a stale device sends — the `group_join_attribution` v1->v2 bump set the in-crate precedent of refusing the predecessor. A mixed-version fleet fails closed to **defer** in both directions, never to the leaky `group_id`, so invariant #4 holds through the flag day. **The tiebreak is the common path, not a corner case:** under ADR-0043 option (b) an owner **plus N admins** may each rotate, so one removal produces **N+1 same-epoch mints** — epoch alone would leave them mutually incomparable and arrival-order-dependent (that IS residual 7), and `S_old` would **tie** with a same-epoch rotation, so epoch alone would not close F-A either. The **secret bytes** are the tiebreak because they are the only discriminator available to every receiver **by construction** AND **stable across relay**: a sender-derived one (leaf index, role rank) changes the value's identity on every re-broadcast and breaks the `accept-identical` fast path, and a minter identity carried in the envelope is **self-asserted** (the only MLS-authenticated attribution is the *relayer's* leaf, and a receiver cannot re-derive a minter's role *at the mint epoch*). **The order lives in Rust, once** — `push_routing_secret_is_newer` + the `PushRoutingSecretEnvelope::supersedes` wrapper, **equal is NOT newer** (what makes the retry fast path safe), wrong-length secrets **fail-closed to `false`**, never panics — and is consumed over **both** FFI surfaces (`pushRoutingSecretIsNewer` on uniffi + wasm) so **no client re-implements the comparison**; three clients each hand-rolling it is exactly the drift class that produced F-A. **Load-bearing split: the core owns the ORDER, the client owns the CLAMP.** `mint_epoch` is **opaque to `lakko-contacts`** — never read as wall-clock, never bounded, never fed to `derive_push_gid`; the crate requires only that one group's mints be non-decreasing and names that as the caller's obligation. Sourcing the stamp from **`Group::epoch()`** is a **client-side binding**, and it is what makes the reject-future rule checkable at all (an MLS epoch is shared state every receiver independently observes; a free-running counter could be bounded by nothing). **The mint epoch must be PERSISTED and re-emitted VERBATIM or the fix is a silent no-op** — all six re-broadcast sites recompose from the stored secret, so a site that re-stamps the *current* epoch at send time makes the stale envelope arrive **newer** than the live rotation and reproduces F-A with the ordering gate fully in place. It is therefore durable per-`(account, group)` state, i.e. a **store schema change on all three clients**, landed read-legacy (an unstamped row reads at **epoch 0**, the floor, so any stamped mint supersedes it) rather than as a migration; **web did NOT bump `DB_VERSION`** (keyPath-only store, no indexes, and `sw.js` opens it version-less). One flag-day tie class follows from the epoch-0 floor and is accepted: two **different** pre-v2 secrets both read 0, so a current-vs-superseded pair among them is decided by the byte tiebreak rather than recency — confined to the transition window and self-healing at the next rotation. **The future clamp, evaluated BEFORE authority:** without it an authorised writer stamps `u64::MAX` and **nothing can ever exceed it**, escalating residual 3 from "an admin can churn the routing id" (self-repairing at the owner's next rotation) to "an admin can **freeze** it forever" (which does not) — trading a recoverable denial for an unrecoverable one. It never rejects an honest envelope, since `stamp <= seal epoch <= receiver's epoch at dispatch`. **The deliberate narrowing, recorded as such:** a total order and **unconditional owner-priority cannot both hold**. The owner still wins whenever it mints at a later epoch — the normal case — but **at an exact epoch tie the bytes decide and the owner can lose to an admin**. That is accepted because the property owner-priority existed to provide, **arrival-order independence**, is preserved by a **strictly stronger** mechanism: a total order is arrival-order independent by construction *for every participant*, not merely for the owner's envelope, and both tied values are authorised post-removal mints equally unknown to the evicted member. Clean framing: **authority decides WHO may write; the order decides WHICH write survives** — the gate still runs first and still decides authorisation alone. **A real defect found during implementation, by the web verdict tests, fixed on all three:** `accept-identical` short-circuits **both** the authority gate and the clamp, so re-persisting the *incoming* epoch there let a **PLAIN MEMBER** relay the group's CURRENT secret bytes stamped `u64::MAX`, inflate the stored stamp, and refuse **every** later rotation including the owner's — reaching the exact freeze the clamp exists to prevent, **around it**. All three now preserve the **stored** stamp on that path, each with a named regression test that also proves a later owner rotation still lands. Generalised rule: **a rule that short-circuits a gate must not write any state that gate protects.** **Android goes beyond the web reference and makes stamp-vs-replay impossible to break BY ACCIDENT:** `composeEnvelope` takes a sealed `MintStamp`, not a `ULong` — `MintStamp.Fresh` and `MintStamp.Replayed` have `internal` constructors and one producer each in `src/main` (the port reading the live `Group::epoch()`, and the stored row). **Stated precisely, since the obvious reading overstates it:** Kotlin `internal` is **module**-scoped and `GroupPushRouting.kt` / `ChatRepository.kt` / `ChatClient.kt` share the `:app` module, and `StoredSecret`'s constructor is fully public, so a wrong stamp is *constructible*; what the sealed type removes is the **silent** failure mode — a bare `ULong` (e.g. `group.epoch()`) can no longer be passed where a stamp is expected, that is a **compile error**, so getting it wrong now takes a differently-named, reviewer-visible call. Both natives also widened the gate from binary accept/reject to **named verdicts** — structural, not diagnostic: the persist step must file the STORED epoch on the identical path and the INCOMING epoch elsewhere, and a binary verdict would force the orchestration to re-derive "was it identical?" by comparing bytes a second time, duplicating the gate's own rule 1. The epoch is read **AFTER** the serializing lock on all three (iOS a `@MainActor` provider closure, Android a suspending port — neither holds a `Group`, and a plain value parameter is necessarily evaluated *before* the wait, stamping the trigger epoch rather than the mint epoch). **C-61 gained its first test on any client, on all three — the ONE gap this arc closed outright** (G-36 residual 6): the ordering moved behind one named seam (`publishRemoveThenRotate`) so the two steps cannot be reordered independently, each client asserts on the **emitted envelope's stamp** rather than call order, and each carries a **permanent inverted-order companion** that runs the swap. On both natives the discrimination was **proven by running the swap**, not asserted (iOS recorded three failures under an inverted copy; Android recorded the stamp assertion failing 11 vs 10); both probes were reverted. The sharpened dependency is why it stopped being optional: under ADR-0043 alone a C-61 regression cost forward secrecy, but under ADR-0044 it **also inverts the recency comparison** — a rotation stamped at the pre-removal epoch **ties** with the stale secret and may simply **lose the byte tiebreak**, degrading the guarantee from "always wins" to a **coin flip**, silently. The fixture is load-bearing: the web `0xEE` stale secret landed in `936d849` was latently flaky (a random rotation secret out-sorts it ~1 run in 14, so the rotation would WIN under the swap, passing a test written to watch it lose) and is now `0xFF`, the bytewise maximum. **Honest scope.** **Residual 9 and residual 7's concurrent-rotator core close for the SINGLE PROCESS CONTEXT**, which is the exact qualification and not a hedge — the order alone never sufficed (**the total order's arrival-order independence holds for the pure function but not automatically for the path that calls it**; residual 7's N+1 same-epoch mints arrive in one poll batch, so without serialization the survivor is **scheduling order**). Residual 7's **F-D stale-role window survives untouched and orthogonal** regardless (a demoted rotator is refused on **authority**, not recency). The order **stops a stale value spreading; it does not repair an already-stale holder** (no push-the-live-value; a self-healing reply was considered and deferred, needing a storm guard against N-fold journal amplification). Residuals **1** (the co-membership cluster — still needs per-device-blinded ids), **3**, **4** and **8** are **unchanged**; **residual 5** keeps its crash + store-read-failure paths (only its F-E path closed). `derive_push_gid` and its golden are **untouched** — the epoch never enters the derivation (`derive_push_gid_ignores_the_mint_epoch`), so C-60's algebraic independence AND the routing id's **stability across ordinary commits** (what lets a subscriber survive commits without re-subscribing) both hold exactly as before; the ADR-0041 / C-59 background-decrypt reverse map is untouched. **Verified firsthand:** `cargo test -p lakko-contacts` 214 passed (41 `#[test]` in `push_routing.rs`), `cargo check --workspace --all-targets`, clippy `-D warnings` on both FFI crates, bindings + wasm + `LakkoCore.xcframework` + Android `.so` rebuilt, web `tsc --noEmit` + vitest 548 (130 across the two push suites) + `vite build`, `just spec-check` OK; `:app:compileDebugKotlin` BUILD SUCCESSFUL with `GroupPushRoutingTest` 98 + `PushRoutingSecretStoreTest` 10 green, `xcodebuild` BUILD SUCCEEDED with `PushGroupRoutingTests` 67 green. **After the serializing lock + the two review follow-ups** (verified firsthand by the maintainer against the current tree): web `tsc --noEmit` clean + vitest **562**; `:app:compileDebugKotlin` BUILD SUCCESSFUL with `GroupPushRoutingTest` **107** + `PushRoutingSecretStoreTest` **10**, zero failures; `xcodebuild` BUILD SUCCEEDED with `PushGroupRoutingTests` **73**, zero failures; `audit-index-check` clean (8 I + 62 C + 15 T). `lakko-security` **run TWICE. First, on the landed arc: REQUEST CHANGES**, one blocking HIGH (the unserialized receive path, reproduced by execution — residual 10 above), plus two LOW (a stale `invariant-index.md` C-60 envelope description + C-61 "no test pins it" claim, both since corrected; and the flag-day tie class where two different pre-v2 secrets both read `mint_epoch = 0`, so a pair among them falls to the byte tiebreak rather than recency — bounded to the transition window, self-healing at the next rotation, which always stamps above 0) and one INFO (the ADR's "surface the mint epoch in diagnostics?" open question was already answered in code — all three log both epochs; defensible since an MLS epoch is public in the framing pre-decrypt). **Second, on the serializing-lock fix: APPROVE WITH NOTES**, no CRITICAL/HIGH, its two actionable findings (the per-realm web lock; the unbounded hold across network I/O) both since closed — **which is what the Accepted status rests on**. Invariants #2 (server opaque, push-gw byte-identical) / #3 (untouched — no `lakko-mls` change) / #4 (reject-future + reject-stale both **drop**, and both cross-version refusals fail to **defer**, never downgrade) / #5 (**unchanged posture** — restores the ADR-0043 forward-unlinkability in the cases F-A was silently revoking, does not touch the cluster) |
| [0045](docs/adr/0045-remote-commit-epoch-fork.md) | **Proposal-based membership commits with a designated committer** (G-38 residual 1). **Status: PROPOSED — BUILT END TO END, S7 INCLUDED** (core + both FFI surfaces + all three clients; S7 landed `576ea14`, so there is **no direct-commit path left in a role-table group** and G-38 residual 1 is closed for external joins too). What is still open is the **external crypto review** — which since [ADR-0046](docs/adr/0046-external-crypto-review.md) is tracked **once**, in **G-39**, as a **release gate** (no build reaches users while it is open) rather than as a per-ADR blocker: carried inside each MLS-touching document it made every one of them permanently un-Acceptable however finished it was, while specifying nothing an auditor could act on. The per-change half of invariant #3 — an ADR plus property tests before the change lands — is unchanged, still enforced per change, and **has been satisfied on every sprint** (this ADR, and 268 tests in `lakko-mls`; an earlier revision of this clause read "neither has happened on any sprint", which was a fragment left behind from when it named the crypto review and the `lakko-security` pass together — the latter has since run). What remains beyond G-39 are the three client-side gaps listed at the end of this row. Closes the last route to a divergent MLS epoch, the one **no client-side lock can address by construction** because the second committer is a **different device**: A and B both build a membership commit against epoch N, the DS journal orders by arrival, the loser's commit is dropped by every peer as `WrongEpoch` while its own author has already eager-merged it — both sides report N+1 holding different state, silently and permanently, with the receive loop's catch-and-continue swallowing it. **Decision: Option B.** Members do not commit; a member emits an MLS **Proposal** and exactly one elected owner-or-admin sweeps the pending set into one Commit. A proposal is **not epoch-advancing**, so concurrent intents compose instead of forking the loser — *the retry moves from the expensive artifact to the cheap one*. The recon originally recommended Option C (client-side loser rollback + bounded retry) and the maintainer **overturned** it on scale: the hazard's frequency grows with (admins × churn) and Option C's convergence **degrades in the same variable**, plus its retry needs a per-intent "is my intent already satisfied?" predicate (two admins approving the *same* join have nothing to redo). **Option A (a DS-side epoch gate) is NOT built** and the case against it survives verbatim: the DS cannot verify a commit, so it must accept well-formed garbage and bump its counter on it, handing **anyone who has ever held a `/g/` invite link a permanent group-freeze primitive** (the DS has no auth and the pseudonym IS the `group_id`); it would silently skip DeviceGroup commits (wrapped in the ADR-0015 envelope, unparseable as MLS); it would **break** ADR-0040's restart sweep; and it changes the DS's transport-only posture. **Decision 1 — who commits:** `lakko_mls::group_roles::committer_rank(table, pubkey)` — rank 0 the owner, rank k+1 the k-th admin in the role table's own canonical order, keyed on **signature public key never leaf index**. A pure total function of the authenticated `0xFF02` GroupContext extension, so there is no election message, no handover protocol and no committer state — it cannot split-brain on *who* rank k is. It **narrows** who commits without widening who **may** (that stays exactly ADR-0022's set). Deliberately not `lakko_calls::link_invite::elect_committer`, which keys on leaf index and needs an SFU presence oracle a chat group has none of. **Decision 2 — liveness: a request has NO deadline.** The lowest-ranked *active* candidate sweeps; if none is running, the request simply waits. The retry cap, the terminal *stalled* state and the `owner_grace` constant were all deleted, because from inside a client *the budget ran out* and *the admins are asleep* are the same observation. The variant question (a candidate **heartbeat** vs a **staggered** takeover) is **RESOLVED — the maintainer chose the heartbeat, variant 1** (`3f09ca1`), on the ground that online/offline visibility is ordinary messenger functionality so the privacy cost does not outweigh making the model literal; variant 2 is kept as the documented fallback, not deleted. The heartbeat **is built in the core, on both FFI surfaces and on all three clients** and is described in the `lakko-contacts` module-map row — a field-less 27-byte beacon envelope + the pure `acting_committer_verdict` fold + the `should_emit_beacon` throttle + four constants, plus `Group::committerRank` for ranking a beacon's sender. ~~It is INERT~~ — **the election is LIVE**: every client emits a throttled beacon from a foregrounded chat screen on its own ticker (web `setInterval`, iOS `Timer` bound to screen visibility + `applicationState`, Android a `repeatOnLifecycle(RESUMED)` loop whose scope IS the foreground gate), stores observations keyed on the sender's **signature key** (never a leaf, never a rank), and folds them at **verdict** time with ranks re-derived through `Group::committerRank` — so an admin-only group no longer waits for an absent owner. Pinned as **C-64**. **The asymmetry this sentence used to record is closed:** the freshness window is `2 x interval + worst-case poll`, and the web client — which had **no periodic poll at all** until the chat route gained its automatic receive loop — now long-polls the open chat's journal (25 s `wait`, paused only while the tab is hidden and no call is up, the same foreground condition its beacon already required), so all three clients meet the constraint. The correctness caveat is unchanged by the choice and must not be read as softened by it: a heartbeat removes the *wait*, **not the race** (each candidate computes over its own poll-timed view, and a beacon is stale by up to one interval even when its sender is perfectly healthy), so the stand-down journal-head re-read and the committer rollback are required in **both** variants. **Decision 3 — the invariant-#3 piece:** before this, a received proposal was `ProcessedMessageContent::ProposalMessage(_) => Incoming::Proposal` — **discarded unexamined**, safe *only* because Lakko stored none. Now `crates/lakko-mls/src/group_proposals.rs` `authorise` is **the** rule, written once and consumed at three layers: the **receipt gate** (store only if the author's role authorises it, else drop), the **sweep filter** (`CommitBuilder::build`'s closure — never `commit_to_pending_proposals`, which hardcodes `\|_\| true`), and the **peer-verified commit gate** (judge **every** proposal a commit carries against its own author's role, because the committer is not trusted). By-reference is judged against the **proposer**, by-value against the **committer** — so every direct-commit path behaves byte-for-byte as it did under ADR-0022. **Decision 4:** external joins converge onto the same store + sweep — **S7, BUILT in `576ea14`**. This row long read "still NOT built — `Group::approve_external_join` calls `add_member` and eager-merges, so it is the one remaining direct-commit path in a role-table group", and the `a90fa90` `refuse_if_proposals_pending` hardening had made that user-visible by refusing an approve while any membership request was pending.
