Both are now history: approving publishes an `Add` **proposal the admin authors itself**, the guard no longer reaches it, and no direct-commit path is left in a role-table group. Decision 4 was wrong on one point, and the module map records it: the `pending_external_joins` quarantine is **not** retired, because it is what keeps a non-member out of the member proposal store whose quota a `/g/` link-holder could otherwise fill (C-66). The external-join arm stays **explicitly separate** in the filter, defaulting to **exclude**, or a stranger with a `/g/` link would be auto-admitted past ADR-0027's gate. **Decision 5:** naming the states only; the UX spec is [`docs/ux/proposal-based-membership.md`](docs/ux/proposal-based-membership.md). **No migration and NO dual path** — no deployment, no users, so it is a flag day; a "legacy direct-commit fallback" would be a second ungated route into membership, i.e. the hazard rebuilt under a prudent name. A group with **no role table** keeps direct commits, and `Group::create` has seeded one since ADR-0022. **BUILT (four core sprints, three commits): S1+S2 the gate + the committer (`9a60ab3`), S3 both FFI surfaces + the S0 UX spec (`c5c0788`), ownership transfer + `owner_survives` (`e780952`), the staged-sweep rollback + the mute fix (`fe3de6c`).** Each of the last three found something the ADR did not record, and all are now written into it: **(1)** the rollback point is the **absence of the eager merge**, not a "retained epoch-N snapshot" — `stage_pending_proposals` leaves the group at N holding a staged commit and `merge_staged_sweep` / `abandon_staged_sweep` adjudicate it; nothing is captured or persisted separately, and the discard is per-group `clear_pending_commit` rather than a whole-provider `restore_storage` that would roll **sibling groups** back past commits they already published. **This is ADR-0040's own deferred "Option B" (defer the eager merge in the core), scoped to the sweep path only** — the four direct-commit paths still merge eagerly, so ADR-0040's outbox wraps both shapes unchanged and the two answer different questions (*did my commit reach the wire* vs *did it win the wire order*). **(2)** a staged sweep **freezes that group's own membership operations** (OpenMLS `is_operational()` refuses `propose_*` / `add_members` / `remove_members` / `self_update` on `PendingCommit`) and the staged commit is **durable** (`write_group_state`), so a client that stages and never adjudicates wedges membership **across restarts** — a hard new client obligation; incoming messages are unaffected. **(3)** a pending proposal **muted the whole group's chat**: OpenMLS refuses `create_message` while the proposal store is non-empty, so from one admin's proposal until the sweep — which under decision 2 may be hours — *every* member failed to send. Fixed by `create_application_message` **parking** the pending set around the encrypt call (clone, clear, encrypt, restore; unchanged fast path when nothing is pending), with a failed unpark beating a successful send. **(4)** the obvious alternative fix was refuted in source: **by-reference resolution runs at commit receipt on EVERY member** (`ProposalQueue::from_committed_proposals`), not only at sweep time on the committer, so the store must be populated on every member for exactly the interval that fix would have kept it empty — which is what makes "store at receipt" **mandatory rather than tidy**. **(5)** the security content of the ownership sprint was **`owner_survives`, not the transfer wrapper**: a role table names 32-byte keys, so `set_group_roles` could always install an owner **no leaf holds** — permanently un-administrable, since a role change is owner-only and the owner's leaf is unremovable — and this refuses that commit on **every peer** independently of whether anyone uses the transfer API; it also catches the shape ADR-0045 itself created, a transfer riding alongside the new owner's removal in one composed commit. **Indexed as C-63** (whose sweep-side enforcement layer only acquired a production caller with the client sprints, while its authoring layer still has none). Transfer itself is deliberately **not a new commit type** — it is the owner-only role-table rewrite with the owner slot moved, judged against the **pre-merge** table — and it does **not** relieve ADR-0022's lost-owner case, because minting one needs the owner's key. **Two more sprints landed since:** **S3b the FFI back-fill** (`6c77cd6` — the staged pair + ownership transfer + `OutgoingOwner` on both surfaces, so a client can now stage-and-adjudicate rather than only eager-merge; this row previously said "neither surface exposes them", which is CLOSED), and the **decision-2 beacon sprint** (`94825ec` core + `94da492` both FFI surfaces + the slim extension surface, `5d6af94` the three background filters, `f53d11a` the three foreground filters). The beacon sprint added a filter obligation nobody anticipated: a beacon is an ordinary MLS application message, and `parse_body_envelope`'s deliberate lossy-UTF-8 legacy fallback degrades it to a **chat bubble reading `lakko/committer-beacon/v1`** plus two control bytes — so all three clients gained an arm ahead of the chat parser on **both** the foreground timeline path and the background notification path, and a recognised beacon surfaces as its **own event variant rather than being dropped**, because the sender leaf and the DS row age — the two things the field-less payload deliberately omits — exist only at that point in the chain and are exactly what the fold consumes — which is no longer a future tense: all three clients now drive it. **THE THREE CLIENT SPRINTS LANDED (2026-08-25):** S4a `1de5fe7` (the web reference — admins propose, the owner sweeps, the five obligations each placed where a test can run it), S4b `02f0037` (the beacon emitter + observation store + fold, which gives `composeCommitterBeacon` its first call site anywhere and ends the admin-only group's wait), `000d9f8` (an unplanned **send-path** serialization), and S5+S6 `1f230ab` (the iOS + Android mirrors). **Six things the clients found that the ADR did not anticipate**, all now in its `What the client sprints found` section: **(a)** the ADR's own verdict answer was wrong in the **dangerous** direction — epoch-alone plus "not one of our artifacts" reads another member's ordinary application message as a **loss** and abandons a sweep that **won**, which is the same brick in reverse; the clients instead apply the rows below their own commit and ask whether the epoch moved, which needs no discrimination and makes the proposed `contentTypeOfMessage` FFI addition unnecessary; **(b)** the stand-down therefore stands down on **any** unapplied row, since standing down costs one retry while staging on an unapplied commit costs a commit every peer drops; **(c)** a **send** can be sealed at one epoch and published across a commit that changes it — `create_message` gates on `is_active` not `is_operational`, so a staged sweep does not block it and the loss is a **certainty** for the whole verdict read, and with `max_past_epochs` unset (openmls default 0) such a message is **permanently undecryptable**, not late; closed by taking the per-group MLS lock across seal-and-publish on all three, which closes the **local** half only; **(d)** **C-61 moves with the removal** — under ADR-0045 a removal usually becomes a proposal, so rotating the push-routing secret at `removeMemberFromGroup` would seal it at an epoch the evicted member can still open (and tie the ADR-0044 stamp); it moves to the **sweep** via the membership-shrink diff, and the general rule is that any client discipline seamed onto a direct-commit call site must be re-seamed onto the sweep; **(e)** **concurrent role changes wedge membership** — RFC 9420 admits one `GroupContextExtensions` proposal per commit and OpenMLS enforces it as a HARD build failure (`TooManyGCEProposals`), so a pending set holding two cannot be swept at all and every committer holding both has its membership operations dead; the silent single-drop the sweep was thought to do is only the asymmetric case, where one committer happens to hold just one. **Closed in the core (2026-08-28), C-65**: `refuse_if_role_change_pending` at propose time, plus a one-role-change bound in `sweep_keeps` for the ADR-0040 crash window the propose-time guard structurally cannot see (a device that loses its own record before persisting proposes a second legitimately, and every PEER then holds two). Ownership transfer is a role-table rewrite, so it collides both ways and inherits the guard. What remains is readable copy on Android — web and iOS pre-check correctly narrowly (on a pending role change, not on any pending proposal); **(f)** the `refuse_if_proposals_pending` guard reached `approve_external_join`, which was the sharpest practical argument for S7 — and S7 answered it by removing the direct commit the guard was protecting, rather than by loosening the guard. **Ownership transfer shipped end to end (2026-08-28, `0274fa7`)** — UX spec §6.1 plus all three clients, so the owner dead-end is now closed in the product too. The design pass settled a question rather than answering it: the **direct arm is structurally unreachable**, since `isOwner` and `usesProposalPath` are the same read (the role table's presence), so clients drive `proposeOwnershipTransfer` only — iOS proved it by execution, the direct arm advancing the epoch AND stripping the transferor locally, so the next role change is refused as *proposer is not the owner*. **Self-eviction handling shipped too** (§6.2 + `Group::is_active()` + `isActive` on both FFI surfaces): before it, no client handled its own removal landing, so the chat stayed live with the composer enabled over a group that can never receive another message — and five background disciplines kept running against it, one of them (the ADR-0043 shrink rotation) doing real harm rather than spinning, because a `Remove` rewrites no `GroupContext` so an evicted admin's role table still named them admin and the client minted a secret, subscribed to a push id nobody will ever notify, and retried a throwing broadcast on every chat-open. Tearing down the device's own push row **closes the half of G-36 residual 2 ADR-0043 recorded as unclosable**. **Still not built** (S7 has come off this list): the UX spec's **superseded** re-request (a request that LANDED and was then dropped by a sweep — distinct from the queue-full case, which `21f6c8c` / `1839da7` built for both requests and approvals) and its committer-side failure surface (the last is unimplementable as specified — it is gated on an *activation delay* belonging to the staggered variant the maintainer did not choose); and the status flip. **The `lakko-security` pass RAN (2026-08-29) and returned REQUEST CHANGES** — three HIGH findings plus a confirmed impersonation, all seven closed in `c50bdd7` / `0274fa7` / `8763f3e` / `5b2b654` and recorded in the ADR's new `What the security review found` section, indexed as **C-66** (a group must never lose the ability to change its own membership), **C-67** (durable bookkeeping must never claim more progress than actually landed) and **C-68** (an author is a signature key resolved at receipt, never a leaf index). **The headline is that none of the seven needed an attacker and four needed nobody at all:** the ADR-0023 author gate compared a raw leaf index and OpenMLS reuses a vacated leaf, so a remove-then-add — one commit under this ADR — let a newcomer rewrite and delete a departed member's history, permanently on both natives; `Update` proposals bypassed `owner_survives` and could flood the pending set; a full pending set took away the owner's own `Remove` and `RoleChange` so a flooder locked the owner out of responding, with the only exit committing their payload (first mis-triaged as *weaker than what an admin already holds*, which is wrong because **capabilities compose** — removing members is loud and repairable, filling the queue removes the means of repair); an unbuildable sweep is a permanent, invisible, group-wide membership freeze reachable from ONE proposal, and one of its fifteen enumerated causes is **KeyPackage expiry**, i.e. elapsed time alone against a request decision 2 gives no deadline; a Welcome fan-out failing after a won merge was unrecoverable, because the merge clears every signal the recovery paths branch on (**the merge is not the end of the sweep's obligations** — an artifact still owed after the epoch-advancing step needs its own durable name); and a receiver could strand itself off the group forever, because the durable snapshot and the journal cursor live in stores no transaction can span while the cursor advanced first (**the cursor may lag the durable state, never lead it**). Because enumerating a library's build rules is a race Lakko does not own, the enumeration is backed by `narrow_sweep_by_exclusion`, which needs only to know *which* proposal is uncommittable and not *why* — it rescued a live unenumerated case immediately, an admin proposing its own departure wedging its own sweep. **The external crypto review has still NOT happened** and remains the largest open item; the surface it would cover grew again with these seven fixes. Invariants #1 / #2 (untouched — no server change, no wire-format change, no new artifact on the journal) / #3 (**a direct change, four ways**: the per-proposal gate, `owner_survives`, the new stage/merge/abandon commit lifecycle, and the send-path park; 268 tests across the `lakko-mls` lib and its seven integration binaries — the figure was 180 across seven when `719d0df` recorded it, before the ownership-transfer, membership-state and typed-refusal binaries) / #4 (fail-closed at every new decision point — an unauthorised or unclassifiable proposal is dropped, an unauthorised commit is refused not merged, a second stage is refused, `abandon` re-reads and refuses to report a rollback it did not perform, a failed unpark beats a successful send) / #5 (untouched) |
| [0046](docs/adr/0046-external-crypto-review.md) | **External cryptographic review — a programme gate, not a per-change blocker.** **Accepted (maintainer's call).** Security invariant #3 requires *"an ADR + property tests + (after Phase 2) an external crypto audit"* for a `lakko-mls` change, and the third clause had been carried **inside each ADR that touches it** — four of them — as a reason that ADR could not be Accepted. That reading had a consequence nobody chose: **no MLS-touching ADR can ever be Accepted, however finished**, and the debt was simultaneously blocking everything and specified nowhere. ADR-0045 is the case in point: built end to end across core, both FFI surfaces and three clients, through a `lakko-security` pass whose seven findings are closed, 268 core tests (242 on the day this ADR was written), seven indexed invariants — and permanently `Proposed` for a reason having nothing to do with it. **The invariant does not say what that reading assumed:** it scopes the audit with *"after Phase 2"*, a **programme phase**, not a per-change precondition; an ADR records a decision, a cryptographic review examines an accumulated interacting surface from outside, and binding the first to the second was a misreading rather than a policy. **Decision:** the review is tracked **once**, as **G-39**, which owns the scope and the trigger; invariant #3's text is **unchanged** and its per-change half stays enforced per change; an MLS-touching ADR may reach `Accepted` on its own merits and carries a pointer to G-39 instead of its own copy of the debt; and the gate is a **release** gate — no store submission, no GA tag, while G-39 is open, which is where *"after Phase 2"* bites and is what keeps this from being a way to avoid the audit. **Explicitly does NOT** relax #3, shrink the scope, lower the per-change bar, or make an unaudited change safe — it makes one **shippable to nobody**. **Risk stated:** one tracked item is easier to defer than four blockers each stopping its own document; the mitigation is that the gate is unambiguous and the debt is now specified, so deferring is a visible choice rather than a default. Alternatives rejected: keep the per-ADR blocker (it produced the current state — a blocker that blocks unconditionally and forever stops being read); drop the requirement (the internal `lakko-security` pass found three HIGH findings and a confirmed impersonation in an already-shipped surface, four needing nobody at all — an external reviewer does not share those blind spots); audit per-ADR for real (a review examines an **interacting** surface, and the interactions between ADR-0022's role table, ADR-0045's proposal gate and C-63's commit rule are exactly what a per-change review would each see only a slice of) |
| [0047](docs/adr/0047-media-messages-and-gif-proxy.md) | **Media messages, emoji picker and a server-side GIF proxy.** **Accepted** (GIPHY behind Lakko's server is the maintainer's choice). UX spec: [`docs/ux/media-messages.md`](docs/ux/media-messages.md). Voice messages, video notes and GIFs are **ordinary encrypted attachments** with optional, bounded metadata on `AttachmentRef` (`kind` / `duration_ms` / `waveform` / `width` / `height` / `thumb`), no wire-version bump; a field that fails its bound or type is dropped and the envelope survives; **inline playback is gated on a MIME allowlist, never on `kind`**. One recording format per kind on every platform (voice AAC-LC in MP4, video note H.264 + AAC in MP4, square, <= 60 s); a browser that cannot record MP4 disables recording. Emoji data is bundled; reactions accept any single emoji grapheme within `MAX_EMOJI_BYTES`. GIF search and media go through a stateless `services/gif-proxy` (session-token auth, id-based media route — no client-supplied upstream URL, size and content-type caps, per-account rate limit, no persistence or query logging, `503` when unconfigured); the picked GIF is re-uploaded as an encrypted attachment with `kind: gif`, so **recipients never contact the proxy or GIPHY**. Cost stated in the UI: the Lakko server learns a signed-in account's GIF search terms. Invariants #2 / #5 unchanged for message content. **History bundles carry the metadata too (§5):** `lakko_mls::history_placeholder` is version 2 (per-attachment `kind` / `duration_ms` / raw `waveform` / `width` / `height` / `thumb`, version 1 refused), structure-only in `lakko-mls`, with the semantic bounds applied in both FFI crates through `lakko_attachments::RawMediaMetadata::bounded` — a codec change, not a state-machine change, with a round-trip property test. **Core landed:** the `lakko-attachments` fields, bounds, lenient decode, `inline_media_type`, the extended fuzz oracle, and the UniFFI / wasm / extension FFI source (new UniFFI record fields default to `None`, so existing Swift/Kotlin initializer calls stay source-compatible after regeneration) |
| [0048](docs/adr/0048-profile-names.md) | **Profile names, carried end-to-end encrypted and bound to the sender's key.** **Accepted** (the maintainer chose own profile name + username). UX spec: [`docs/ux/profile-names.md`](docs/ux/profile-names.md). **Rust core built** (clients not yet wired; bindings not regenerated): a bounded, fail-closed `lakko-contacts::profile` envelope `domain "lakko/profile/v1\0" ‖ version(1) ‖ updated_at_ms(8 BE) ‖ name_len(1) ‖ name ‖ username_len(1) ‖ username` (display name trimmed, 1-64 scalars, **<= 255 bytes** — the one-byte `name_len` overrides the ADR's 256 — no line break, no `Cc`, no `Cf` from an explicit table, so ZWJ/ZWNJ emoji and Persian spellings are refused too; username canonical or empty), rejected as a whole on any violation, `compose` refusing exactly what `decode` refuses; `profile_is_newer` (strictly greater, per signature key); `IncomingEnvelope::Profile` so it never renders as a bubble; fuzz `profile_decode`. **Carriers:** the contact request's sealed plaintext is now `profile_len(u16 BE) ‖ profile ‖ intro` (no dual format) — a well-framed but invalid profile opens the request with `profile = None` rather than dropping it, a broken framing fails the open; and an MLS application message per group (the join / change / growth republish is client work). **Trigger 4, added 2026-09-19:** the three triggers are push-only and a newcomer depends on other devices noticing the growth, which a device that was away, or a closed web tab, never does (live: three of five members read `Member` on the device that joined last) — so a newcomer also asks, with a field-less `lakko/profile-request/v1` envelope (`lakko-contacts::profile_request`, 26 bytes, the committer-beacon shape) that every reader answers through the throttled growth path; sent on join and, at most daily per group, on opening a group with an unnamed member (`should_request_profiles`, one rule on both FFI surfaces; the extension crate decodes it only to suppress it). **Binding** is the receiver's: stored under the sender's signature key resolved at receipt (C-68), username shown only after discovery resolves it to that key. **Username character set (§4):** `[a-z0-9_]`, 3-32 after normalisation, defined once in `lakko-contacts::username` and enforced at the OPRF blinder (`lakko-discovery-client::validate_username`), so register and lookup refuse by construction; `@channel` handles keep their own policy. FFI source: `validateUsername` (typed `Username*` variants appended to `FfiError` / a `usernameError` JS property), `composeProfileEnvelope` / `decodeProfileEnvelope` / `profileIsNewer` / `normalizeDisplayName`, the `Profile` dispatch kind, `composeContactRequest` + `openEnvelope` reshaped; the NSE extension gets decode (no fields) + min/max length only. Invariants #1/#2 (the server sees ciphertext only) / #5 (no server-side profile store) hold; #3 untouched (no `lakko-mls` change) |
| [0049](docs/adr/0049-identity-recovery-word-vault.md) | **Identity recovery: three of the twenty-four words, a confirmation prompt on another device, and a passkey** (account-recovery overhaul, Part 3). **Accepted; the word vault, the another-device confirmation prompt and the passkey vault are built on web + `services/auth` (2026-09-15); new-device chat membership is NOT built and is blocked on per-device conversation leaves (ADR-0007 Phase 2b), see the ADR's corrected section 5** (2026-09-15; the maintainer chose the direction after the live test on `test.lakko.me`: three numbered words on every recovery path, another device without a camera, passkey as a third route, twelve or twenty-four words rejected as UX). UX spec: [`docs/ux/identity-recovery.md`](docs/ux/identity-recovery.md). **The word vault:** three words cannot derive the seed (33 bits of a 264-bit string), so the client seals the 32-byte BIP39 **entropy** under a key Argon2id-stretched from the words at three fixed positions chosen at random at registration, and `services/auth` stores `verifier || sealed_entropy` (wrapped again under a server-static `LAKKO_VAULT_ENC_KEY`, the TOTP pattern) behind a guess budget: the TOTP 5-in-15-minutes lockout plus a **10-strike destruction cap**. `POST /vault/enroll` (bearer) / `POST /vault/recover/{start,finish}` (anonymous + PoW, enumeration-safe: an unknown account gets pepper-derived positions and a dummy compare). A correct open reconstructs the full phrase with `Mnemonic::from_entropy` and hands it to the **existing** `provideRecoveryPhrase` (fail-closed bundle check) and, for ACCESS, the existing recovery OPAQUE login, so nothing downstream is new. Modelled on Signal's Secure Value Recovery (Argon2-stretched PIN, enclave-held secret with a guess counter); three BIP39 words are about 2^20 times a four-digit PIN. **Another device:** the fresh device posts its pairing payload (the bytes the QR carries) to `POST /device/request`; signed-in devices show "Is this you? Code 47 19 03" and **Confirm** runs the unchanged ADR-0018 flow (grant + Path B seed seal + history bundle). The six-digit comparison code is BLAKE3 over the payload and is what stops a malicious server substituting its own device. QR stays as an option. **Passkey (phase 2):** WebAuthn PRF output wraps the entropy into a server-held opaque `passkey_vaults` blob so a synced passkey carries identity; no PRF support exists yet in the vendored `webauthn-rs-core` or the web client, so until then a passkey login is followed by the vault or the prompt. **Finding 12 is a requirement here:** after any another-device flow the old device adds the new device to every chat group (ADR-0045 proposals for role-table groups); after a vault or passkey restore with no other device alive, chat membership is not recoverable (MLS forward secrecy) and the UI says so instead of showing an empty list. **Invariant #2 is weakened for the recovery path, stated explicitly:** the operator, ignoring their own counter and holding the environment key, faces a 2^33 memory-hard search per account (arithmetic on an assumed per-evaluation cost, to be measured; the ADR proposes the exception text for the invariant). Argon2 parameters are a measured cost budget carried in the row, not a fixed set. Migrations `0017_word_vault.sql` (`word_vaults`, `vault_opens`) and `0018_device_requests_passkey_vaults.sql` (`device_requests`, `passkey_vaults`); the comparison code is SHA-256 over the payload, computed on both devices, never by the server; the web passkey ceremonies request the PRF extension and strip its output before the blob reaches the server; a fresh-device passkey login is now possible on web (it was refused outright before) and lands either from the passkey vault or in the restore prompt. Enclave / SVR3-style hardening recorded as the follow-up. Flag for `lakko-security` before merge |
| [0050](docs/adr/0050-per-device-conversation-leaves.md) | **Per-device conversation leaves, a second device is a full client** (ADR-0007 Phase 2b; closes G-40). **Accepted 2026-09-15; S1 core, S2 FFI, S3 discovery and S4 web built the same day and live-tested on `test.lakko.me` (a device added through the ADR-0049 confirmation was admitted to the account's existing chat by the confirming device and received later messages); S5 natives built 2026-09-16 on iOS + Android (xcodebuild test and gradle unit tests green, not yet run on a device; QR-pairing path only, no native ADR-0049 prompt); S6 security+docs open.** Two facts the build added to the ADR: a founder adds every device in ONE commit (`Group::add_members`; one commit per leaf stranded every leaf but the last at a Welcome epoch nobody could reach), and the published KeyPackage is a last-resort package (one fetch admits a device to N chats; the forward-secrecy cost and the one-time-batch follow-up are recorded in 2.6). On web the ADR-0045 sweep also runs at the tail of every background poll, so a proposal lands without its chat being opened. Built later the same day: the scheduled re-admission arm (any member re-checks the device lists of the accounts in a chat against its roster at the tail of its background polls; a leaf of the own account whose directory row is gone is retired, which is how `forgetDevice` — it deletes its row first — retires itself), the pairing state bundle (contacts, renames, pins, known group ids), and the history bundle on the confirmation route, which had been skipped outright. Unbuilt: incremental contact sync between devices, channel subscriptions in the bundle. Every conversation leaf becomes the per-device `DeviceIdentity`, bound to the account key by a signed binding carried as the leaf's `BasicCredential` identity bytes (an empty credential = a Phase 1 account-key leaf; `Identity::generate(name)` no longer carries the name), verified by every peer at every admission gate because OpenMLS carries it and checks nothing; the account key stays the published, pinned, KT-logged identity and the safety number. The core's single leaf-to-identity resolver (`pubkey_at_leaf`) becomes `account_key_at_leaf`, and roles, authorship, read state, external-join dedup, committer rank and the call dedup key on the resolved account key; the `0xFF02` role-table wire is unchanged because Phase 1 keys already are account keys. Two self-service arms in `authorise`: a member may add or remove a leaf of its own account, and may add a bound device of an account already in the group (automatic re-admission after a lost-everything restore); `MAX_LEAVES_PER_ACCOUNT = 8`; a self-service-only pending set may be swept by the proposing account's lowest-leaf device. Discovery stores one KeyPackage per (account, device) and serves the list; DS, auth, push-gw and the KT log do not change. Pairing and the ADR-0049 confirmation fan the new device into every known group through one hook in `runScanFlow`; the pairing bundle carries contacts and channel subscriptions; incremental sync uses the reserved DeviceGroup proto fields. UX spec `docs/ux/second-device.md` |
| [0051](docs/adr/0051-group-and-channel-names.md) | **Group and channel names carried end-to-end encrypted to every member and subscriber.** **Accepted 2026-09-17** (the maintainer chose the envelope-plus-record design over a `GroupContext` extension); UX spec [`docs/ux/group-and-channel-names.md`](docs/ux/group-and-channel-names.md). Until then a group name lived on the creator's device only (web lost it on reload, iOS had no field) and a channel name on the publisher's, so subscribers saw the bare handle. **Group name:** a bounded, domain-separated envelope `lakko/group-name/v1\0 ‖ version ‖ updated_at_ms ‖ name_len ‖ name` on an MLS application message (`lakko-contacts::group_name`, the profile template; empty name = clear; whole-envelope rejection; `IncomingEnvelope::GroupName`, never a bubble; fuzz `group_name_decode`), **accepted only from the owner or an admin resolved at receipt through the live role table** (plain member dropped, no-table group open, unreadable dropped), ordered by a strictly-greater stamp per group that a growth re-send re-emits verbatim (the ADR-0044 lesson), sent after the creating commit, on rename and on growth (throttled like the profile republish); background filters (web SW, Android background decrypt, iOS NSE via the decode-only extension surface) suppress it. **Channel name + description:** a bounded record `lakko/channel-meta/v1\0 ‖ version ‖ updated_at_ms ‖ name ‖ description` carried as the **plaintext of an ordinary channel post** (`lakko-channels::meta`; authenticity = the post signature, confidentiality = the epoch seed; `classify_post_plaintext` -> `Text` / `Meta` / `Malformed` runs before the readers' text path, on both FFI surfaces so no client hand-rolls the sniff; fuzz `channel_meta_decode`), re-issued by the publisher at creation, on edit and after every rotation so a late subscriber always finds one it can read. Alternatives rejected: server-side name fields (#2), a `GroupContext` extension (a commit per rename under ADR-0045, and `lakko-mls`), the seed envelope. Core + both FFI surfaces built 2026-09-17; the three clients are the same day's fan-out, see PRODUCT-READINESS |
| [0052](docs/adr/0052-reply-forward-pin.md) | **Reply, forward and pin** — the three new-message / keep-message actions of the Telegram-class message menu. **Accepted 2026-09-26** (the maintainer chose: forward re-encrypts and carries an origin reference only for a local jump; several pins, shared outranks personal; the quote is a snapshot). UX spec [`docs/ux/message-actions.md`](docs/ux/message-actions.md). **Decision 1 — Reply is a reference carried BESIDE the text, never one of the mutually exclusive ops** (`edit_of` / `delete_of` / `react_to` are things done TO a message; a reply is a message that also points somewhere, so putting it in that set would make replying and typing exclusive): `reply_to` + a bounded snapshot `reply_author` / `reply_excerpt` taken when the reply is composed, so every receiver — a later joiner, a fresh device — draws the same quote, and a later edit or deletion of the original changes nothing; one level deep. **Built end-to-end 2026-09-26** (core + both FFI surfaces + web / iOS / Android: Reply row first in the card, composer strip, quote block, tap-to-jump, quote persisted; seen live in the web harness only, not yet walked on two devices). **Decision 2 — Forward** re-encrypts the attachments (a forwarded reference would let the storage tier correlate two conversations through one blob) and carries the original author as a display name only, plus a conversation-id + messageId origin reference inside the sealed payload for a local jump; the server learns nothing. **Decision 3 — Pin** is two features: a per-device personal pin and a shared pin distributed as a group envelope (`lakko-contacts`, its own domain + codec + fuzz target), owner/admin — or either participant in a 1×1 — may unpin, shared outranks personal, several pins with a bar showing one at a time. **Decision 2 built end to end 2026-09-26** (walked web -> iOS, iOS -> iOS, Android -> Android). **Decision 3 built end to end 2026-09-26** — `lakko-contacts::pin` (31-byte fixed envelope, one message per envelope, `pin_is_newer` per message, fuzz `pin_decode`), both FFI surfaces + the NSE decode arm, the three clients per UX spec 4.1 (scope sheet, personal set + shared map with stored unpins, the receive gate under the per-group lock, growth re-send, background suppression, the pinned bar); walked live: personal pin on all three, the shared pin in a one-to-one iOS <-> Android both directions, and in a group the owner's `Pin for everyone` + `Unpin` from web reaching a web member whose own sheet offered `Pin for me` alone; and an iOS owner's `Pin for everyone` reaching an Android member and a web member; NOT walked: the list control, the growth re-send. Channel pins unbuilt. No `lakko-mls` change (invariant #3 untouched); #2 / #5 hold — every new field rides inside the MLS-sealed payload |
| [0053](docs/adr/0053-ds-journal-authorisation.md) | **A journal on the Delivery Service is opened by a key, not by its name** (closes G-42). **Accepted 2026-09-27; S1-S4 built the same day and deployed to the test stack: the `lakko-ds-proof` crate, the DS side (`services/ds/src/access.rs`, migration `0002`, seven DB-gated tests), the FFI signer on UniFFI / wasm / the NSE surface, and all three clients (web S3, iOS + Android S4) signing, claiming, registering each epoch before the commit that reaches it, healing on apply, knocking to join and delivering sweep Welcomes to mailboxes. Walked live: a message crossed web, iOS and Android in all six directions in a claimed group, and the same group by name alone was refused (unsigned read / write / forged proof 401, competing registration 409). S3/S4 amended the server before the clients: open question 1 resolved as a two-epoch WRITE tier with a read-only tail for the journal retention window (else a device offline across two commits was stranded for good), an idempotent claim, and a knock that takes several payloads and an empty list as an epoch probe; `lakko-mls` gained two read-only accessors, `Group::staged_export_secret` (the N+1 key of a staged sweep, registered before publishing) and `Group::process_knock` over `is_external_join_proposal_for` (a knock row is refused unless it is a stranger's external-join proposal for this group, closing a fork a member could cause by posting a real commit to the knocks), both property-tested and on both FFI surfaces. Not walked yet: the `/g/` knock join, the sweep's mailbox Welcome, the three background readers; `lakko-security` S5 open.** `services/ds` serves and accepts any journal to whoever names it; the name travels (a forward carries the origin `group_id` - ADR-0052's "opaque id they cannot open" was false of the journal; a mailbox is `BLAKE3(identity_pk)` and the key is public; the `/g/` joiner reads a journal it is not in), so a leaked name reads metadata, writes garbage and knocks. **Decision:** a claimed journal answers only a request signed with a key it was claimed under - a per-epoch Ed25519 key every member derives from `Group::export_secret("lakko/ds/access/v1", ...)` (rotates with the epoch, eviction revokes at the commit; the server holds public keys per epoch, no `lakko-mls` change), the identity key for a mailbox (reads gated, writes stay the open cold inbox); a failed proof is one uniform `401` (amended before S1: a silent drop would hide a wrong-clock member's lost publishes, and a 256-bit name cannot be probed); the non-member's one act moves to a PoW-gated **knock** whose response carries the epoch (the ADR-0028 whole-journal read goes away) and the link joiner's Welcome moves to its mailbox (C-69 amended); rendezvous mailboxes stay capability-by-name as the stated residual. One shared crate `lakko-ds-proof` (canonical bytes + verifier, the `lakko-auth-token` shape). Rejected: hiding the id from co-members only (a symptom), the auth session token on the DS (names the account: invariant #5), a symmetric MAC key (the server could mint requests), the ADR-0042 static-secret pattern (the exporter gives rotation for free), a DS epoch gate (still rejected per ADR-0045). Flag day; a `lakko-security` pass before merge. **Who registers an epoch key is now [ADR-0057](docs/adr/0057-journal-ownership.md)'s** (the security pass's HIGH finding): a journal is claimed under a root key and only the root and its registrars register epochs; readers, writers and knocks are unchanged |
| [0054](docs/adr/0054-anti-abuse-without-client-work.md) | **Anti-abuse is decided by the server, not paid for by the phone.** **Accepted 2026-09-27; S1 (wake through the DS, server + three clients) and S2 (`lakko-anon-token` + `lakko-anon-token-server` + `POST /auth/tokens`) built the same day; S3 (discovery, DS knock and push-gw take a token instead of proof of work), S4 (auth: server counters, one heavy proof at signup start only) and S5 (sign-in and recovery by name at auth with a peppered login handle, no discovery lookup before a session; handle bound at signup auth-first, after sign-in and on every session landing) built 2026-09-28; all deployed to the test stack and walked live in part (the DS wake, short links, token spends, a handle bind); open: another-device recovery on a device with no cached account id, the signup effort measurement, the security pass**. Per-request proof of work (34 server call sites across auth, discovery, push-gw and the DS) is removed: a phone without a JIT paid seconds per request while an attacker's hardware pays little. (1) A group wake becomes a consequence of the signed ADR-0053 publish: an `x-lakko-wake: <push_gid>;<kind>` header, forwarded by the DS to push-gw over the internal network only after the proof gate passed; `/push/notify-group` leaves the public vhost, the caller-less `/push/notify` is deleted, the DS caps wakes per group; the DS holds the group / `push_gid` pair in memory only. (2) Anonymous tokens (the privately verifiable Privacy Pass shape, a VOPRF on the existing `voprf` crate) issued by auth to a session under a **per-period** quota refilled in the background, never requiring a re-login; spent once per consuming service on lookup, OPRF, invite consume, channel-name register, the DS knock and push-gw register / subscribe; chat messages never spend one. (3) Bearer routes and pre-session guessing routes lose the proof for per-account counters and a per-IP backoff, with extra limits on OTP sends and device-request prompts. (4) Account creation keeps one deliberately heavy proof, the single rationing point the token quota rests on. Plan S1-S5 in the ADR |
| [0055](docs/adr/0055-websocket-delivery.md) | **WebSocket where a push beats a poll, HTTPS everywhere else.** **Proposed 2026-09-28.** A foreground client holds one WebSocket to the DS: the DS pushes `{journal, head}` notices for the journals it reads (rows are still fetched over the signed HTTPS GET, so a lost notice costs latency, never a message) and relays MLS-sealed ephemeral signals (typing leaves the journal; read receipts stay). Subscriptions carry the ADR-0053 proof and are re-verified on epoch rotation; long poll stays as the transport fallback; background delivery stays on APNs / FCM / Web Push; auth, discovery, tokens, storage, push-gw and publishing stay HTTPS; gRPC rejected (gRPC-Web proxy in the browser, HTTP/2 inside the ADR-0026 tunnels). Capacity of the test host as configured, measured 2026-09-28: about 700 concurrent sockets, bound by nginx `worker_connections 768` x 2 workers at two connections per proxied socket, then the DS soft open-file limit 1024; with those raised, about 17 000 (host `fs.file-max` 51200 at three descriptors per socket; memory is about 137 KiB per idle socket, measured on Linux with axum 0.8.9, about 20 000 in the 2.8 GiB available). Plan S1-S4 in the ADR |
| [0056](docs/adr/0056-pairing-names-account-by-handle.md) | **A fresh device names its account by login handle, not by account id.** **Accepted 2026-09-28.** Since ADR-0054 S3 a device with no cached account id could not start another-device recovery (the id came from a username lookup, which now needs a session). `PairingQr` v2 carries the S5 `login_handle` (field 5; `account_id` field 2 reserved) and `pairing::decode(bytes, expected_login_handle)` compares it with the confirming device's own handle; `POST /device/request` takes the handle (unknown handles land on a stand-in account nobody polls); `POST /device/authorize/redeem` takes the grant alone and returns the account id. A `lakko-mls` codec change under invariant #3: property tests + the `pairing_qr` fuzz target. Rejected: a pre-session id resolve (a UUIDv7 stand-in leaks existence through its timestamp). **S1 built 2026-09-28** (proto, core, fuzz, both FFI surfaces, bindings, auth + migration `0022`, host-tested against the local MariaDB); S2 (the three clients) open |
| [0057](docs/adr/0057-journal-ownership.md) | **A journal has an owner; only those who change a group register its epochs.** **Accepted 2026-09-28** (option (a) of the security pass; closes its HIGH finding on ADR-0053). Every group journal gets a root key derived per group from the owner's account seed (DeviceGroup: the account; PublisherGroup: the channel owner; ordinary group: the ADR-0022 owner). Epoch keys are registered by registrars the root appoints in three ranks - root, admin, member (ADR-0050 self-service commits) - with seeds derived by the root and sent in-group; a higher rank replaces what a lower one registered and revokes it in the same transaction, so an evictee's fake next epoch and its registrar key go with the removal. A same-root claim is a root reset, which re-claims a re-founded DeviceGroup / PublisherGroup. A push routing id is bound to one journal (keyed hash, unique) by the root or an admin; a wake naming another journal's id is dropped. Residuals: an admin removed by another admin races until the owner acts; squatting a deterministic id before the owner's first claim. **S1 + S2 built 2026-09-28** (DS host-tested against the local MariaDB; web, iOS and Android host-tested; deployed to the test stack, not walked live): `lakko-ds-proof::statement` (derivations, six signed statements), `services/ds/src/ownership.rs` + migration `0006`, the `lakko-contacts::registrar_seed` envelope, both FFI surfaces, the NSE decode arm. Built differently from the text, recorded in the ADR: the registrar seed is **sealed to the member's account X25519 key** (in the clear, every member would read every admin's seed); a same-root claim is idempotent and replacing the chain is an explicit **reset**; the replace rule covers a **suffix** of lower-rank registrations, so an evictee that raced two epochs ahead is still replaced in one step; an ownership transfer hands the old owner's root seed to the new owner (envelope rank `root`, `composeRootHandoverEnvelope`), who signs `root` naming its own root and re-appoints everyone; a lazy claim that meets `409` registers nothing. **Second security pass 2026-09-28 (REQUEST CHANGES, four HIGH), fixed the same day and next:** registration carries a mode - *Append* only at `latest + 1`, a member at most one epoch past the newest admin / root registration, *Replace* only over strictly lower ranks with no root override (an owner or admin repairs a fake next epoch from above); a root-only `registrars-set` replaces the whole registrar set from the roster; claims fail closed at creation on all three clients; the root handover survives retries (new owner keeps its registrar seed until confirmed, the old owner probes with a `root` statement naming itself, the receiver keeps the opened seed wrapped); a push binding precedes the secret's broadcast. Unbuilt: a registrar for co-publishers (a PublisherGroup carries no application message) |
| [0058](docs/adr/0058-chat-folders.md) | **Chat folders, carried between the account's own devices.** **Accepted 2026-09-28 (the maintainer chose a per-element merge, a final deletion, and to cover the existing synced maps too); S1 core + both FFI and the three clients built 2026-09-28, merged into main 2026-09-29; not walked live.** UX spec [`docs/ux/chat-folders.md`](docs/ux/chat-folders.md). A folder is a saved view of the merged list (All chats stays unchanged): three kind toggles, three exclude toggles (muted / read / archived), chats added and excluded by hand, its own pins; a folder bar of tabs on all three clients and all widths. State is a set of independently HLC-stamped registers - folder meta, one `NONE` / `INCLUDED` / `EXCLUDED` register per (folder, conversation), the pin list, a removal tombstone, the folder order - each conversation named by a `(kind, id)` ref so channels fit; LWW per register with a bytewise tiebreak on equal stamps; a folder deletion is final (later edits to it are refused); tombstones and `NONE` kept. Two new DeviceGroup kinds: `FolderChanges` 18 (a live edit) and `StateSnapshot` 19 (14-17 stay with ADR-0050). A late device catches up by a snapshot of the whole folded state - folders AND the four existing maps (pins / mutes / archive, read cursors, drafts, conversation order), which no new device receives today - re-sent with original stamps by the lowest-leaf prior member on DeviceGroup growth, and from `BackupPayload` v5 when no other device is alive. Bounded fail-closed decode; client-side creation limits (20 folders, 200 + 200 chats, 10 pins) are not decode bounds. Fold in `lakko-sync` on both FFI surfaces (replacing the three hand-written folds); `folder_matches` + `order_conversations_in_folder` in `lakko-ui-rules`. No server change; invariants #2 / #5 hold |

The full strategic plan and trade-offs live in `~/.claude/plans/1-nested-wreath.md`.
The tactical decomposition of all 6 phases with sprint breakdowns lives in [`docs/ROADMAP.md`](docs/ROADMAP.md).
**What an ordinary user can actually rely on today lives in [`docs/PRODUCT-READINESS.md`](docs/PRODUCT-READINESS.md) (2026-09-17): it is the product-level truth and overrides any per-ADR "built end-to-end" claim in the table above; update it with every change that moves one of its rows, and never mark a row OK on the strength of an ADR.**
The Phase 3 closeout punch list — gap inventory + sprint waves with exit criteria — lives in [`docs/ROADMAP-PHASE3-CLOSEOUT.md`](docs/ROADMAP-PHASE3-CLOSEOUT.md).

## Module map

### `crates/` — Rust core

| Crate | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `lakko-core` | **An empty placeholder, not an umbrella.** `src/lib.rs` is twelve lines with no `pub use` and no `mod`, its `Cargo.toml` has no `[dependencies]` section at all, and nothing in `crates/`, `services/`, `apps/` or `tools/` names `lakko_core`. Reach for the individual crate; this row exists so nobody hunts for a re-export surface that was never built |
| `lakko-crypto` | HKDF-SHA-256, AEAD (`Algorithm::{ChaCha20Poly1305, Aes256Gcm}`), Argon2id, BLAKE3, X25519 agreement, Ed25519 signing — modules `aead`, `agreement`, `error`, `hash`, `kdf`, `random`, `secret`, `signing`. **HPKE and OPRF are NOT here** despite the crate's own Cargo description saying so (they never were — no commit ever added either): the RFC 9180 `hpke` crate lives only in `lakko-transport`'s ECH adapter, the OPRF (`voprf`) in `lakko-discovery-client` / `lakko-auth-client` / `services/discovery`, and the "HPKE seed envelope" of `lakko-channels` is a DHKEM-shaped construction composed **from** this crate's X25519 + HKDF + AEAD rather than an HPKE implementation |
| `lakko-mls` | OpenMLS wrapper, group state, exporters for calls. **Group-admin roles (ADR-0022)** — `group_roles.rs` (`RoleTable`: owner + admin set keyed on signature pubkey; bounded fail-closed `0xFF02` codec) + `Group::process_incoming_verifying_roles` (the peer-verified refuse-to-apply gate: committer via `ProcessedMessage::sender()`, pre-merge role read, fail-closed on an unauthorized add/remove/role-change) + `set_group_roles` (owner GCE commit) + `Group::create` seeds owner-at-creation; `lakko_leaf_capabilities` advertises both `0xFF01` (DeviceGroup) + `0xFF02` (roles). 8 role property tests + the `role_table_decode` fuzz target. **External-join approval (ADR-0027, G-3)** — `Incoming::ExternalJoinProposal { joiner_signature_key }` surfaces a non-member's external Add (extracted from its KeyPackage, refusing a non-Add / non-`NewMemberProposal` sender) into an in-memory per-group queue (cap + signature-key dedup); `approve_external_join` (role-gated owner/admin, reuses the audited `add_member` path → Commit + Welcome) / `reject_external_join` / `pending_external_joins`. **S7 (`576ea14`) converged it onto the sweep**: `Group::approve_external_join` now publishes an `Add` **proposal the approving admin authors itself**, carrying the retained KeyPackage — so it advances no epoch, returns no Welcome, leaves the ADR-0040 outbox entirely, and two admins approving two joiners against one epoch compose instead of forking. `refuse_if_proposals_pending` no longer reaches it (there is no direct commit left to guard), closing the user-visible dead end. The direct arm survives **only** as `pub(crate) approve_external_join_directly`, one caller, refusing any group that carries a role table — because `PublisherGroup` seeds none and the proposal path refuses a table-less group by construction, so the ADR-0028 OQ3 co-publisher approve keeps its synchronous epoch advance and its load-bearing reseal. The `pending_external_joins` quarantine is **NOT** retired, contrary to decision 4: it is what keeps a non-member out of the member proposal store, whose quota a `/g/` link-holder could otherwise fill to freeze the group (**C-66**). `pending_external_joins()` filters members, so an approved joiner retires itself from every admin's list. The Welcome moved to the sweep, which posts a `WelcomeRendezvous` for **every** added key because it cannot tell which came from a link — **C-69**. Joiner side: `Identity::create_external_join_proposal(group_id, epoch)` (`key_package.rs`) crafts the external `Add` proposal as a non-member (embeds a fresh KeyPackage whose private keys persist in its own provider). 12 external-join property tests (the original 6, plus 6 S7 added for the proposal path — the planted-stranger exclusion and its control, a stranger's own `Add` never entering the proposal store, the table-less group keeping the direct arm, the direct arm refusing a group that has a committer, and an approve no longer blocked by a pending proposal) incl. a full in-process e2e (`joiner_api_full_loop_*`: non-member proposes → admin approves → Welcome via `WelcomeRendezvous` → joiner `join_from_welcome` → member). **Proposal-based membership ([ADR-0045](docs/adr/0045-remote-commit-epoch-fork.md), G-38 residual 1 — built end to end; **the residual is CLOSED for external joins too since S7**, so this parenthetical no longer reads "OPEN for external joins, since `approve_external_join` still eager-merges")** — `group_proposals.rs` is the new home of the **one** per-proposal rule: `ProposalKind` (`Add` / `Remove` / `Leave` / `Update` / `RoleChange` / `Unsupported`, the last always refused) + `classify` + **`authorise`**, written down once and consumed at **three** call sites so they cannot drift — the **receipt gate** inside `process_incoming_verifying_roles` (store an incoming proposal only if the *author's* role permits it, else drop it and surface `ProposalRecord { stored: false }`, which is the wire-visible form of a refusal since a proposal advances no state), the **sweep filter** (`CommitBuilder::build`'s closure; never `commit_to_pending_proposals`, which hardcodes `\|_\| true`), and the **peer-verified commit gate** (`authorise` over **every** proposal a received Commit carries, judged against the **pre-merge** table — the committer is not trusted). By-**reference** is judged against the proposer, by-**value** against the committer, so every direct-commit path is byte-for-byte its ADR-0022 self. Two corrections the build made to the ADR are recorded in the module docs: for a by-reference proposal the **receipt gate IS a boundary** (a peer that dropped it has nothing for OpenMLS's reference resolution to find → `InvalidCommit(MissingProposal)`, enforced by RFC 9420 itself), and the sweep filter's stated demotion race **is not reachable** (OpenMLS empties the store on every merged commit and the table can only change *by* a commit) so it is defence in depth over the store's other writers. Committer election: **`group_roles::committer_rank`** — rank 0 the owner, k+1 the k-th admin in the table's canonical order, keyed on **signature public key never leaf index**; a pure total function of the authenticated `0xFF02` extension, so no election message and no committer state. Proposer side: `propose_add_member` / `propose_add_member_from_wire` / `propose_remove_member` / `propose_group_roles` (each locally pre-authorised as a courtesy — the peers' gates are the enforcement), `pending_proposals`, `own_committer_rank`. Committer side: `commit_pending_proposals` (eager) and the **staged pair `stage_pending_proposals` / `has_staged_sweep` / `merge_staged_sweep` / `abandon_staged_sweep`** — the ADR-0045 rollback point, which is the **absence of the eager merge** rather than any retained snapshot: staging leaves the group at epoch N holding an un-merged commit so the caller can publish, read the DS journal verdict and only then merge or abandon. This is **ADR-0040's deferred "Option B" scoped to the sweep path only** (the remaining direct-commit paths still merge eagerly, so the outbox wraps both unchanged — after S7 that is `add_member` / `remove_member` / `set_group_roles` plus `self_update`, and the crate-private `approve_external_join_directly` reachable only through `PublisherGroup`), and the discard is per-group `clear_pending_commit`, not a whole-provider `restore_storage` that would roll sibling groups back. **A staged sweep freezes that group's own membership ops** (OpenMLS `is_operational()`) and is **durable**, so a caller must reach a verdict; incoming messages are unaffected. All three clients discharge that obligation the same way — every sweep adjudicates what it staged, an owed verdict is resolved at restart through ADR-0040's `sweepPendingCommits` seam, and the owed-verdict check runs **ahead of** the acting-committer rank check, since an admin can be demoted while holding one and gating the resolution behind the rank check would freeze that device permanently. Note the staged sweep also does **not** block a chat send (`create_message` gates on `is_active`, not `is_operational`), which is what makes an epoch straddle a certainty for the duration of a verdict read and why the clients serialize seal-and-publish under the per-group lock (**C-64**). **`create_application_message` parks the pending proposal set around the encrypt call** — OpenMLS refuses `create_message` while the proposal store is non-empty, so a stored proposal would otherwise mute the whole group's chat until the sweep; the set never leaves the OpenMLS storage provider (so `snapshot_storage` / `load` keep working unchanged) and a failed unpark beats a successful send. **Security-review hardening (2026-08-29, C-66/C-67/C-68)** — `authorise` now refuses `ProposalKind::Update` outright (it bypassed `owner_survives`, and openmls does not bind an Update's leaf node to the sender's existing key, so a member could rotate its own key out from under the role table; each also carries a fresh encryption key hence a distinct `ProposalRef`, which the dedup cannot collapse, so 64 filled the pending set). `sweep_keeps` grew from two clauses to five, enumerating fifteen `CommitBuilder::build` hard-failure conditions across three families — the single `GroupContextExtensions` slot (C-65), key uniqueness across signature / encryption / init keys, and the added leaf's admissibility to *this* group (capabilities, required capabilities, leaf extensions, credential types, **KeyPackage lifetime**) — because an unbuildable sweep is a **permanent, group-wide, invisible** membership freeze: the store empties only on a merged commit, the direct paths are refused while proposals pend, nothing discards, and `create_application_message` parks the set so chat keeps working over it. One proposal suffices, and the lifetime condition fires from **elapsed time alone** against a request decision 2 gives no deadline. Since fifteen conditions is a mirror of rules this crate does not own, `stage_pending_proposals` backs them with `narrow_sweep_by_exclusion` — additive selection plus one re-offer pass, bounded by `2 x candidates + 1` builds, which needs to know only *which* proposal is uncommittable and never *why*, and which immediately rescued a live unenumerated case (an admin proposing its own departure wedges its own sweep with `CannotRemoveSelf`). Each trial re-asks `owner_survives`, because dropping an `Add` can strand a role change naming that person owner — openmls builds it, every peer refuses it, trading a local freeze for a fork. And `pending_capacity` / `is_owner_response` admit a small reserve past `MAX_PENDING_PROPOSALS` to the **owner's** `Remove` or `RoleChange` only, shared by `authorise_own_proposal` and `gate_incoming_proposal` so the two cannot disagree — closing a lockout where a flooder took away the owner's means of response and the only exit committed the flood. **Automatic re-request (2026-09-01)** — the client half of the maintainer's rule *retry when a queue was full, never when the request was refused for its own state*. **The trigger is an observation, not a timer**, and that is the design: a queue drains when a committer sweeps rather than when time passes, so a schedule would fire uselessly or spin forever. All three clients re-send when the pending set is **strictly smaller** than at the refusal — a state their poll already reports. One rule, derived from the core's *definition* rather than from either reason's name: the set shrinks only when a commit **merges**, and `RetryLater` means *resolves once a committer has swept*, so a nineteenth `RetryLater` reason works with no edit here. No attempt cap — attempts are paid for by a sweep that was going to happen anyway, so an unbounded number cannot spin, while a cap would abandon a merely **busy** group; stopping is by **state** (membership ended, already applied, already pending, no role table). The record is in memory because its content is a comparison baseline and a count from a past session compares nothing. Firing is a lock-guarded take-and-remove that the **manual path also claims**, so a user tapping again disarms rather than races. Subjects keyed on the 32-byte signature key, never a leaf. **A fix this found, since closed end to end:** `approve_external_join` re-wrapped `propose_add_member`'s error in prose and destroyed the `RefusalReason` it had just produced, so an approve refused by a full queue reached every client with no reason at all and could never participate. The core now propagates the typed error and wraps only what carries none, and the client arm followed — **six kinds, not five**. Approving needed a third stopping condition the others do not (it opens with `role_table_or_refuse`, so a table-less group answers permanently and a retry would wait forever), carries **one** field as its descriptor because the joiner's signature key is both identity and handle, and delegates the not-retained rule to `externalJoinAffordance` rather than restating it. **Two in-memory lifetimes meet there and the mismatch is handled rather than prevented:** the quarantine lives on the `Group` while the retry record lives beside the chat, and the ADR-0040 crash-abort reset and the web rehydrate both rebuild the `Group` mid-session — so a record can outlive its request with no restart, and the verdict reads the live retained set and drops rather than firing an approve the core would refuse. **Typed refusal reasons (2026-09-01)** — `refusal.rs`: `RefusalReason` (18 fieldless variants) + `RefusalDisposition { RetryLater, DoNotRetry, NotPermitted }` + `Error::MembershipRefused { reason, detail }`, whose `Display` is byte-identical to the sentence the refusal always carried, so logs are unchanged and only the *reading* is new. Built because three clients had drifted three ways on one intended sentence — web surfaced the spec's string, iOS threw its own near-variant prose, Android surfaced the core diagnostic raw — and mapping core prose to fixed copy by substring is the drift class the UX spec flags. **The axis is three-valued and two would have been wrong:** under a two-valued availability/authorisation split `SweepAlreadyStaged` classifies as retryable while this crate's own docs say to reach the verdict rather than retry, so the type would have contradicted the crate. The cut is mechanical — **`RetryLater` is the disposition that resolves on its own**, the identical call succeeding once any committer sweeps with the caller doing nothing — and exactly two reasons qualify. **FFI shape forced by `flat_error`**, which lowers a variant as its ordinal plus `to_string()` and drops associated data: one flat `FfiError` variant per reason, **appended at the end** because uniffi decodes by ordinal and inserting mid-enum would make older bindings decode unrelated errors as the wrong variant. wasm throws a real `Error` carrying `refusalReason` / `refusalDisposition` as own properties, `.message` unchanged. `Incoming::ExternalJoinProposal` also gained **`retained`** — the quarantine used to drop a request at `MAX_PENDING_EXTERNAL_JOINS` while still firing the event, so an admin saw a pending request no approve could ever act on; reported as **data, not an error**, since erroring there would let a stranger fail a member's receive loop. **Membership state** — `Group::is_active()` (+ `isActive` on both FFI surfaces): OpenMLS marks a group `Inactive` when a commit removes your own leaf, persisting it **before** the merge, and documents it as a state the group cannot exit from. Reads still answer (`members`, `epoch`, `group_roles`) but are frozen; `create_message`, `export_secret` and — decisively — `unprotect_message` all refuse, the last **before it looks at the message and not epoch-scoped**, so nothing new can ever be decrypted, including old-epoch rows still unfetched on the DS. `epoch()` reports N+1 over epoch-N secrets, so a caller must consult this rather than reason from the epoch. Pinned by `tests/group_membership_state.rs`, whose property is that the accessor and the roster inference agree on every eviction path — not that the accessor is false, which would test OpenMLS. Deliberately **absent from `lakko-ffi-extension`** (see the ADR-0041 row: that process cannot persist a verdict, C-59). **Ownership transfer (ADR-0022's deferred follow-up, built here)** — `RoleTable::with_owner_transferred` + `OutgoingOwner::{Admin, Member}` + `Group::transfer_ownership` / `propose_ownership_transfer`: deliberately **not a new commit type**, just the owner-only role-table rewrite with the owner slot moved, judged against the pre-merge table. Its security content is **`group_proposals::owner_survives`** (**C-63**) — the one rule that judges a **commit** rather than a proposal: a commit must not leave the group with an owner who is not a member of it, nor erase the role table. It closes a hole that predates the feature (`set_group_roles` could always name an owner key no leaf holds — permanently un-administrable) and one ADR-0045 created (a transfer composed with the new owner's removal). 37 proposal + 12 ownership-transfer property tests in those two binaries (22 proposal at the ownership sprint; S7 and the security review grew it), plus per-layer isolation lib tests. `group_admin_roles.rs` is **no longer unchanged** — the security review added an eighth. The crate runs 297 tests (measured 2026-09-26) across the lib (186) and **nine** integration binaries: `group_proposals` (37), `membership_refusal_reason` (21), `group_ownership_transfer` (12), `device_leaves` (12), `group_admin_roles` (8), `group_id_width` (8), `end_to_end` (5), `group_membership_state` (5), `external_join_persistence` (3 — the ADR-0027 retained queue surviving a restart). **On both FFI surfaces since `6c77cd6`:** the staged pair (`stagePendingProposals` / `hasStagedSweep` / `mergeStagedSweep` / `abandonStagedSweep`) and ownership transfer (`transferOwnership` / `proposeOwnershipTransfer` / `OutgoingOwner`), so a client can stage-and-adjudicate rather than only eager-merge. **Peer ranking landed in `94da492`** (this row previously read "Not exposed: the free `committer_rank`" — that is CLOSED): `Group.committerRank(signatureKey)` on **both** surfaces answers the peer-facing half `ownCommitterRank` could not, returning `Some(0)` for the owner, `Some(k+1)` for the k-th admin, and `None` for a plain member / non-member / wrong-length key / no role table. It is a **`Group` method, not a free function taking a caller-supplied table**, and both surfaces carry the reason as a doc heading — *rank the key when you take the verdict, not when the beacon arrived*: a rank is a **position** in the role table's canonical order, so promoting, demoting or removing any admin renumbers every admin sorting after it, and a rank captured under one table compared against a rank taken under another is not merely stale but a position in a **different ordering**. Callers therefore persist the sender's 32-byte **signature key** (never a leaf index — OpenMLS reuses a vacated leaf for the next Add) and rank it at fold time. This **orders** the authorised committers; it does not widen them. `undefined`/`None` and `0` are never interchangeable — wasm's doc says `if (rank)` is wrong in both directions. Verified by `peer_committer_rank_agrees_with_own_rank_from_every_vantage_point` + `committer_rank_never_conflates_absent_with_rank_zero`. **DS access ([ADR-0053](docs/adr/0053-ds-journal-authorisation.md)), two read-only additions:** `Group::staged_export_secret` exports from the epoch a staged sweep moves to (OpenMLS's own staged-commit exporter), so a committer can register the next epoch's Delivery Service key before publishing; and `Group::process_knock` / `is_external_join_proposal_for` refuse a knock row unless it is a `PublicMessage` proposal from a `NewMemberProposal` sender framed for this group, before OpenMLS processes it. Pinned by `a_staged_sweep_exports_the_secret_of_the_epoch_it_moves_to` and `a_knock_row_is_processed_only_when_it_is_an_external_join_proposal`; on both FFI surfaces as `dsStagedAccessSigner` and `processKnock` (`Group` and `PublisherGroup`). A third, from the live walk: `Group::pending_external_join_account_key` names the account behind a pending external-join request from the retained KeyPackage's verified device binding, so an admin's pending list can show a contact by name instead of "Member" (the request is keyed on the device leaf); pinned by `a_pending_join_request_names_the_joiners_account`, on both FFI surfaces as `pendingExternalJoinAccountKey`. **Pairing payload v2 ([ADR-0056](docs/adr/0056-pairing-names-account-by-handle.md), 2026-09-28):** `pairing::encode(login_handle, key_package, rendezvous)` / `decode(bytes, expected_login_handle)` bind the payload to the ADR-0054 S5 32-byte login handle instead of the account id (`PairingQr` field 5, field 2 reserved, `PAIRING_VERSION` 2 and nothing else accepted), compared in constant time through `lakko_crypto::secret::SecretBytes`; 11 tests incl. three proptests (round trip, any wrong handle refused, arbitrary bytes never accepted unbound); on UniFFI as `buildPairingQr(loginHandle, keyPackage, rendezvousPseudonym)` / `parsePairingQr(wire, expectedLoginHandle)`, on wasm as `encodePairingQr` / `decodePairingQr` with the same argument order |
| `lakko-attachments` | **The chat wire envelope** — the JSON `Envelope` that every MLS Application payload actually carries, and the one place its grammar is defined. `compose` / `compose_edit` / `compose_delete` / `compose_reaction` / `compose_typing` / `compose_read_receipt` on the send side; `parse` + **`parse_body` → `MessageBody::{Chat,Edit,Delete,Reaction,Typing,ReadReceipt}`** on the receive side, bounded and fail-closed (>1 op field, or a reaction without a valid emoji, degrades to `Chat`). Also per-attachment encryption (`encrypt_attachment` / `decrypt_attachment` / `AttachmentRef`, blob id = SHA-256 of the ciphertext) against the content-addressed `services/storage`. **Media metadata ([ADR-0047](docs/adr/0047-media-messages-and-gif-proxy.md))** — `AttachmentRef` gains optional `kind` / `duration_ms` / `waveform` / `width` / `height` / `thumb` (`ThumbRef`, a second encrypted blob), `WIRE_VERSION` still 1. Deserialization goes through a lenient wire shape: a field of the wrong JSON type or outside its bound is **dropped alone** and never fails the ref or the envelope (a failed envelope would render as raw JSON through the legacy fallback). Bounds: duration kept only for `voice` (1..=15 min) / `video_note` (1..=60 s), waveform 1..=128 decoded bytes, dimensions 1..=4096, thumb size 1..=256 KiB with a decodable ref; zero is dropped too. The same sanitizer runs in `compose`, so the wire never carries a value receivers discard. `MediaKind::{Voice,VideoNote,Gif,Other}` never fails on an unknown kind; **inline playback is `inline_media_type(mime)` alone** (MIME essence, case-insensitive: `image/{jpeg,png,gif,webp}`, `audio/mp4`, `video/mp4`; never `kind`, never SVG), also exported on UniFFI + wasm. `RawMediaMetadata::bounded` applies the same bounds to already-decoded values (the history-bundle route), pinned to the envelope route by a test. **Reply ([ADR-0052](docs/adr/0052-reply-forward-pin.md) decision 1)** — three more optional lenient fields beside the text, `reply_to` (the DS `messageId` answered) + the snapshot `reply_author` (<= 255 bytes) / `reply_excerpt` (<= 512 bytes), **never in the op set**: `MessageBody::Chat` carries `reply: Option<Reply>`, an op envelope that also carries them is still that op, a snapshot without a target is dropped, an over-long snapshot is dropped alone on parse and truncated on a char boundary by `compose_reply`; the quote is what the replier saw and is never re-read from the original (a later edit or delete changes nothing), one level deep. On UniFFI `compose_reply_envelope` + `ParsedBody.reply_to/reply_author/reply_excerpt`, on wasm `EnvelopeBuilder.buildReply` / `composeReplyEnvelopeText` + the `replyTo` (`bigint`) / `replyAuthor` / `replyExcerpt` getters; the NSE extension matches the reply away (a notification previews no quote). **Forward ([ADR-0052](docs/adr/0052-reply-forward-pin.md) decision 2)** — three more lenient fields beside the text: `forwarded_from` (the ORIGINAL author's display name as a snapshot, <= 255 bytes, a label and never a route to the person) and the origin pair `forward_origin_group` (standard base64 of the origin group id, <= 128 bytes) + `forward_origin_message` (its DS `messageId`), kept only as a whole; `MessageBody::Chat` carries `forward: Option<Forward { author, origin }>` (present when the name or a whole origin survives), `compose_forward` truncates the name and drops an over-long origin; attachments are RE-ENCRYPTED by the caller before compose (the refs passed are the new ones), so the two conversations never share a blob; the origin rides only inside the sealed payload. On UniFFI `compose_forward_envelope` + `ParsedBody.forwarded_from/forward_origin_group_b64/forward_origin_message_id`, on wasm `EnvelopeBuilder.buildForward` + the `forwardedFrom` / `forwardOriginGroupB64` / `forwardOriginMessageId` (`bigint`) getters. 62 tests + the `message_body_parse` fuzz target. **`parse_body`'s deliberate lossy-UTF-8 legacy fallback is why service envelopes need a filter ahead of it** — see the `lakko-ffi-extension` row |
| `lakko-proto` | Generated types from `schemas/` (7 `.proto` files via `build.rs`). **No service depends on it** — the dependents are all client-side (`lakko-mls`, `lakko-calls`, both FFI crates), so `schemas/` is the shared source of truth but this crate is not the shared artifact |
| `lakko-transport` | quinn (QUIC) + tungstenite (WS) + pluggable obfs trait + signed bootstrap rendezvous + DoH fetcher + chunked AEAD stream + SS-2022 TCP transport + ECH (parser + RustCrypto/hpke adapter wired into HTTP + WebTransport) (Phase 5: G5-5 + G5-5-doh + G5-aead-stream + G5-3-header + S-5-shadowsocks-tcp + G5-6 + G5-6-wireup all done; G5-3 complete; G5-6 wire-up landed via [ADR-0012](docs/adr/0012-ech-hpke-adapter.md)). `DohFetcher` resolves bootstrap domains over JSON-DoH (Cloudflare / Google / Quad9 / AdGuard), then IP-pinned HTTPS GET + 4-stage `BootstrapConfig::verify`. `DohFetcher::fetch_pinned_bytes(domain, path, max_bytes)` exposes the same IP-pin pipeline as a generic `Vec<u8>` getter with body-size cap — it is **designed for** downstream callers (TUF metadata fetch via `lakko_tuf::urls`, KeyPackage bundles, signed identity blobs) to reuse rather than re-implement IP pinning — stated in the present tense here for a long time, but the only production call site is `lakko-ffi-uniffi`'s bootstrap-config fetch; none of those three consumers exists yet; refuses payloads exceeding the cap with `DohError::BodyTooLarge`. `AeadStreamWriter` / `AeadStreamReader` ship chunked ChaCha20-Poly1305 framing (16 KiB max chunk). `ShadowsocksTransport::connect` + `accept_inbound` deliver a working SS-2022 client/server stack over TCP with full handshake (salt + sealed prelude) + per-direction subkey separation + AEAD body. `parse_ech_config_list` decodes draft-ietf-tls-esni-18 §4 wire format with strict version pinning (0xfe0d only), bounded allocations (16 KiB outer / 16 inner configs / 4 KiB per config), full field validation (cipher_suites non-empty + 4-byte aligned, public_name 1..=255 + UTF-8, max_name_length non-zero). ECH wire-up (G5-6-wireup, ADR-0012) — `crates/lakko-transport/src/ech/adapter.rs` exposes three `LakkoHpkeSuite*` structs implementing `rustls::crypto::hpke::Hpke` on top of `hpke 0.13` (RustCrypto), bundled as `ALL_SUPPORTED_SUITES` for `rustls::client::EchConfig::new`; `wireup.rs::build_ech_mode_from_bytes` runs a two-stage validation (our parser + rustls's own) and returns `EchMode::Enable`. `HttpTransportConfig::ech_config_list` + `WebTransportConfig::ech_config_list_b64` plumb the bytes into the rustls ClientConfig builder; WebTransport bypasses `web-transport-quinn::ClientBuilder` when armed and assembles `quinn::ClientConfig` manually with a Lakko-side `CertHashVerifier` (W3C `serverCertificateHashes` semantics) + refuses ECH-without-cert-pins (`EchRequiresCertHashes`). REALITY is intentionally not wired for ECH — both target the same outer-ClientHello SNI slot for SNI hiding via different mechanisms (REALITY already provides SNI privacy). `rand_core` 0.9 `OsRng` wrapped via `UnwrapErr` to satisfy hpke's infallible `CryptoRng + RngCore` bound. rustls's IANA-aligned Hpke* enums imported via the `internal` re-export path (documented external-adapter consumer surface, but off the stable interface). REALITY v2 Path C — pre-shared-key model (G5-1-v2 + S-5-reality-driver-v2 + S-5-reality-server + S-5-reality-rng-hook all complete; both E2E integration tests green): publisher distributes 32-byte `tag_secret` per `(client, short_id)` via signed bootstrap config. Client surface (4 pieces): (1) `reality::cert::AcceptAnyCertVerifier` (pub(crate)-scoped, REALITY-aware safety contract: PKI is bypassed because the cryptographic auth lives in session_id + ServerHello.Random HMAC chain, not in TLS PKI); (2) `reality::driver` with `build_reality_client_config` + `build_reality_client_connection` (arms per-thread `RealityRng` inject slot with the REALITY authenticator immediately before `ClientConnection::new`, so `SessionId::random` consumes the authenticator bytes and they enter rustls's transcript hash) + `drive_handshake` async byte-intercept loop manually driving `rustls::ClientConnection` via `write_tls`/`read_tls` + post-loop ClientFinished drain (TLS 1.3 requires flushing writes queued by the final `process_new_packets` after `is_handshaking()` goes false) + (optionally) `extract_server_hello_random` + `verify_server_response` on the first inbound record, refusing with typed `RealityDriverError::ServerAuth` if HMAC doesn't match (active-probe detection); (3) `reality::stream::RealityConnection<S>` — post-handshake `Connection` trait impl that shuttles length-prefixed (u32-BE) frames inside the TLS tunnel via rustls's plaintext writer + buffered plaintext reader (pre-loop `pump_decrypt` in `read_until` drains rustls-internal plaintext before blocking on `socket.read`, defending against TCP coalescing on loopback), with `MAX_FRAME_BYTES` cap on both `send` and `recv`, idempotent `close` driving `send_close_notify` + socket shutdown; (4) `RealityTransport::connect` wire-up — opens TCP, builds rustls `ClientConnection` pointed at `RealityConfig::sni`, drives the handshake, returns `Box<dyn Connection>` (no silent downgrade per security invariant #4). Server surface: `reality::handshake::{extract_client_hello_session_id, patch_server_hello_random}` wire-format mirrors; `reality::server_driver::{drive_server_handshake, build_reality_server_config, make_self_signed_cert}` server-side byte-intercept driver verifying the inbound ClientHello.session_id against an `(short_id, tag_secret)` allow-list and surfacing typed `AcceptedClient` on success (arms `RealityRng` with `compute_server_response` bytes before the first `process_new_packets` so `ServerHello.Random` receives the mutual-auth HMAC); `reality::server_stream::RealityServerConnection<S>` server-side `Connection` impl (same pre-loop `pump_decrypt` fix); `reality::listener::RealityListener` accept loop over TCP. `reality::rng::RealityRng` — custom `rustls::crypto::SecureRandom` impl wired as `CryptoProvider::secure_random`; per-thread one-shot `Cell<Option<[u8;32]>>` inject slot consumed by the first `fill(32)` call on the thread, all other fills delegate to ring's `SystemRandom`. Client builds session_id auth as `HMAC(HKDF(tag_secret, "lakko/reality/v2"), short_id || ts)` truncated to 16B. v2 dropped ECDH from v1 because rustls 0.23 doesn't expose TLS 1.3 ephemeral-X25519 hook — Path C uses stock rustls; cost is wire-incompatibility with upstream xtls/REALITY (Lakko closed ecosystem) + loss of authenticator-level forward-secrecy (cover TLS FS preserved, mitigated by tag_secret rotation through bootstrap config TTL).
