Strongly-typed `RealityConfig` (with `tag_secret_b64` instead of v1's `server_pubkey_b64`) / `RealityTagSecret` / `RealityShortId`, eager parse-time validation. 78 unit tests across `crates/lakko-transport/src/reality/` (25 handshake-wire + 14 authenticator + 8 config + 7 driver + 7 server_driver + 6 rng + 5 transport + 4 stream + 2 cert; the old "62 … (13 + 7 + 5 + 25 + 6 + 4 + 2 + 8 + misc)" was both stale and self-inconsistent — its own parts summed to 70) plus 3 E2E integration tests (all green: `reality_v2_listener_refuses_active_prober` (wrong-tag_secret REALITY prober) + `reality_v2_listener_refuses_a_vanilla_tls_prober` (G5-7-reality-probe — a plain non-REALITY TLS prober, the commonest censor probe vector, is refused; the refusal is a connection DROP — full probe-INDISTINGUISHABILITY from the cover site needs the deferred live-cover-proxy) + `reality_v2_loopback_roundtrip`). WebTransport real impl (G5-4): `WebTransportPt` over `web-transport-quinn` 0.11, cert-hash pinning (W3C `serverCertificateHashes` mode, SHA-256 of full DER cert) + system-roots fallback, u32-BE length-prefixed framing with `MAX_FRAME_BYTES` enforcement, 13 unit tests. `cert_hashes_b64` is the wire-config field name (renamed from `spki_pins_b64` for spec alignment — cert-hash ≠ SPKI). MASQUE foundation (G5-2): `MasqueConfig` parser (eager validation of proxy https + inner_target host:port + bearer), `build_connect_udp_request` emitting RFC 9298 §3 pseudo-headers + RFC 9297 §3.4 `capsule-protocol`, HTTP Datagram codec per RFC 9297 §3 + RFC 9298 §3 (QUIC varint Context ID, ctx=0 for CONNECT-UDP, 65 KiB hard cap). 32 unit tests. **`MasqueTransport::connect` is fully wired** (S-5-masque-handshake): opens a `quinn::Endpoint` (TLS 1.3 + `h3` ALPN + system roots), runs the h3 extended-CONNECT-UDP request, awaits the 200, and returns a `MasqueConnection` that send/recv-s UDP payloads over `quinn::Connection` datagrams (the h3-datagram higher-level surface is bypassed). `MasqueTransport::with_extra_roots(cfg, roots)` (G5-2 trust hook) folds caller-supplied DER trust anchors into the connect-time root store on top of the OS native certs — for pinning a **private-CA** proxy (the DoH-IP-pin / REALITY-custom-verifier posture) and to let a loopback e2e test trust a self-signed server. **Live roundtrip proven (G5-2)**: `masque_loopback_roundtrip` (`crates/lakko-transport/tests/masque_e2e.rs`) stands up a real in-process h3 CONNECT-UDP proxy (quinn server + `h3::server` extended-connect/datagram + verbatim QUIC-datagram echo) and drives production `connect` -> `send` -> `recv` through it on `with_extra_roots`-pinned trust. **That test caught + fixed a real bug**: `connect` dropped the h3 `SendRequest` at return, and h3's `SendRequest::drop` closes the whole HTTP/3 connection with `H3_NO_ERROR` once the last sender handle is gone — tearing the datagram tunnel down the instant it was established (the wiring/spike sprints validated compilation + the request path but never a datagram roundtrip). Fix: `MasqueConnection` now holds the `SendRequest` so its lifetime equals the tunnel's. **WS-over-tunnel (ADR-0026):** `byte_stream.rs` `ByteStreamConnection` adapts a framed `Connection` to one `AsyncRead + AsyncWrite` byte stream (pump task: split-duplex + mpsc + `select!` recv/send over the single `&mut conn`, the obfs-proxy relay idiom; writes chunked under `MAX_FRAME_BYTES`; 4 unit tests), and `ws_tunnel.rs` `tunneled_websocket` runs a real RFC 6455 client handshake + frame codec (`tokio-tungstenite`, `handshake`-only — no inner TLS, the tunnel encrypts) over it; the `services/obfs-proxy` loopback e2e proves a WebSocket (handshake + subprotocol + text/binary frames) round-trips through a REALITY tunnel + the byte-relay. See [`docs/security/transport.md`](docs/security/transport.md) T-1..T-15 |
| `lakko-storage` | Encrypted local SQLite store — `EncryptedStore` / `SqliteStore` / `GroupStateStore` / `MessageStore` / `InMemoryStore` over `rusqlite` in WAL mode. **Application-layer ChaCha20-Poly1305, explicitly NOT SQLCipher** (the crate's own `cipher.rs` argues the choice; full-DB SQLCipher is a listed Phase-2 deferral), and there is **no blob store here** — content-addressed ciphertext blobs live in `services/storage` with the client half in `lakko-attachments` |
| `lakko-backup` | Encrypted-backup envelope — a `BackupPayload` (v3: contacts incl. the per-contact `account_id`, v1/v2-compatible) wrapped in an AEAD envelope keyed by an Argon2id KEK derived from the BIP39 recovery phrase, uploaded as one opaque blob to `services/storage`. `head_frame.rs` carries the [ADR-0037](docs/adr/0037-backup-head-rollback-commitment.md) `0xB1` container framing (`frame_backup_blob` / `parse_backup_blob` → `BackupBlob::{Framed, Legacy}`) so head + envelope swap atomically in one `PUT`. `chain.rs` (the v2 sequence chain) is **dormant** — wired to no FFI and no client, which is exactly the correction ADR-0037 made to the earlier claim that it closed the rollback case. 44 tests + 3 fuzz targets |
| `lakko-sync` | Hybrid logical clock + edit chain + drafts. **Single-device only, and no CRDT** — the crate ships the single-device variants deliberately, and its `edit.rs` carries a "Why no character-level CRDT" section: an edit is a flat `Vec<EditOp>` LWW chain ordered by HLC. Cross-device sync of edits / read receipts / drafts over DeviceGroup application messages is listed as Phase-2 out-of-scope. Depended on by `lakko-ffi-wasm` only, so it does not reach iOS or Android at all **Since ADR-0058 (2026-09-28) it also holds `synced/`: the one fold every client uses for the DeviceGroup-synced state** - chat folders as per-register HLC stamps plus the four existing maps (flags, read cursors, drafts, conversation order); strictly-greater stamp wins, equal stamps break on the canonical bytes, a folder deletion is final; `merge_from` (backup restore), `snapshot` (growth re-send), `to_bytes` / `from_bytes` (a Lakko-own length-prefixed encoding, not protobuf) and `should_send_snapshot`. Exposed on UniFFI and wasm (`SyncedState`); proptests pin commutativity / idempotence over random permutations |
| `lakko-calls` | SFrame, jitter buffer, codec negotiation, ICE/TURN. Call **safety number** (G3-{1,2}-e2ee-fingerprint) — `safety_number::call_safety_number(seed)` = domain-separated BLAKE3 (`blake3_derive_key_from_material("lakko/call/safety-number/v1", seed)`) of the shared per-call MLS-exporter seed, rendered as 4 groups of 4 decimal digits (golden-pinned, canonical so all platforms render byte-identical). Displayed in the in-call E2EE pill for an out-of-band MITM check (matching codes = same E2EE root key, invariant #4); FFI-exposed as `callSafetyNumber` (uniffi + wasm), wired into the SFU-call E2EE pill on web + iOS + Android. **Mid-call rekey ([ADR-0029](docs/adr/0029-mid-call-key-rotation.md), G-18)** — `call_key_ring::CallKeyRing`: the seed-based overlap keyer (current + previous `CallKey`; `try_open_with_overlap` / `try_open_framed_with_overlap` try current then, during the window, previous; `rekey_from_seed_at` / `prune_expired` / `has_live_previous`; time-source-free, `[OVERLAP_MIN_MS, OVERLAP_MAX_MS]` clamp, 7 tests in `call_key_ring.rs`). `GroupCallSession` delegates its overlap fields to it (23 tests in `group_call.rs`, 8 of them overlap/rekey). Note the whole crate's "property tests" are plain `#[test]` functions — `lakko-calls` has no `proptest` dependency. FFI-exposed (uniffi + wasm) + surfaced in the `lakko-sframe-native` C ABI, and wired into all three clients' SFU call sessions to rotate the call key on a mid-call MLS epoch advance. **Per-(leaf, stream) key derivation via the header KID ([ADR-0035](docs/adr/0035-sframe-per-stream-kid-key.md), supersedes [ADR-0034](docs/adr/0034-sframe-per-media-stream-key.md)'s per-media-TYPE discriminator)** — the SFrame header KID is now a per-`(sender_leaf, stream)` identifier `KID = (sender_leaf << 8) | stream_index`; `SframeHeader::kid_for(leaf, stream)` composes it (fail-closed `StreamIndexOverflow` on `stream > 255`), `leaf_of`/`stream_of` split it. `CallKey::participant_key(kid: u64)` folds the FULL KID into **both** HKDF `info` strings (`participant_key_for(leaf, stream)` is the sender convenience), so every one of a participant's concurrent streams — audio (0), camera (1), screen-share (2), … — derives a **disjoint** `(key, nonce_salt)` space for **any** stream count (0034's per-media-TYPE tag re-collided two video streams from one leaf; the KID is the only per-stream signal both endpoints share through the SFU, which forwards the RFC 9605 header verbatim). The receiver reads the KID off each frame's header and derives `participant_key(KID)` with **no** local media/kind signal. `ParticipantKey` carries the `kid` (`sender_leaf()`/`stream_index()` accessors); `seal_framed` stamps it, `open_framed` checks it. `MediaType` removed. Property test `three_streams_of_one_leaf_get_distinct_keys_and_nonces` (audio + 2 video). The `SframeHeader` codec / `header_decode` fuzz / ADR-0013 AAD are unchanged (extended-KID form). Threaded through `CallKeyRing` + `GroupCallSession` |
| `lakko-channels` | Two-tier broadcast. Publisher path: PublisherGroup exporter seed + per-message AEAD + Ed25519 signature on every post. Subscriber path: HPKE seed envelope (`seal_seed` / `open_seed`) — full surface exposed via UniFFI + wasm-bindgen (G4-3c). See [ADR-0010](docs/adr/0010-channels-two-tier-broadcast.md). **Channel metadata record ([ADR-0051](docs/adr/0051-group-and-channel-names.md))** — `meta.rs`: `ChannelMeta { name, description, updated_at_ms }` composed as the plaintext of an ordinary post (`CHANNEL_META_DOMAIN_V1 = "lakko/channel-meta/v1\0"`, 22 bytes, frozen; name 1..=64 scalars / 255 bytes, description 0..=300 scalars / 1024 bytes with line breaks allowed; `char::is_control` and the bidi/invisible `Cf` table refused, ZWJ/ZWNJ permitted; whole-record rejection, compose refuses what decode refuses) plus `classify_post_plaintext(&[u8]) -> PostContent::{Text, Meta, Malformed}` — the first content discriminator the post plaintext ever had, domain prefix checked first so a text post can never collide (the trailing NUL) and a domain match that fails a bound is `Malformed`, dropped rather than rendered — and `channel_meta_is_newer` (strictly greater). 23 tests + fuzz `channel_meta_decode`; the `post.rs` wire header is unchanged and the record consumes a real counter |
| `lakko-contacts` | Username discovery (OPRF), invite capabilities, pseudonym lifecycle. **Profile envelope + username rule ([ADR-0048](docs/adr/0048-profile-names.md))** — `profile.rs`: `Profile { display_name, username, updated_at_ms }`, a bounded fail-closed envelope (`lakko/profile/v1\0` domain, version byte, 8-byte BE stamp, one-byte-length name and username, then a one-byte-length account id of 0 or 16 bytes — the ADR-0021 peer-tag input that lets a group-call tile name a member who is not a contact, added 2026-09-19 as version `0x02`, `0x01` refused; min 29 / max 332 bytes) rejected as a whole on any bound violation (UTF-8, trimmed, 1-64 scalars, <= 255 bytes, no line break, no `Cc`, no `Cf` via the explicit `FORMAT_CHAR_RANGES` table), `compose_profile` / `try_unwrap_profile` / `decode_profile` / `normalize_display_name` / `profile_is_newer` (strictly greater), dispatched as `IncomingEnvelope::Profile` (the enum is no longer `Copy`), fuzz `profile_decode`. `username.rs`: `check_canonical_username` + `UsernameError` (reason codes that never echo the input) — the one definition of the `[a-z0-9_]{3,32}` rule, which `lakko-discovery-client` depends on, placed here because it needs no Unicode tables and this crate is what the NSE extension links. `request.rs`: the **sealed plaintext is `profile_len(u16 BE) ‖ profile ‖ intro`**; `compose` takes `Option<&Profile>`, `open_envelope` returns `(Option<Profile>, intro)` — an invalid profile inside intact framing yields `None` with the intro kept, broken framing is `InvalidEncoding`. **Profile request ([ADR-0048](docs/adr/0048-profile-names.md) §2, trigger 4)** — `profile_request.rs`: `PROFILE_REQUEST_ENVELOPE_DOMAIN_V1 = "lakko/profile-request/v1\0"` (25 bytes, frozen) ‖ version `0x01`, 26 bytes and no fields, for the reasons the beacon gives; `compose_profile_request` / `try_unwrap_profile_request` / `decode_profile_request`, dispatched as `IncomingEnvelope::ProfileRequest` ahead of the intro fallback, plus the pure `should_request_profiles(joined_just_now, unnamed_members, last_requested_at_ms, now_ms)` with `PROFILE_REQUEST_MIN_INTERVAL_MS` = 24 h (always on a join; otherwise only with an unnamed member and no sooner than a day after the last request; a backwards clock reads as recently asked). The answer is the ordinary profile envelope through the growth publisher, so N members answering one request are throttled and jittered like a growth re-send. 8 tests + fuzz `profile_request_decode`; on UniFFI and wasm as `composeProfileRequest` / `decodeProfileRequest` / `profileRequestEnvelopeLen` / `profileRequestMinIntervalMs` / `shouldRequestProfiles`; the extension crate exports decode and the length only. **Group name envelope ([ADR-0051](docs/adr/0051-group-and-channel-names.md))** — `group_name.rs`: `GroupName { name, updated_at_ms }`, `GROUP_NAME_ENVELOPE_DOMAIN_V1 = "lakko/group-name/v1\0"` (20 bytes, frozen), wire `DOMAIN ‖ version ‖ updated_at_ms(8 BE) ‖ name_len(1) ‖ name`, min 30 / max 285 bytes; an empty name clears, a non-empty one passes the profile display-name rule verbatim (`GroupName::validate` reuses `check_display_name`); `compose_group_name` / `try_unwrap_group_name` / `decode_group_name` / `normalize_group_name` / `group_name_is_newer` (strict); dispatched as `IncomingEnvelope::GroupName` (step 7, ahead of the intro fallback). The envelope establishes nothing about authority: the receiver resolves the MLS sender to the account key at receipt (C-68) and admits the name only from the owner or an admin of the live role table (a table-less group is open), under the same per-group lock as the push routing secret (C-62 sub-invariant 5); a growth re-send carries the original stamp so it can never clobber a later rename (pinned by `a_growth_resend_with_the_original_stamp_never_clobbers_a_rename`). 19 tests + fuzz `group_name_decode`; the sibling-isolation tests in `profile.rs` and `committer_beacon.rs` now list every domain. **Shared-pin envelope ([ADR-0052](docs/adr/0052-reply-forward-pin.md) decision 3)** — `pin.rs`: `PinOp { message_id, action: Pin | Unpin, updated_at_ms }`, `PIN_ENVELOPE_DOMAIN_V1 = "lakko/pin/v1\0"` (13 bytes, frozen), wire `DOMAIN ‖ version ‖ updated_at_ms(8 BE) ‖ action(1) ‖ message_id(8 BE i64)`, a fixed `PIN_ENVELOPE_LEN` = 31; one envelope names ONE message, so a conversation's shared pins are a set the receiver folds envelope by envelope and "several pins, no product limit" costs the codec nothing; `compose_pin` (infallible) / `try_unwrap_pin` / `decode_pin` / `pin_is_newer` (strictly greater per message; a re-send carries the original stamp and is not an update, and an unpin is STORED rather than deleted so a stale pin can never resurrect it); dispatched as `IncomingEnvelope::Pin` (step 7c). Authority is the receiver's: owner or admin of the live role table resolved at receipt (C-68), any member of a table-less group, either participant of a one-to-one, under the per-group lock (C-62 sub-invariant 5). 10 tests + fuzz `pin_decode`; on both FFI surfaces as `composePinEnvelope` / `decodePinEnvelope` / `pinIsNewer` + the `Pin` dispatch kind (uniffi `FfiPinOp { message_id, pinned, updated_at_ms }`, wasm `PinOp`); the extension crate decodes it and exports the length only. **Registrar-seed envelope ([ADR-0057](docs/adr/0057-journal-ownership.md))** — `registrar_seed.rs`: a journal root hands one member its DS registrar seed inside the group: `lakko/ds-registrar-seed/v1\0 ‖ version ‖ journal_id(32) ‖ member_account_key(32) ‖ rank(1: member, 2: admin) ‖ sealed(80)`, fixed `REGISTRAR_SEED_ENVELOPE_LEN` = 173, the seed **sealed to the member's account X25519 key** with the `envelope` sealed box and the clear header as AAD (in the clear, every member would read every admin's seed); `compose_registrar_seed` / `try_unwrap_registrar_seed` / `decode_registrar_seed` / `RegistrarSeedEnvelope::open`, dispatched as `IncomingEnvelope::RegistrarSeed` (step 7d). The receiver keeps it only when addressed to its own account key and the sender resolves (C-68) to the owner. Fuzz `registrar_seed_decode`; FFI `composeRegistrarSeedEnvelope` / `decodeRegistrarSeedEnvelope` / `openRegistrarSeedEnvelope(plaintext, accountIdentity)` + the `RegistrarSeed` dispatch kind (payload = the whole envelope); the extension crate decodes it and exports the length only. **Group join-ticket (ADR-0028, G-3)** — `group_invite.rs` (`GroupInviteCapability` + `compose`/`decode_and_verify`: a bounded fail-closed Ed25519-signed capability carrying a group's `group_id` for a joiner-initiated external join; `/g/<base64url>` inline link mirroring the ADR-0009 `call_invite` codec; **v2 since 2026-09-26 (flag day, v1 refused): an optional sealed preview** — `GroupInvitePreview { group_name, inviter_name, member_count }` encoded `version ‖ name_len ‖ name ‖ inviter_len ‖ inviter ‖ count(2 BE)`, sealed with `lakko_crypto::aead::seal_with_key` under a key HKDF-SHA256-derived from a random 12-byte **link secret** (`LINK_SECRET_LEN`, 16 base64url chars since the 2026-09-27 short-link amendment; `info` binds the group id and issuer) the client carries in the URL **fragment** (`#<base64url>`, never sent to a server), embedded inside the signature (`compose_with_preview` / `generate_link_key` / `GroupInviteCapability::open_preview`, `MAX_SEALED_PREVIEW_LEN` bounds the field before allocation, both names follow the display-name rule; UX `docs/ux/group-invite-page.md`); on both FFI surfaces as `composeGroupInviteCapabilityWithPreview` and `hasPreview` / `openPreview(linkSecret)`; + `group_revoke_canonical_bytes`/`group_sign_revocation`: issuer-signed revocation, domain `lakko-group-invite-revoke/v1`, cross-kind-isolated, driving the `DELETE /discovery/group-invite/{blob_id}` ledger delete). **Sealed invite links (2026-09-28 security pass, ADR-0028 / ADR-0009)** — `link_seal.rs`: every `/i/`, `/c/`, `/g/` link carries a 12-byte secret after `#` (`LINK_SECRET_LEN`, shared by all kinds) and the WHOLE signed capability is sealed under it — `kind(1) ‖ version(1) ‖ nonce(12) ‖ ChaCha20-Poly1305(HKDF-SHA256(secret, salt = nonce, info = "lakko/invite-link/<k>/seal-key/v1\0"), aad = kind ‖ version)`; per-kind `seal_link` / `open_link` in `invite` / `call_invite` / `group_invite` (open returns the verified capability AND its plaintext wire, over which the joiner tag and the join attribution are computed), every refusal the one `ContactError::LinkDamaged`; `MAX_WIRE_LEN` / `MAX_SEALED_LEN` per kind (224/254, 4308/4338, 908/938). The ledger stores the sealed blob only; `/g/` keeps its preview sealed inside the capability because the plaintext capability also rides the attribution to the DS knock journal in the clear. On both FFI surfaces every compose returns `InviteLinkMint { sealed, linkSecret, issuerPubkey }` and each capability class gains an `openLink(sealed, linkSecret)` constructor + a `wire` getter (UniFFI refusal `FfiError::InviteLinkDamaged`, wasm an `Error` with `inviteLinkError: "damaged"`). Closes the 48-bit `/c/` bearer, `/i/`+`/c/` substitution and fragment-less `/g/` findings; the random-tag burn of `max_uses` remains. **Join attribution (ADR-0028 OQ2)** — `group_join_attribution.rs` (`compose_attribution`/`decode_attribution`: a bounded fail-closed journal envelope `version ‖ joiner_sig_key(32) ‖ cap_len ‖ capability ‖ joiner_sig(64)` (v2), **self-signed by the joiner's Ed25519 leaf key** over a domain-separated prefix — `compose` takes the joiner's `ed25519Seed`, `decode` verifies the signature against the embedded key (which is the same MLS leaf key the admin matches against the proposal), so a stranger cannot spoof another's joiner key; the joiner posts it alongside its external-join proposal; the admin decodes it — verifying the joiner self-signature + re-verifying the embedded capability's Ed25519 sig — to surface *which member's* link a pending request arrived on + whether that issuer is in the roster; advisory, the ADR-0027 role gate stays the boundary). **Call media-state signal (camera on/off)** — `media_state.rs` (`compose_media_state(camera_on)` / `decode_media_state` / `try_unwrap_media_state` + the `IncomingEnvelope::MediaState { camera_on }` dispatch variant): a fixed 28-byte `domain(26) ‖ version(1) ‖ flag(1)` envelope (domain `lakko/call-media-state/v1`) a call peer broadcasts over the **existing opaque SFU `signal-relay`** so peers deterministically flip a muted participant's roster `camOn` (a camera-off MUTE fires no WebRTC track event, so the receiver tile would otherwise freeze). Client-only, no SFU/proto/server change — the SFU never parses the payload (invariant #2); UI-only, no SFrame/MLS surface. FFI-exposed (`composeMediaState`/`decodeMediaState` + the dispatch `cameraOn` field) on uniffi + wasm; wired into all three clients' SFU call sessions (broadcast on toggle / connect / peer-join; receive → `setParticipantCamera`). **Opaque push routing id ([ADR-0042](docs/adr/0042-push-opaque-group-routing-id.md), invariant #5) + mint-epoch recency ordering ([ADR-0044](docs/adr/0044-push-routing-secret-recency.md))** — `push_routing.rs`: `derive_push_gid(secret)` = **keyed-BLAKE3** with the 32-byte per-group `push_routing_secret` as the key and `"lakko/push/group-routing-id/v1"` as the message (deterministic, one-way, domain-separated, **stable** — **no epoch input**, unchanged by the v2 bump (`derive_push_gid_ignores_the_mint_epoch`), which is what lets a subscriber survive ordinary commits without re-subscribing — and golden-pinned so every platform derives the same id), plus the in-group distribution envelope `compose_push_routing_secret` / `try_unwrap_push_routing_secret` / `decode_push_routing_secret`: since ADR-0044 a fixed **70-byte** **v2** `domain(29) ‖ version(1)=0x02 ‖ mint_epoch(8, BE u64) ‖ secret(32)` payload (domain `lakko/push-routing-secret/v1\0`, **byte-identical to v1** — those 29 bytes are the `incoming_envelope` dispatch prefix and are frozen forever, so the in-payload version byte is the knob that moves) riding an **E2EE MLS application message** on the group journal, with a bounded fail-closed codec (**version gate before** exact-length + domain-match → `WrongDomain` / `BadLength` / `UnknownVersion`) and cross-kind isolation tests against every sibling envelope domain. **Hard flag day — v1 (62-byte) is refused with `UnknownVersion`, no dual-accept**, since a v1 envelope carries no `mint_epoch` and so has no position in the order. `PushRoutingSecretEnvelope { mint_epoch, secret }` is the decoded pair, and **`push_routing_secret_is_newer(incoming_epoch, incoming_secret, stored_epoch, stored_secret)`** (+ the `PushRoutingSecretEnvelope::supersedes` wrapper) is **the canonical total order, defined here once**: the pair `(mint_epoch, secret_bytes)` — epoch first, then the 32 secret bytes lexicographically — **strictly greater wins, equal is NOT newer** (what keeps a benign retry from reading as an update), wrong-length secrets **fail-closed to `false`**, never panics. `mint_epoch` is **opaque to this crate** — never read as wall-clock, never bounded, never fed to `derive_push_gid`; the crate owns the **ORDER** and requires only that one group's mints be non-decreasing, leaving the **CLAMP** (and the `Group::epoch()` binding that makes it checkable) to the caller — as well as the second caller obligation the ADR-0044 review made explicit (**C-62 sub-invariant 5**): the comparison is only meaningful against the caller's **current** stored pair, so every client must run its whole read-decide-write under one serializing per-`(account, group)` lock. A total order compared against a stale snapshot decides nothing. 41 `#[test]` in-module, incl. `a_stale_rebroadcast_never_undoes_a_rotation`, `the_owner_can_lose_an_exact_epoch_tie`, `convergence_is_arrival_order_independent`, `is_newer_is_a_strict_total_order`, `is_newer_matches_lexicographic_wire_bytes`, `domain_separator_bytes_survived_the_v2_bump`. Dispatched by the `incoming_envelope` classifier as `IncomingEnvelope::PushRoutingSecret { mint_epoch, secret }`, so it is never rendered as a chat bubble. Because the secret is independent CSPRNG input, the derived `push_gid` is algebraically unrelated to the DS `group_id` — push routes under it instead, and `services/push-gw` stays byte-identical unchanged across both ADRs (it only ever treated the value as an opaque `BINARY(32)`). FFI-exposed (`derivePushGid` / `composePushRoutingSecret` (now takes the epoch) / `decodePushRoutingSecret` (returns the pair) / **`pushRoutingSecretIsNewer`**) on uniffi + wasm — the order is consumed over the FFI so no client re-implements the comparison, three clients each hand-rolling it being exactly the drift class that produced the ADR-0044 finding. **Committer liveness beacon ([ADR-0045](docs/adr/0045-remote-commit-epoch-fork.md) decision 2, variant 1 — the maintainer's chosen heartbeat)** — `committer_beacon.rs`: a **field-less 27-byte** envelope `domain(26) "lakko/committer-beacon/v1\0" ‖ version(1)=0x01` riding an **E2EE MLS application message** on the group journal, `compose_committer_beacon()` (infallible, argument-less, byte-identical on every call and for every sender) / `try_unwrap_committer_beacon` / `decode_committer_beacon`, bounded + fail-closed (domain match, then **exact** length, then version gate → `WrongDomain` / `BadLength` / `UnknownVersion`), dispatched as its own `IncomingEnvelope::CommitterBeacon` **unit** variant so it is never rendered as a chat bubble, cross-kind-isolated against every sibling domain, and fuzzed (`committer_beacon_decode`). **Carrying no fields is the decision, not an oversight** — three plausible fields were each excluded for a reason recorded in the module doc: an **identity** would add an unauthenticated claim beside the MLS-authenticated sender leaf (a pure spoofing surface); a **timestamp** would let a candidate hold the committer duty by asserting its own freshness, a liveness claim **no peer can check**, where ADR-0045 measures age against the DS row's server-assigned `created_at` that every member reads identically; and a **claimed rank** is one every correct receiver must ignore, so carrying it only invites an implementation to trust it. What remains is the signal itself, plus the version byte as the forward-compat knob (ADR-0044's v2 bump proved it load-bearing on the sibling envelope). Two pure, total, clock-free functions live beside it and are consumed over the FFI so no client re-derives them: **`acting_committer_verdict(own_rank, observed, freshness_window_ms)`** → `ActingCommitter::{Act, Defer{to_rank}, NotACandidate}` (a non-candidate never sweeps and is decided without reading a beacon; **rank 0 short-circuits** so the owner never waits its turn; beacons from non-candidates and beacons past the window are dropped; only a **strictly** lower rank defers, so a device's own echoed-back beacon is harmless; **hearing nothing means Act**, which is the single most likely thing to get wrong), fed by `BeaconObservation { rank: Option<u32>, age_ms }` whose **both fields are the observer's own re-derivation** — rank from the MLS sender leaf through `members()` and `Group::committerRank`, age from the DS row, so nothing in the payload contributes to either; and **`should_emit_beacon(own_rank, last_emitted_at_ms, now_ms)`** (only candidates emit — three emitters in a 50-member group, not fifty; the first beacon of a session is immediate; otherwise one per interval; a backward local clock jump suppresses rather than spams). Four constants: `COMMITTER_BEACON_INTERVAL_MS` **60 s** and `COMMITTER_BEACON_FRESHNESS_WINDOW_MS` = `2*interval + worst_case_poll` = **150 s**, both **CHOSEN, not measured**, and the module says so — the beacon's latency budget is **not interactive** (what waits on it is a membership change ADR-0045 deliberately gave no deadline), so the interval is sized for **journal litter** (a beacon is a journal row persisting to the 30-day prune — ADR-0024 already logs that shape as a grudgingly-accepted anti-pattern, and a beacon is *continuous* rather than event-driven) and for **privacy** (each row publishes that this candidate had the app foregrounded at that moment, so a shorter interval buys a finer-grained activity schedule for no gain anyone waits on); the window's three terms each earn their place (one interval because a healthy candidate's newest beacon is already that old, a second to tolerate exactly one lost beacon, plus `COMMITTER_BEACON_WORST_CASE_POLL_MS` **30 s** — Android's deliberate long poll, observed from the client code — because a beacon is only *observed* at the observer's next poll), pinned by `freshness_window_is_the_documented_construction`. **The module is emphatic that this is NOT a leader election**: each candidate folds over its own poll-timed view, so two can both reach `Act` — a heartbeat removes the *wait*, not the *race*, and ADR-0045's stand-down journal re-read plus the staged-sweep rollback remain **required**. **Why not `lakko-presence`**, recorded because the crate's existence makes reaching for it the obvious move: its blinding buys nothing inside an already-AEAD-sealed MLS message; its `derive_token` attribution is an O(members) scan yielding a **pseudonym** where the fold needs a signature key, and a token is **not authenticating** (every member holds the seed, so any member can mint any other's) where the MLS sender leaf is; its clock model binds the **sender's** wall-clock epoch as a *matching precondition*, contradicting ADR-0045's DS-`created_at` rule and silently reading a clock-skewed candidate as permanently absent; and it is unwired (wasm-only, no UniFFI, no caller, and its `presence_seed` exporter does not exist). What would make it right is recorded too: a beacon travelling **outside** MLS. FFI-exposed on uniffi + wasm (`composeCommitterBeacon` / `decodeCommitterBeacon` / `actingCommitterVerdict` / `shouldEmitBeacon` + all four constants + `committerBeaconEnvelopeLen`). 33 `#[test]` in-module. **The mechanism is LIVE on all three clients** (ADR-0045 S4b `02f0037` web, S5+S6 `1f230ab` iOS + Android) — this row previously read *the mechanism is INERT*, which was true until those sprints and is not now. `composeCommitterBeacon` has a production call site on each (`apps/web/src/routes/Chat.tsx`, `apps/ios/Lakko/Chat/ChatRepository.swift`, `apps/android/.../chat/ChatRepository.kt`), each driven by a **dedicated ticker rather than the poll** — a beacon's whole meaning is a cadence, and web's poll is event-driven — and each gated on **foreground**, the one rule the core cannot check and the one that rests on C-59: a beacon means *able to sweep*, the ADR-0041 background decrypt structurally cannot commit, so a background beacon would be a lie that makes every other candidate defer to a device that is never going to sweep. The fold's inputs are built the only way that is meaningful: an observation stores the sender's **32-byte signature key** resolved from the MLS-authenticated leaf at receipt, and is **ranked at verdict time** through `Group::committerRank` — never at receipt, because a rank is a *position* in the role table's canonical order and any promotion, demotion or removal renumbers everyone after it; storing a leaf would be worse still, since OpenMLS hands a vacated leaf to the next member added. Age comes from the DS row and an unparseable timestamp is **dropped rather than stored as fresh**, which would otherwise make the observer defer forever to something that can never age out. Pinned as **C-64**. **The freshness window is met on all three platforms:** it is `2 x interval + worst-case poll`, and since the web chat route gained an automatic receive loop (a 25 s DS long poll while the chat is open and the tab visible, or during a call — `apps/web/src/ds/long-poll-loop.ts`) the web observer meets it like both natives. This read "two platforms of three" while web polled only on mount, user actions and the Refresh button |
| `lakko-auth-client` | Pure-Rust **OPAQUE-PAKE client** for `services/auth` — the registration + login halves of the flow `services/auth/src/opaque.rs` implements server-side, plus the wire types and the cipher-suite pin. This is where the `opaque-ke` dependency (and, transitively, `voprf`) enters the client graph |
| `lakko-auth-token` | HMAC-SHA256 **session token** issue + verify — the `services/auth` ↔ `services/discovery` contract. A four-part dotted token binding `account_id` + expiry; `TokenSecret::verify` is the parser every protected discovery request reaches first. 11 tests + the `token_verify` fuzz target |
| `lakko-ds-proof` | **The request proof a claimed DS journal requires ([ADR-0053](docs/adr/0053-ds-journal-authorisation.md))**, shared by `services/ds` (verify) and both FFI surfaces (sign, S2) the way `lakko-auth-token` is shared by auth and discovery. `canonical_bytes` = `"lakko/ds/proof/v1" ‖ 0 ‖ method ‖ 0 ‖ path ‖ 0 ‖ query ‖ 0 ‖ BLAKE3(body) ‖ epoch(8 BE) ‖ unix_ms(8 BE) ‖ nonce(16)` (NUL separators so no field can run into the next; the body enters as a hash so the proof binds the published bytes; golden-pinned `881c504b…`), `sign` / `verify` (window first - `PROOF_WINDOW_MS` = 120 s - then Ed25519; errors `Malformed` / `UnknownVersion` / `OutsideWindow` / `BadSignature`), the header `x-lakko-ds-proof: v1.<epoch>.<ms>.<nonce_b64url>.<sig_b64url>` (`Proof::encode` / `parse`, `MAX_HEADER_LEN` 256), `access_signing_key(exporter_seed)` (the per-epoch group key from `Group::export_secret("lakko/ds/access/v1", group_id, 32)`), `MAILBOX_EPOCH` = 0, and `mailbox_pseudonym` / `mailbox_owner_matches` over the ADR-0005 `IdentityPseudonym` derivation (used, not copied). **The client half is `DsProofSigner`** - `for_group(epoch, exporter_seed)` / `for_mailbox(identity_seed)`, `sign_at(req, now_ms, nonce)` (clock and nonce are the caller's, so one type serves the host, the browser and the NSE), `verifying_key`, and the two request bodies a claim needs, `group_claim_body_json` / `mailbox_claim_body_json`, defined here so no client spells a field name. Depends on `lakko-crypto` + `lakko-contacts` only. 10 tests + fuzz `proof_parse`. **On all three FFI surfaces (S2)**: `Group.dsAccessSigner(identity)` / `DeviceGroup.dsAccessSigner` / `PublisherGroup.dsAccessSigner` (the exporter derivation at the current epoch - re-derive after every commit), `dsMailboxSigner(identity)`, `dsProofHeaderName()`, `dsMailboxEpoch()`, and `DsProofSigner.{epoch, verifyingKey, sign(method, path, query, body), groupClaimBodyJson, mailboxClaimBodyJson, mailboxPseudonym}` on UniFFI + wasm (`ds_proof.rs` in each; wasm reads `Date.now()` on wasm32 and the system clock on the host); the extension surface carries `Group.dsAccessSigner` + `dsMailboxSigner` + `sign` only - no claim body, since registering a key is the foreground app's write (C-59). `groupClaimBodyJson` is **superseded by ADR-0057** (the DS no longer accepts that body). **Journal ownership ([ADR-0057](docs/adr/0057-journal-ownership.md)) - `statement.rs`:** `journal_root_seed(account_identity_seed, journal_id)` = BLAKE3-derive-key(`lakko/ds/journal-root/v1`, seed ‖ id) and `journal_registrar_seed(root_seed, member_account_key)` = BLAKE3-derive-key(`lakko/ds/journal-registrar/v1`, root ‖ member) (golden-pinned), `StatementSigner`, and one signed binary statement per kind (`claim` / `reset` / `access` register-epoch with revocations / `registrars` / `root` transfer / `push-binding`): `lakko/ds/statement/v1\0 ‖ kind ‖ journal_id ‖ signer ‖ unix_ms ‖ nonce ‖ fields ‖ sig`, bounded (`MAX_STATEMENT_LEN`, lists <= 256 distinct keys), exact, fail-closed, sent as `{"statement_b64"}` and verified by window then signature; `push_binding_key(pepper, push_gid)` is the DS's keyed hash. Fuzz `statement_decode`. FFI: `dsJournalRootSeed` / `dsJournalRegistrarSeed` / `dsJournalRootSigner(accountIdentity, journalId)` / `dsStatementSigner(seed)` -> `DsStatementSigner.{publicKey, claim, reset, registerEpoch, replaceEpoch, registrars, registrarsSet, rootTransfer, pushBinding}` returning `{path, bodyJson}` (`ds_statement.rs` on UniFFI + wasm). **Since the second security pass:** `RegisterEpoch` carries a signed mode byte (`RegisterMode::{Append, Replace}`; `registerEpoch` = Append, `replaceEpoch` = Replace), a seventh kind `RegistrarsSet` (route `registrars-set`, up to `MAX_REGISTRARS` = 4096 entries, the signer may not list itself), and `composeRootHandoverEnvelope` / rank `root` (3) on the registrar-seed envelope carries the old owner's root seed to a new owner |
| `lakko-device-link` | **The camera-less add-device confirmation ([ADR-0049](docs/adr/0049-identity-recovery-word-vault.md) section 2, amended 2026-09-28).** The signed-in (old) device chooses six uniformly random digits (rejection sampling) and shows them; the person types them on the new device; SPAKE2 (RustCrypto `spake2` 0.4, Ed25519 group, old device = side B and moves first) over that code, with both identities and the transcript binding the auth request id and the login handle, yields a payload key and two HMAC confirmation keys (HKDF-SHA-256). The new device seals the ADR-0056 pairing payload (ChaCha20-Poly1305, transcript as AAD) and sends `confirm_a` over the transcript and the sealed bytes; `Responder::accept(self, ..)` checks it in constant time and opens the payload, else `WrongCode` - consumed, so one guess per request; `ConfirmationCheck::verify` checks the old device's `confirm_b`. Replaces the SHA-256 comparison code a relay could grind (the security pass finding). Deps: `lakko-crypto` + `spake2` (default features off, no new backend: curve25519-dalek 4 / hkdf 0.12 / sha2 0.10 already in the graph; not independently audited and does not zeroize its own scalar or password copy - recorded for G-39). 12 tests (incl. a 200k-sample digit-uniformity check and three proptests) + fuzz `device_link_accept`; on UniFFI (`DeviceLinkResponder` / `DeviceLinkInitiator` / `deviceLinkNormalizeCode` / `deviceLinkFormatCode`, `FfiError::DeviceLinkWrongCode` + `DeviceLink` appended) and wasm (same names, `"<code>: <reason>"` errors) |
| `lakko-discovery-client` | Pure-Rust **VOPRF client** for `services/discovery` — blinds a username (`blinder.rs`) so the server evaluates without learning it, plus `normalize.rs`, the NFKC + lowercase + trim canonicalisation both sides must agree on byte-for-byte. **Username character set ([ADR-0048](docs/adr/0048-profile-names.md) §4):** `validate_username` normalises then requires `[a-z0-9_]`, 3-32 characters (the rule itself is `lakko_contacts::username::check_canonical_username`, re-exported with `UsernameError`), and `OprfBlinder::blind` runs it before blinding (`DiscoveryClientError::InvalidUsername`), so neither register nor lookup can carry a non-conforming name — reversing the module's earlier stance of not rejecting confusables. `blind_channel` deliberately keeps its own policy (normalise, refuse empty). 25 tests |
| `lakko-mnemonic` | BIP39 **24-word recovery phrase**, fixed to one shape (24 words, English) rather than the nine languages and five lengths upstream `bip39` allows — the phrase seed is what [ADR-0017](docs/adr/0017-phrase-seeded-identity.md) derives `AccountIdentity` from, so the shape is load-bearing, not cosmetic. 15 tests + the `mnemonic_parse` fuzz target |
| `lakko-shamir` | Shamir's Secret Sharing over GF(256) for social recovery — splits the 32-byte recovery entropy into `<index>:<base64>` shares held by guardians. 32 tests + the `share_decode_combine` fuzz target (a malicious guardian submits crafted shares, so the duplicate-index guard is what keeps the Lagrange interpolation from dividing by zero) |
| `lakko-ui-rules` | **Presentation rules the three clients must agree on, written once** ([`docs/ux/navigation.md`](docs/ux/navigation.md) section 8). Five so far, and the last two were each minted the day a person found the clients disagreeing: `capture::call_holds_capture` (whether a live call is holding the microphone, so the composer's recorder must stand aside — the argument is "is a call live AT ALL", not "in this chat", because a call collapsed into the bar of `docs/ux/calls.md` section 4.5 is live in every chat) and `call_bar` (whether that bar is on screen and which ONE thing its status line says — a sum type rather than three booleans, because the spec's "never two at once" is an invariant a view expressed in booleans is one careless branch from breaking). Then: `conversation::order_conversations` (the order of the ONE merged conversation list — pinned first, archived last, newest between, keyed on a six-field `ConversationRow`, returning the input **INDICES** rather than rows so a client reorders what it already holds instead of marshalling every avatar and preview across an FFI boundary twice; total, so two conversations nothing has happened in yet never reshuffle on an unrelated refresh) and `lookup::looks_like_a_name` (whether a typed string is worth a network lookup — **the predicate IS `lakko_contacts::username::check_canonical_username`**, not a copy of it, so it cannot drift behind the rule the discovery blinder enforces; asking first is the difference between a quiet "type the whole name" and making a person pay a proof of work to be told no), and `info` (what the name at the top of a conversation opens and what is on it — `info_target` plus `person_info_sections` / `group_info_sections` / `channel_info_sections` over three capability records the CLIENT computes and this crate never re-derives, with section 2.1's `Never` list carried as `InfoSection::group_only` on each block so a group block added later inherits the rule instead of having to be remembered into it). **The third one is the worked example of why this crate exists**, and the cost is on the record: the info rule was written three times — Android as enum cases, iOS as a struct of booleans, web as an array of kebab strings — and the three disagreed on three separate points, each a sentence of one spec rather than a platform difference. `Remove contact` existed on web and Android while iOS carried a comment asserting no client had one; `Unsubscribe` existed on web and on neither native, whose comments asserted the operation did not exist while both already held the two functions it is made of; and `Delete this chat` reached an ended one-to-one only on Android, so on the other two a one-to-one that ended could never be closed at all. All three were found by a person opening two phones side by side, not by any test. Here for the same reason `should_request_profiles`, `acting_committer_verdict` and `push_routing_secret_is_newer` are in Rust: three hand-written copies of one rule drift, and the drift is only found when a person notices the clients disagreeing. **Deliberately its own crate rather than a module of `lakko-contacts`** — sorting a conversation list is not a fact about contacts, and the crates an external reviewer reads ([`docs/security/external-review-scope.md`](docs/security/external-review-scope.md)) should not grow presentation logic; nothing here is security-relevant and nothing here should become so, so a rule that needs a key, a secret or a peer's assertion is in the wrong crate. Its only dependency is `lakko-contacts`, for that one username rule. 27 tests; on **both** FFI surfaces (`orderConversations` / `bareName` / `looksLikeAName` / `infoTarget` / `personInfoSections` / `groupInfoSections` / `channelInfoSections` + the `ConversationKind` / `ConversationRow` pair and the three capability records — uniffi records and constructible wasm classes following `BeaconObservation`), so no client re-implements any of the rules. One asymmetry worth stating, because it is what the tests on each side can and cannot do: the sections cross to uniffi as a typed `InfoSection` enum and to wasm as their **kebab-case names**, which is the vocabulary web's own section type has always been written in — so web is handed the names rather than translating them. iOS unit tests link `LakkoCore` and so can assert the rule THROUGH the boundary, and so does **web**: its vitest loads the wasm through the node glue, which several suites already do — the earlier claim here that it has no wasm was stale and was repeated into three agent briefs before a web agent corrected it (2026-09-24). Android's host JVM suite genuinely has no native library, so there alone the rule is asserted once in Rust and the client tests cover the wiring. The distinction matters: a client test driving a spy can only assert WHAT THE RULE WAS ASKED, never that the client asked it about the right thing — and handing a rule the wrong input is the failure a spy is blind to by construction |
| `lakko-presence` | Blinded per-conversation presence tokens (`PresenceToken` / `derive_token` / `current_epoch` / `PresenceTracker`). **Unwired**: exposed on wasm only, no UniFFI, no client caller, and the `presence_seed` MLS exporter it is specified against does not exist — see the `lakko-contacts` committer-beacon row for a worked argument on why reaching for this crate is the obvious move and the wrong one |
| `lakko-push` | Push wake-up payload **framing** — the compact-JSON v1 wire format (`{v,k,gp,ep}`, kinds `g` / `m` / `w`) carrying only a 32-byte pseudonym + a journal cursor, so the provider learns nothing and the client fetches + MLS-decrypts on wake. **Not encrypted**: the crate's whole dependency set is `thiserror` / `serde` / `serde_json` / `base64`, and its own docs list server-side payload encryption under "What's NOT here" — the privacy argument is that there is nothing in the payload worth encrypting, which is a different claim. Web Push RFC 8291 framing lives in `services/push-gw`, not here |
| `lakko-transparency` | CONIKS-style key transparency log (Merkle tree, signed roots, inclusion proofs). Identity supersession proof (G-4, invariant #6, sprints 1–3) — `supersession.rs`: a compact Ed25519 blob signed by the OLD identity key over the NEW key (`SupersessionProof::sign` + strict `verify_supersession` — sig → trusted-old-key binding → ≤30-day window → freshness) so a rotation bridges trust without a blind TOFU re-bounce. Sprint 2: `verify_supersession_chain(pinned, evidence, now)` ties it to the KT log — operator-root sig + no-rollback + Merkle inclusion + `username_hash` match + newer version + proof authorises exactly the entry's key (leaf commits the `(username_hash, version, pubkey)` tuple, not the sig bytes, so point-malleability can't fork an entry). Sprint 3: FFI surface for the proof primitive — `sign_supersession_proof(old_seed, new_seed, issued, expires)` + `verify_supersession(proof, trusted_old_pubkey, now)` on UniFFI (Swift/Kotlin) + wasm (`signSupersessionProof`/`verifySupersession`); sign derives both pubkeys from each identity's `ed25519_seed()` so the embedded old key always matches the signer. The chain verifier is **no longer Rust-internal** — this row used to say it "stays Rust-internal … lands with the service", and it landed: `verify_supersession_chain` is exposed on UniFFI and wasm, reaches the generated Swift/Kotlin bindings, and is exercised end-to-end by `services/discovery/tests/recovery_kt_e2e.rs`. **Client-authorised leaf (G-30 #2, ADR-0019)** — `leaf_auth.rs`: `sign_leaf_genesis` / `verify_leaf_genesis` (a 64-byte Ed25519 genesis self-sig over `"lakko/transparency/leaf-genesis/v1" ‖ username_hash ‖ pubkey`, self-certifying the served key's first log entry; domain-separated from the supersession proof), FFI-exposed. **Merkle consistency + witness co-sign (G-30 #1, ADR-0020)** — `merkle.rs`: `consistency_proof(old_size, leaves)` + `verify_consistency(old_size, old_root, new_size, new_root, proof)` (RFC 6962 §2.1.2, reconstructs BOTH roots, bounded + panic-free); `witness.rs`: `sign_witness_cosig` / `verify_witness_cosig` (a 105-byte blob `version ‖ tree_root(32) ‖ log_size(8 BE) ‖ sig(64)`, domain `lakko/transparency/witness/v1`, FFI verify-only — signing stays in `services/witness`) + **`verify_witness_quorum` (K-of-N, ADR-0039, G-30 finding-1)** — the dedup-by-DISTINCT-key K-of-N generalisation (counts distinct pinned keys that produced a valid cosig over the served `(tree_root, log_size)`, never cosigs, so a replayed / duplicated cosig or a duplicate pinned key can never inflate the quorum; fail-closed on `threshold == 0` (`WitnessQuorumBadThreshold`) / below K (`WitnessQuorumNotMet`); N=1/K=1 ≡ `verify_witness_cosig`), FFI verify-only. The transparency service + 3-client wiring landed (see the `services/` table + ADR-0019/0020/0039). **Shared account-key derivation (ADR-0036)** — `account.rs`: `account_username_hash(account_id) = BLAKE3("lakko/transparency/account/v1" ‖ account_id)` (`ACCOUNT_KEY_DOMAIN`), the ONE definition — promoted from discovery — shared by discovery (log append), auth (genesis-attestation mint), and the clients (verify); a `golden_pins_the_account_username_hash` drift test makes a domain change fail CI instead of silently fail-closing the ADR-0036 genesis-attest gate |
| `lakko-call-jwt` | Call-admission JWT (Phase 3b4) — JWS Compact + EdDSA-Ed25519 + JWKS publication, shared between `services/auth` (mint) and FFI crates (parse / verify) |
| `lakko-attest` | Action attestation (G-13, [ADR-0015](docs/adr/0015-device-removal-authorization.md), sprint 1) — a compact fixed-layout Ed25519-signed blob `version‖account_id(16)‖action_hash(32)‖expires_at(8 BE)‖sig(64)` (121 B) proving an account completed a passkey ceremony for a specific action. `ActionAttestation::sign` (auth side) + `verify_bound` (strict: signature → account/action binding → freshness) for the peer-verified device-removal path. Built on `lakko_crypto::signing` Ed25519 — no JWS/JWKS-fetch dep in the audited core; the trusted verifying key is supplied by the caller. `#![forbid(unsafe_code)]`, bounded parser, 7 unit/property tests. **Genesis attestation (G-30 finding 2, [ADR-0036](docs/adr/0036-auth-attested-genesis.md))** — a second fixed-layout Ed25519 blob `version‖account_id(16)‖username_hash(32)‖pubkey(32)‖expires_at(8 BE)‖sig(64)` (153 B, domain `lakko/genesis-attest/v1`, distinct from device-attest so the two cannot cross-verify even under a shared signing key) proving the OPAQUE-authenticated account presented THIS pubkey as its genesis KT-log key (`crates/lakko-attest/src/genesis.rs`). Two verify entry points — `verify_genesis_binding` (client: all-four-field binding, **no** freshness — a permanent artifact) + `verify_genesis_fresh` (transparency gate: `username_hash`+`pubkey` binding + freshness, no `account_id`). PREVENTS KT-log first-entry squatting that ADR-0019's self-sig only detects. 8 genesis unit tests incl. action↔genesis domain-crossing; fuzz `genesis_attestation_open`. **Backup head (G-11, [ADR-0037](docs/adr/0037-backup-head-rollback-commitment.md))** — a THIRD fixed-layout Ed25519 blob `format_version‖account_id(16)‖backup_version(8 BE u64)‖envelope_hash(32)‖signed_at_unix_millis(8 BE i64)‖sig(64)` (129 B, domain `lakko/backup-head/v1`, distinct from device-attest + genesis so no two of the three cross-verify even under the shared signing key — a three-way domain-crossing test) committing an encrypted backup envelope as the account's **latest** at an auth-assigned monotone `backup_version`, closing the fresh-install backup-rollback residual (`crates/lakko-attest/src/backup_head.rs`). Auth-mint (`services/auth`) + client-verify only — no KT-log gate, binds `account_id` **directly**; timestamps in **milliseconds** (unlike the genesis template's seconds). Two verify entry points — `verify_backup_head` (FULL: sig + `account_id` + `envelope_hash` + freshness, returns the head for the caller's version-floor check) + `verify_backup_head_binding` (binding only). The head rides inside the ONE storage blob as a `0xB1` container-tag prefix (`crates/lakko-backup/src/head_frame.rs` `frame_backup_blob` / `parse_backup_blob` → `BackupBlob::{Framed, Legacy}`; `services/storage` untouched, invariant #2), so head + envelope swap atomically in a single `PUT`. 12 backup-head unit tests incl. the three-way action↔genesis↔backup domain-crossing; fuzz `backup_head_open` + `backup_blob_parse`. **Release manifest (GA update notice, [ADR-0038](docs/adr/0038-update-available-notice.md))** — a FOURTH fixed-layout Ed25519 blob `format_version‖latest_release(8 BE u64)‖min_supported_release(8 BE u64)‖signed_at_unix_millis(8 BE i64)‖sig(64)` (89 B, domain `lakko/release-manifest/v1`, distinct from device-attest + genesis + backup-head — a four-way domain-crossing test), the odd one out: **GLOBAL** (binds no `account_id` — the same manifest is served to every client) and signed by a dedicated **OFFLINE publisher** release key (NOT the shared auth attest key), only ever **client-verified** — the signed "update available" notice (`crates/lakko-attest/src/release_manifest.rs`). `verify_release_manifest` (sig + caller-controlled freshness; `i64::MAX` disables the staleness gate) + the pure `release_status` / `release_status_for` → `UpdateStatus { UpToDate, UpdateAvailable, UpdateRequired }` (`min_supported` takes precedence). 12 release-manifest unit tests incl. the four-way domain-crossing; fuzz `release_manifest_open` |
| `lakko-pow` | Client-side Equix proof-of-work **solver** — the half the clients run against the challenges `lakko-pow-server` issues. Separate from the server crate because the client only solves and the server only verifies. **Uses the `equix` JIT on every native target except iOS** (an app may not map executable memory there) and wasm32 (none exists), and **checks every solution of a run** against the effort gate rather than only the first: measured 2026-09-27 at 8 bits, 16-21 s -> 0.6 s on a Mac, about 2 min -> about 1 s on the Android emulator, 6.8 s in the iOS simulator (interpreter). Solver memory is reused across attempts. 7 tests |
| `lakko-pow-server` | Server-side Equix proof-of-work — challenge issue + verify + consume + prune, used by `services/auth` only since ADR-0054 S3 (discovery, the DS and push-gw spend anonymous tokens instead; it was shared across `services/{auth,discovery,push-gw}` for the G-29 dedup; previously duplicated near-verbatim in each service's `src/pow.rs`, a drift risk where a security fix to one copy could miss the others). `issue_challenge(pool, effort)` + `verify_proof(pool, proof)` (atomic `SELECT FOR UPDATE → DELETE` single-use consumption — a stolen proof can't be replayed) + `spawn_pruner(pool)` + the pure crypto core (`verify_crypto`: Equix `verify_bytes` + SHA-256 leading-zero-bits effort gate) + `configured_effort_bits(var_name)` (each service names its own env knob: `LAKKO_POW_EFFORT_BITS` / `LAKKO_DISCOVERY_POW_EFFORT_BITS` / `LAKKO_PUSH_GW_POW_EFFORT_BITS`). `From<PowError> for (http::StatusCode, String)` so each service's thin axum `issue_challenge` handler `?`-converts. Distinct from `lakko-pow` (the wasm-portable CLIENT *solver*); the server side keeps the `equix` `compiler` JIT. Each service's `src/pow.rs` is now a thin re-export + handler shim. `#![forbid(unsafe_code)]`, 5 unit tests |
| `lakko-anon-token` | **Anonymous rate-limit tokens, the pure primitive ([ADR-0054](docs/adr/0054-anti-abuse-without-client-work.md) decision 2, S2).** A privately verifiable Privacy-Pass-shaped token on `voprf` 0.5 in **VOPRF mode** (Ristretto255-SHA512, the version and suite `lakko-discovery-client` / `services/discovery` already pin), so the batched DLEQ proof lets a client check the issuer evaluated under the key published for the `(context, epoch)` (anti-tagging). `TokenContext` is a closed enum with frozen wire bytes, one per rationed action group rather than per service (`discovery-lookup` 1, `discovery-register` 2, `invite-consume` 3, `ds-knock` 4, `push-register` 5) so the issuer can ration each on its own quota; `epoch = floor(unix_secs / window_secs)`, default window one day (`DEFAULT_WINDOW_SECS`), and a verifier accepts the current and the previous epoch only. **No key table:** `IssuerKey::derive(master, context, epoch)` = BLAKE3 derive-key over `master ‖ version ‖ context ‖ epoch` then RFC 9497 `DeriveKeyPair` with `info = "lakko/anon-token/v1" ‖ version ‖ context ‖ epoch` (golden-pinned public key); `issue` evaluates a batch of at most `MAX_BATCH` (32) blinded elements with one DLEQ proof, refusing an empty / oversized batch or any invalid or identity point before evaluating. Client: `TokenRequest::new` (random 32-byte nonce per token, input `"lakko/anon-token/v1 input" ‖ version ‖ context ‖ epoch ‖ nonce`, blind) and `finalize` (proof checked against the caller-supplied public key, all or nothing). Token wire, fixed 106 bytes: `version(1) ‖ context(1) ‖ epoch(8 BE) ‖ nonce(32) ‖ output(64)`, bounded fail-closed `Token::decode`. `verify_token(master, bytes, expected_context, now, window)` checks layout, context, epoch window and the VOPRF output (constant-time compare) and returns a `SpendId` (BLAKE3 over version, context, epoch, nonce) for the caller's spent-set. **S3 added `wire.rs`**: `TOKEN_HEADER = "x-lakko-anon-token"`, `Token::to_header_value` / `decode_header_value` (base64url without padding, 142 characters, length-checked before decoding, canonical), `encode_b64url` / `decode_b64url_fixed`, `TokenRequest::blinded_elements_b64url` / `finalize_b64url`, and `AnonTokenError::code()` (frozen kebab codes both FFI surfaces carry). `#![forbid(unsafe_code)]`, wasm32-portable, no new third-party crate (`base64` 0.22 was already in the tree). 31 tests (16 unit + 15 integration incl. 2 proptests) + fuzz `token_verify`, which also drives the header decoder |
| `lakko-anon-token-server` | **Server bookkeeping for anonymous tokens ([ADR-0054](docs/adr/0054-anti-abuse-without-client-work.md) decision 2)** — the `lakko-pow` / `lakko-pow-server` split again. `AnonTokenServerConfig::from_env` (`LAKKO_ANON_TOKEN_MASTER_SECRET` 64 hex, unset = dormant, malformed = error; `LAKKO_ANON_TOKEN_WINDOW_SECS` default 86400, zero or malformed = error, since a window that differs between services fails every token); the issuer's per-account quota `reserve_issuance` (one transaction, row lock, all-or-nothing, a limit of 0 issues nothing) over `anon_token_issuance`; a verifier's spent-set `spend` (one INSERT on the `(context, spend_id)` primary key, a duplicate is `AlreadySpent`) over `anon_token_spent`; hourly pruners dropping rows older than the previous epoch. Both counters **fail closed** on a store error (unlike the ADR-0033 / ADR-0037 limits). Ships the two schemas as `ISSUANCE_MIGRATION_SQL` / `SPENT_SET_MIGRATION_SQL`; a service copies the file byte for byte into its own `migrations/` and pins the copy with a test (auth's `0019_anon_token_issuance.sql` does, and discovery `0019`, the DS `0003` and push-gw `0004` for the spent-set). **S3 added the spend gate** (`gate.rs`): `TokenVerifier::new(pool, Option<config>)` → `spend_header(headers, context)` (disabled → `503 anon-tokens-disabled` first, then `401 anon-token-required` / `-malformed` / `-wrong-context` / `-expired` / `-invalid` / `-spent`, `500 internal` on a store error, body `{"error": code}`), the `require_token` axum middleware over `TokenVerifier::gate(context)`, `spawn_pruner`, `log_startup`; and a `testing` feature (`testing::mint_header`) for other crates' tests. 14 tests, 7 of them gated on `LAKKO_ANON_TOKEN_TEST_DATABASE_URL` |
| `lakko-sfu-proto` | SFU signaling wire frames (SFU multi-party arc, subsprint 1, complete) — `ServerFrame` / `ClientFrame` / `RelayTarget` / `PeerSnapshot` / `ErrorCode`, serde tagged-enum (`kind`, kebab-case), `MAX_INBOUND_FRAME_BYTES` cap + encode/decode + base64url payload helpers. Extracted from `services/sfu/src/protocol.rs` so the **server** (`services/sfu`, whose `protocol.rs` now re-exports this crate — its `client_frame_decode` fuzz target transitively fuzzes this parser) and the **clients** (via `lakko-ffi-uniffi::sfu_signaling` — full typed `SfuServerFrame` enum + `parse_sfu_server_frame` + `compose_sfu_*` + payload helpers — and `lakko-ffi-wasm::sfu_signaling` — `validateSfuServerFrame` + `composeSfu*` + payload helpers; the web reads fields via a TS type over native `JSON.parse` since wasm-bindgen doesn't return enum-with-data ergonomically) parse / emit one wire definition with no drift. `payload_b64` is opaque to the SFU (opacity invariant). `kind_str()` on both `ServerFrame` + `ClientFrame` returns the stable kebab `kind` tag (single source of truth for low-cardinality metric labels / structured logs; drift-guard tests assert it matches the serde tag — used by the SFU's `direction`+`kind`-labelled `lakko_sfu_signaling_frames_total`). 21 crate unit tests + 5 UniFFI + 4 wasm |
| `lakko-tuf` | TUF v1.1 client-side metadata verifier (GA-5 scaffold + rotation + target-bytes verifier + the **delegated-targets walk** + URL conventions module; this header used to say "defense-in-depth delegation rejection", which the delegation walk replaced — `verify_chain` no longer refuses a `delegations` block, and the crate's own module doc still carries the stale refusal text). **No production consumer**: nothing outside its own fuzz crate depends on it, consistent with the network-fetch and install-glue follow-ups named at the end of this row. Root / Snapshot / Targets / Timestamp Ed25519-signed envelopes, chain-of-trust walk via `verify_chain`, version-rollback detection, expiry check, threshold-of-keys per role. `verify_root_chain` walks the root version chain — each new root must be signed by the previous root's `root` role, anchoring at a binary-pinned trust-anchor key id set. `verify_target_bytes` closes the last metadata-side gate — given a `TargetMeta` from `verify_chain` + the actual fetched binary, confirms length + SHA-256 digest match; unknown hash algos skipped (forward-compat); refuses if no known algo got verified. **Delegated-targets walk (GA-5) landed** — `Targets.delegations` is now the typed `Delegations { keys, roles: Vec<DelegatedRole> }` (was opaque `serde_json::Value`); `verify_chain` no longer refuses a delegations block (it returns the top-level direct targets map), and `resolve_target(name, top_targets, provider, now)` walks the TUF §5.6 delegation tree to resolve a single target: pre-order DFS, each delegated role's metadata verified against the keys + threshold its DELEGATING role published (reusing the audited `verify_signatures`), `paths` fnmatch-glob (ReDoS-safe iterative matcher, `*`/`?`) XOR `path_hash_prefixes` (SHA-256 hex prefix) matching, `terminating` short-circuits a branch, bounded against a hostile graph (`MAX_DELEGATION_DEPTH=32` + visited-role cycle guard + `MAX_DELEGATED_ROLES_VISITED=256` budget). The crate stays OFFLINE — the caller supplies a `DelegatedTargetsProvider` (any `FnMut(&str)->Option<Vec<u8>>`) to fetch delegated metadata by role name; the crate never fetches. `urls` module pins the TUF v1.1 §5 non-consistent-snapshot URL convention (`{base}/{version}.root.json` / `{base}/timestamp.json` / `{base}/snapshot.json` / `{base}/targets.json` / `{base}/targets/{name}`) so platform-specific install glue does not re-derive it from the spec; `target_artifact_url` carries the path-traversal defence (refuses empty name, NUL, `/`, `\\`, `..`). `mirrors` module (GA-5-mirrors) adds pure-Rust mirror selection + failover: `Mirror { url_base, confined_target_dirs }` (a mirror may be CONFINED to glob target-path patterns — a low-trust mirror cannot serve a target outside its remit), `MirrorList::for_metadata()` (all mirrors) / `for_target(name)` (only confinement-allowed, refuses `NoMirrorForTarget`), and `try_in_order(candidates, fetch)` failover (first-success wins, aggregates `AllFailed` per-mirror errors); the crate stays offline (the caller's `fetch` owns the network) + selection is a TRANSPORT choice independent of trust (bytes verified only via `verify_chain`/`verify_target_bytes`). Network fetch + platform-side install glue (atomic-swap binary replacement) are out-of-scope follow-ups |
| `lakko-ffi-uniffi` | UniFFI surface for Swift / Kotlin bindings. **The bulk of this surface is the MLS one and is documented in the `lakko-mls` row, not here** — group + proposal + staged-sweep + ownership-transfer + typed-refusal + beacon; note in particular the `flat_error` constraint that ties this crate's hands: a variant lowers as its **ordinal**, so a new `FfiError` variant must be **appended**, never inserted, or older bindings decode unrelated errors as the wrong variant. SFrame call-key surface (G3-sframe step 1, re-keyed by [ADR-0035](docs/adr/0035-sframe-per-stream-kid-key.md)): `CallKey.from_seed(seed) → Arc<CallKey>`, `CallKey.participant_key(sender_leaf, stream_index) → Arc<ParticipantKey>` (the row used to show a one-argument `participant_key(sender_leaf)`; the per-`(leaf, stream)` KID added the second), `ParticipantKey.seal(aad, plaintext) → SealedFrame { counter, ciphertext }`, `ParticipantKey.open(aad, ct, counter) → Vec<u8>`, plus `seal_framed` / `open_framed` and the `CallKeyRing` overlap pair whose open takes the whole `kid`. `ParticipantKey` wraps the underlying `lakko_calls::sframe::ParticipantKey` in a `Mutex` so WebRTC's codec-thread call into seal is safe without external serialisation. New `FfiError::Call(String)` variant collapses CallKey seed-length / SFrame counter-exhaustion / AEAD-fail. 15 unit tests in `sframe.rs` pin the round-trip + distinct-participant + wrong-counter + receiver-side semantics. The MLS-exporter seed plumbing (`Group.export_secret("lakko/call/v1", call_id, 32)`) is the caller's job — same posture G4-mls-seed established for channels. Per-platform wiring into libwebrtc's `FrameEncryptorInterface` / `FrameDecryptorInterface` landed via the G3-sframe-native arc (N-1..N-6, closes G-28) — see the `lakko-sframe-native` row below + the `apps/shared/sframe-bridge/` C++ bridge + the iOS Obj-C++ shim at `apps/ios/Lakko/Calls/SFrameAttach.{h,mm}` + the Android `lakko-sframe` Gradle module. **Anti-censorship transport FFI (ADR-0026, no wasm — browser sandbox)** in `transport.rs`: `tunneled_http_request(config, anchor, now, endpoint, method, path, host, headers, body) → FfiTunneledResponse` (verify the signed config against a pinned trust anchor → `BootstrapDialer::from_signed_config` → one HTTP/1.1 request over the winning obfs tunnel, fail-closed) + `fetch_bootstrap_config(doh_domain, path, trust_anchor, now) → Vec<u8>` (DoH-resolve + IP-pinned HTTPS GET + parse + `BootstrapConfig::verify` → the verified raw config bytes; an unverified config never leaves the call). Both `FfiError::Transport`, no silent fallback (#4). The native clients route through these when armed (iOS `AntiCensorshipTransport.perform`, Android `AntiCensorshipEngine`). **Release-manifest verify surface (ADR-0038)** in `release.rs`: `verifyReleaseManifest(manifest, verifying_key, now_millis, max_staleness_millis) → VerifiedReleaseManifest { latestRelease, minSupportedRelease, signedAtMillis }` + the pure `releaseUpdateStatus(current, latest, min_supported) → UpdateStatus` — **verify-only** (signing is the offline publisher tool's job); every verify failure folds into `FfiError::Release` (no stale-vs-invalid split — the client fails toward UpToDate). **Anonymous tokens ([ADR-0054](docs/adr/0054-anti-abuse-without-client-work.md) S3)** in `anon_token.rs`: `AnonTokenRequest(context, epoch, count)` (blind; `blindedElementsB64url()`, `issueBodyJson()` for `POST /auth/tokens`) → `finalize(evaluationsB64url, proofB64url, publicKeyB64url)` → `[AnonToken { context, epoch, headerValue }]`, all or nothing on the DLEQ check, the key taken from `GET /auth/tokens/key/{context}/{epoch}` and never from the issuance response; plus `anonTokenHeaderName()` / `anonTokenContexts()` / `anonTokenDefaultWindowSecs()` / `anonTokenMaxBatch()` / `anonTokenEpochAt()` / `anonTokenIsLive()`. Two appended variants: `FfiError::AnonToken` (message `"<code>: <reason>"`, the frozen `AnonTokenError::code()`) and `AnonTokenAlreadyFinalized` |
| `lakko-ffi-wasm` | wasm-bindgen surface for Web / Tauri. **As on UniFFI, the MLS surface is the bulk and lives in the `lakko-mls` row**; where UniFFI lowers a typed refusal as a flat variant, wasm throws a real `Error` carrying `refusalReason` / `refusalDisposition` as own properties with `.message` unchanged. SFrame mirror of the UniFFI surface (G3-sframe step 1, re-keyed by [ADR-0035](docs/adr/0035-sframe-per-stream-kid-key.md)): JS-side `new CallKey(seed)`, `callKey.participantKey(senderLeaf, streamIndex) → ParticipantKey` (this row used to show a one-argument `participantKey(leaf)`), `pk.seal(aad, plaintext) → SealedFrame`, `pk.open(aad, ct, counter) → Uint8Array`, `sealFramed` / `openFramed`, the `CallKeyRing` overlap pair, plus `senderLeaf` / `counter` getters and a `SealedFrame { counter, ciphertext }` Object. Wired through to the browser's `RTCRtpScriptTransform` API by `G3-web-sframe step 2` (separate sub-sprint). **Release-manifest verify surface (ADR-0038)** in `release.rs`: `verifyReleaseManifest` → `VerifiedReleaseManifest` (`latestRelease` / `minSupportedRelease` / `signedAtMillis` getters) + `releaseUpdateStatus` + the `UpdateStatus` number-enum; **verify-only**, every failure throws a single `release:`-coded `JsError` (fail toward UpToDate). **Anonymous tokens ([ADR-0054](docs/adr/0054-anti-abuse-without-client-work.md) S3)** in `anon_token.rs`, the UniFFI mirror: `new AnonTokenRequest(context, epoch: bigint, count)` → `issueBodyJson()` / `finalize(evaluations, proof, publicKey)` → `AnonToken` (`context` / `epoch` / `headerValue` getters) and the same six helpers; a refusal throws an `Error` whose message is `"<code>: <reason>"` (`"already-finalized: ..."` on a second finalize) |
| `lakko-sframe-native` | Flat C ABI around `lakko_calls::sframe` (G3-sframe-native N-1, closes G-28 alongside N-2..N-6). Bottom of the iOS Obj-C++ + Android JNI native-shim stack — every per-frame seal / open / RFC 9605 wrap_frame / unwrap_frame call from the platform shims goes through these 19 `extern "C"` functions (13 base + the 6 `call_key_ring_*` the ADR-0029 rekey added). `crate-type = ["staticlib", "cdylib", "rlib"]`: staticlib for iOS link (lipo'd from `aarch64-apple-ios` + `aarch64-apple-ios-sim` + `x86_64-apple-ios`); the Android `libsframe_jni.so` statically links the cargo-ndk staticlib output for each ABI. Opaque handle types `CallKey` / `ParticipantKey` + status enum `lakko_sframe_status_t` (`Ok / NullPointer / BadSeedLength / KeyDerivation / CounterExhausted / AeadFailed / WireParse / KidOverflow / CtrOverflow`). Memory ownership: every `*mut u8` / `*mut CallKey` / `*mut ParticipantKey` returned transfers ownership; matched-pair `_free` symbols reclaim. cbindgen-generated header mirrored to stable `target/lakko-include/lakko_sframe_native.h` (a build artifact — it only exists after `just cross-build-sframe-native-*`; Xcode + Gradle NDK build systems do NOT glob HEADER_SEARCH_PATHS, so the stable mirror is what they `-I`). 10 unit tests + 6 integration tests (`tests/sfu_opacity_e2e.rs` — SFU opacity, AAD-tampering rejection, wrong-key isolation, multi-party fan-in, cross-call isolation, counter monotonicity) + 3 cross-stack golden tests (`tests/golden_cross_stack.rs` — the `extern "C"` `seal_framed` / `open_framed` pinned to the canonical `GOLDEN_WIRE_HEX` the core / web-wasm / iOS-Android-UniFFI golden tests all share, so the native media-shim C ABI is proven byte-identical to every other stack; the automatable headless slice of G-26). Cross-compile via `just cross-build-sframe-native-ios` + `just cross-build-sframe-native-android`. C++ bridge layer at `apps/shared/sframe-bridge/` (N-3) consumes this ABI; iOS Obj-C++ shim at `apps/ios/Lakko/Calls/SFrameAttach.{h,mm}` (N-4) + Android Gradle module `apps/android/lakko-sframe/` (N-5) wrap the bridge for their respective platform's `webrtc::FrameEncryptorInterface` / `FrameDecryptorInterface` surface. **Mid-call rekey ([ADR-0029](docs/adr/0029-mid-call-key-rotation.md), G-18)** — a `CallKeyRing` opaque handle + `lakko_sframe_call_key_ring_{new,free,rekey,prune,participant_key,open_framed_with_overlap}` (`Mutex`-guarded so the codec-thread open races the signaling-thread rekey safely), renamed to `lakko_sframe_call_key_ring_t` via cbindgen. The C++ bridge's `LakkoFrameDecryptor` now holds the ring (overlap open + `Rekey`/`Prune`) and `LakkoFrameEncryptor` swaps its seal key on `Rekey`; `test_bridge.cc` gains `test_mid_call_rekey_overlap` (10th assertion). **Per-(leaf, stream) key derivation via the header KID ([ADR-0035](docs/adr/0035-sframe-per-stream-kid-key.md), supersedes [ADR-0034](docs/adr/0034-sframe-per-media-stream-key.md)'s per-media-TYPE discriminator)** — the participant-key entry point takes a `stream_index: u8` and composes `SframeHeader::kid_for(sender_leaf, stream_index)` (`KID = (sender_leaf << 8) | stream_index`) internally; the open-framed entry reads the KID off each frame's header (`open_framed_with_overlap(header.kid, …)`) with **no** media argument. `LakkoFrameEncryptor::Create` takes the `stream_index` (stamped into the seal-side KID); `LakkoFrameDecryptor` **drops** the `cricket::MediaType` argument entirely and reads the KID from the header. The `BadMediaType` status variant + the `LAKKO_SFRAME_MEDIA_TYPE_*` constants are removed. Closes the two-video-streams (camera + screen-share) reuse 0034 missed. Cross-stack golden regenerated to the ADR-0035 canonical vector (`910300…`) |
| `lakko-ffi-extension` | Slim decrypt-only UniFFI surface for the iOS NotificationServiceExtension (NSE) — [ADR-0041](docs/adr/0041-ios-silent-push-nse-decrypt.md) Phase 2. `#![forbid(unsafe_code)]`; depends ONLY on `lakko-mls` + `lakko-attachments` + `lakko-contacts` + `lakko-calls` (+ transitive `lakko-crypto` / `lakko-proto` / openmls) — `cargo tree` confirms tokio, `lakko-transport`, `lakko-channels`, `lakko-sfu-proto`, `lakko-call-jwt`, and tungstenite are **all absent** (193 crates vs the full `lakko-ffi-uniffi` surface's 356). `lakko-contacts` was added for the background service-envelope filter and is deliberately cheap: measured on `aarch64-apple-ios` with `--edges normal` it adds **exactly 5** crates (172 → 177 — itself, `tracing` ×3, `pin-project-lite`), because its only dependencies are `thiserror` and `lakko-crypto`, both already present. `lakko-calls` was added for the call-signalling classifier and measured the same way: it adds **exactly 1** crate (177 → 178, itself), because its whole dependency set — `lakko-mls`, `lakko-crypto`, `lakko-proto`, `prost`, `lakko-contacts`, `thiserror`, `zeroize` — was already in the tree; SFrame, the jitter buffer and the codec tables are unreferenced and dead-stripped, since the crate touches only `lakko_calls::signaling::parse_signaling_payload`. Exposes exactly the read-only-throwaway NSE decrypt subset and nothing more: `Identity.from_seed` (ADR-0017) + `restore_storage`, `load_group`, `Group.process_incoming_verifying_roles` (with the sender leaf — the audited ADR-0022 role-verifying path), `parse_body_envelope` → `ParsedBody` (ADR-0023/0024), and `decode_push_routing_secret` → `FfiPushRoutingSecret` (ADR-0042/0044 — **decode only**, so the NSE can recognise a service envelope and suppress it instead of rendering it as lossy-UTF-8 garbage; `compose` / `derive_push_gid` / `push_routing_secret_is_newer` are deliberately NOT brought over, since the NSE only asks the classification question), `decode_group_name_envelope` + `group_name_envelope_min_len` / `_max_len` for the [ADR-0051](docs/adr/0051-group-and-channel-names.md) group-name envelope (the third envelope kind the NSE must recognise and suppress; decode only, no compose, the golden pins the 20-byte prefix, 30 / 285 lengths and first byte `0x6c`), `decode_pin_envelope` + `pin_envelope_len` for the [ADR-0052](docs/adr/0052-reply-forward-pin.md) shared-pin envelope (a fifth service envelope the NSE suppresses; decode only, the golden pins 31 bytes and first byte `0x6c`), `decode_profile_request` + `profile_request_envelope_len` for the [ADR-0048](docs/adr/0048-profile-names.md) profile request (the fourth service envelope the NSE suppresses; a newcomer sends one right after joining, so the newest row on a push wake is one whenever someone has just joined; decode only, its golden pins 26 bytes and first byte `0x6c`), and — since `94da492` — `decode_committer_beacon` + `committer_beacon_envelope_len` for the [ADR-0045](docs/adr/0045-remote-commit-epoch-fork.md) committer beacon, the **same classification question reached by a second envelope kind** and **more** reachable than the first rather than less: a routing secret is broadcast on a membership change, while a beacon is emitted by every owner/admin every 60 s, so in an active group the newest journal row — the one the NSE previews — is frequently a beacon, and unfiltered it reaches the notification body as the literal text `lakko/committer-beacon/v1` plus two control bytes (`parse_body_envelope`'s deliberate lossy-UTF-8 legacy fallback, load-bearing for pre-1.19 wire compat, so the fix belongs here and not in `lakko-attachments`, which does not depend on `lakko-contacts`). The envelope **length** is exported rather than hand-copied as `27` into Swift precisely because a stale literal fails in the **dangerous** direction — a too-narrow peek silently stops suppressing beacons and restores the defect — and `golden_pins_the_swift_prefilter_shape` pins the exported value **against literals** (27 and `0x6c`) so a wire revision fails Rust CI rather than a notification; `compose_committer_beacon` / `acting_committer_verdict` / `should_emit_beacon` and the timing constants are DELIBERATELY absent, since this process structurally cannot sweep (C-59) so a beacon from it would be a lie, and it holds no observation history to fold. **`parse_call_signal` → `CallSignal { Offer { call_id, video, group } / End { call_id } / Other { call_id } }`** is the call-wake classifier `docs/ux/calls.md` §7 names as the single transport change the whole of §2 depends on: until it existed the NSE could not tell a call offer from any other non-chat payload, so an iPhone never rang. It is CLASSIFY only over `lakko_calls::signaling::parse_signaling_payload` — no compose, so the extension can never answer, decline or end a call (each is an MLS application message, i.e. a ratchet advance C-59 forbids) — and it carries no SDP, no ICE candidate and no timestamps, since a ring needs none of them. `group` separates the 1×1 offer from the group-call announce because their accept paths differ and nothing else on this surface recovers the distinction; folding the announce into `Other` would have meant a group call never rings, silently.
