Two goldens pin it: `golden_pins_the_wire_shape_swift_branches_on` (whole-wire hex for a video offer, a voice offer and an end, plus the one `media_flags` byte that is the whole of "say what kind of call it is") and `golden_pins_every_body_tag_the_ios_peek_tabulates` (all seven `body` oneof tags — `0x52` offer / `0x5a` answer / `0x62` candidate / `0x6a` end / `0x72` group-start / `0x7a` group-join / `0x82 0x01` group-leave — because the iOS `nseLooksLikeCallSignaling` byte peek hand-copies that table, the ADR-0044 drift class again, failing dangerously: a renumbered tag stops the peek matching and the frame falls through to the lossy chat fallback). **`Group.account_key_at(leaf)`** resolves a leaf to the ADR-0050 account key that names the *person* (not a leaf key, which names one of their phones), so a wake can be titled with a contact's name instead of "Lakko"; one leaf in, one key out, never the roster — and at receipt the same value already arrives on `ProcessedMessage.Application.sender_account`, resolved at the one moment the roster IS the epoch the message was sealed at (C-68), which is what a caller should prefer. **No compose / send / channel / transport / persist surface** — the FFI carries no `snapshot_storage` method at all, the structural bottom layer of the read-only-throwaway no-write guarantee (C-59). The Swift API shape mirrors `lakko-ffi-uniffi` so the Phase-3 NSE handler calls the same names against the slim framework. Cross-compiled to a separate `LakkoCoreExtension.xcframework` (its own FFI module, so the app + NSE carry both frameworks with no symbol clash) via `just build-ios-extension`; the dead-stripped linked decrypt footprint is ~1.6 MB vs the 333 MB `LakkoCore.xcframework`, so it fits the ~30 MB NSE budget with an order of magnitude of headroom. 9 unit tests incl. the full `fromSeed → restoreStorage → loadGroup → processIncomingVerifyingRoles → parseBodyEnvelope` round-trip recovering plaintext, plus a golden that pins the 70-byte envelope shape the Swift-side cheap pre-filter hardcodes — so a wire-format drift fails in Rust CI instead of silently un-suppressing the filter — and 8 more in `committer_beacon.rs`, incl. `an_unfiltered_beacon_would_otherwise_render_as_chat_text` (which asserts the defect the arm exists to close, so the arm cannot be deleted silently) and `advertised_length_matches_the_composed_wire`. 41 tests in total as of the call-classifier landing (3 lib + 11 `call_signaling` + 8 `committer_beacon` + 6 each in `group_name` / `profile` / `push_routing` + 1 `attachments`), the call module's own including `an_unfiltered_offer_would_otherwise_render_as_chat_text` and a cross-kind isolation test proving the call arm and the two service-envelope arms never accept each other's bytes |

### `services/` — Backend (Rust)

| Service | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `auth` | OPAQUE-PAKE + WebAuthn passkey, no PII stored. **Genesis-attestation mint (ADR-0036, G-30 finding 2)** — `POST /auth/genesis/attest` (`genesis_attest.rs`): a bearer-authed (`AuthenticatedAccount` extractor), stateless endpoint that signs a 153-byte `GenesisAttestation` binding the OPAQUE-authenticated `account_id` + the derived `username_hash` (`lakko_transparency::account_username_hash`, never from the request body) to the client-supplied genesis pubkey, reusing the device-attest signing key + JWKS (domain-separated so the two attestation kinds cannot cross-verify), TTL 3600 s. Holding a valid session token IS the proof of OPAQUE-authentication that only the account holder can produce — the anti-squatting basis. **Backup-head mint (ADR-0037, G-11)** — `POST /auth/backup/head` (`backup_head.rs`): a bearer-authed (`AuthenticatedAccount`), **STATEFUL** endpoint that read-bumps a per-account monotone `backup_version` counter (`backup_head_versions` table, migration `0015`, one transaction via the MariaDB `LAST_INSERT_ID` idiom — the first auth state outside authentication proper, storing ONLY `(account_id, version)`: no key material, no envelope bytes, no hash) then signs a 129-byte `BackupHead` over `(account_id, backup_version, envelope_hash, signed_at)` with the shared attest signing key (domain-separated `lakko/backup-head/v1`), returning `{ backup_head_b64, backup_version }`. The version is auth-assigned, never client-supplied → a rollback regression is structurally impossible; a restoring client verifies the head to detect a storage-tier rollback. The sole deviation from the ADR-0036 stateless genesis mint (and the reason a migration is required). **A second table has since joined it** on the same endpoint: `backup_head_mint_limits` (migration `0016`) backs the ADR-0037 F5b per-account mint rate-limit — `LAKKO_BACKUP_HEAD_MINT_RATE_LIMIT` + `_WINDOW_SECS`, default limit 0 = disabled, fail-open — so "0015 is the reason a migration is required" is no longer the whole story. **Anti-abuse without proof of work ([ADR-0054](docs/adr/0054-anti-abuse-without-client-work.md) S4, 2026-09-28)** — `/signup/start` carries the ONLY proof of work left in the system (`/pow/challenge` issues at `LAKKO_SIGNUP_POW_EFFORT_BITS`, default 8, to be set by measurement), and `/signup/finish` carries none: it must consume a `signup_states` row the start wrote after its proof verified (migration `0020`, TTL 600 s, deleted in the account-creating transaction; none → `410`, recovery-upload mismatch → `400`), so one proof creates at most one account. Every other former proof route (login / recovery login / password-reset start, TOTP recover, vault recover start / finish, recovery-contact enroll-start / recover start / finish, device authorize start / redeem, device request, TOTP enroll start) lost its `pow` field and is limited in `src/limits.rs` instead: DB-backed fixed-window counters keyed on a BLAKE3 of what the request names (login handle, contact hash, target account), charged before any lookup so unknown and known names trip alike, fail closed (`503 rate-limiter-unavailable`) on a store error; plus in-memory per-address token buckets (addresses never stored). A limit answers `429 {"error", "retry_after_secs"}` + `Retry-After`, never `401`; codes `too-many-attempts` / `recovery-code-send-limited` / `device-request-limited` / `signup-rate-limited`; knobs `LAKKO_AUTH_LIMIT_*` / `LAKKO_AUTH_IP_LIMIT_*` (`N/SECS`, defaults chosen, not measured). The per-row TOTP and vault lockouts and the recovery-code 429 were removed: each answered `429` only for an existing row, an existence oracle **Sign in by name (ADR-0054 S5, `login_handle.rs`, migration `0021`):** since S3 a discovery lookup needs an anonymous token and a token needs a session, so the pre-session routes (login / recovery login / password-reset start, TOTP recover, vault recover start / finish, recovery-contact recover start / finish) take `handle` (base64url, 32 bytes; `loginHandle` on both FFI surfaces = `BLAKE3-derive-key("lakko/auth/login-handle/v1", canonical username)`, defined in `lakko_contacts::username`) instead of `account_id`; `login_handles` stores only `keyed-BLAKE3(LAKKO_AUTH_HANDLE_PEPPER, handle)` -> account id (one handle per account, FK cascade on account deletion), bound with bearer `PUT` / removed with `DELETE /auth/login-handle` (`409 handle-taken`); an unbound handle resolves to a stand-in account id derived from its key, so the same lookups run and OPAQUE takes its fake-credentials path with a credential identifier stable per name; the account id reaches the client only in the finish response. Pepper unset -> those routes and `/auth/login-handle` answer `503 login-by-name-disabled`. The passkey allow-list start still takes `account_id`. **Since [ADR-0056](docs/adr/0056-pairing-names-account-by-handle.md) S1 (2026-09-28) the device routes do not:** `POST /device/request` takes `{handle, pairing_payload_b64}` (resolved like the S5 routes, the per-target counter keyed on the handle, `503` without the pepper) and an unknown handle's request is stored against its stand-in, which no device polls, so `/device/request/status` answers alike for both; migration `0022` drops the `device_requests` foreign key for that (account deletion clears the row explicitly); `POST /device/authorize/redeem` takes `{grant_token_b64}` alone, finds the grant by `SHA-256` through a new unique index on `device_grants.grant_hash` (locking read + delete in one transaction), and its response's `account_id_b64` is where the fresh device learns the id **Device link (ADR-0049 section 2, amended 2026-09-28)** — `device_request.rs` relays the `lakko-device-link` SPAKE2 exchange opaquely and enforces one exchange per request (migration `0024`, `device_requests` rebuilt: `pake_b` once, `pake_a` + `sealed_payload` + `confirm_a` once, `outcome` + `confirm_b` once): anonymous `POST /device/request {handle}` / `/device/request/status` / `/device/request/payload`, bearer `GET /device/requests`, `POST /device/requests/{id}/pake`, `POST /device/requests/{id}/result`, `DELETE /device/requests/{id}`; a stand-in (unknown-handle) request answers every anonymous route like a real request nobody answered; the SHA-256 comparison code is gone. **Wrong-code budget (second security pass, migration `0025`):** wrong-code outcomes are counted per account; 3 in 24 h pause the account's device requests for 1 h, 6 for a day (`LAKKO_AUTH_LIMIT_DEVICE_LINK_WRONG_CODE_{SHORT,LONG}` + `_SUSPEND_{SHORT,LONG}_SECS`, chosen); a paused account's new requests are filed under the stand-in, answer like any other, and the pake route refuses |
| `ds` | MLS Delivery Service (FIFO journal per group + per-device mailbox). **Wake forwarding ([ADR-0054](docs/adr/0054-anti-abuse-without-client-work.md) S1, `src/wake.rs`):** a group publish may carry `x-lakko-wake: <push_gid 43-char base64url>;message|call`, parsed strictly before the append (malformed -> 400, nothing stored; on a mailbox -> 400); on a claimed journal whose proof verified, the DS forwards `(push_gid, message_id, kind)` to push-gw's internal listener fire-and-forget (5 s timeout, 256 in flight), capped per journal and per `push_gid` (`LAKKO_DS_WAKE_BURST` 30, `LAKKO_DS_WAKE_PER_MINUTE` 60, chosen not measured), logging only kind and outcome; an unclaimed journal validates the header and drops the wake. `LAKKO_DS_PUSH_GW_URL` + `LAKKO_DS_PUSH_GW_SECRET` both unset -> dormant, one set -> refuses to start. The header is not covered by the ADR-0053 proof signature (S5 item). **[ADR-0053](docs/adr/0053-ds-journal-authorisation.md) S1 landed** in `src/access.rs` + migration `0002_journal_access.sql` (`ds_group_access` per `(group_id, epoch)`, `ds_mailbox_access`, `ds_group_knocks`, `ds_proof_nonces`, and a `pow_challenges` table dropped by `0004` in ADR-0054 S3): a **claimed** group's `GET`/`POST messages` and `GET knocks` and a claimed mailbox's `GET messages` require an `x-lakko-ds-proof` header verified by `lakko-ds-proof` under one of the two newest registered epoch keys (mailbox: the identity key at epoch 0), the nonce consumed through the `ds_proof_nonces` primary key so a replay is refused, every failure one uniform `401 proof required`; an unclaimed journal keeps the Phase 1.3 open posture, as do a mailbox `POST` (the cold inbox) and a group's `POST knock` (16 KiB payload, response carries the latest epoch; since ADR-0054 S3 it spends one `ds-knock` anonymous token in `x-lakko-anon-token`, checked only after the body validates so a malformed knock costs none, through `lakko-anon-token-server`'s gate over the spent-set migration `0003`; the `/pow/challenge` route, `LAKKO_DS_POW_EFFORT_BITS` and the `lakko-pow-server` dependency are gone). Registration is **ADR-0057's since 2026-09-28** (`src/ownership.rs`, migration `0006`: `ds_group_owner`, `ds_group_access` with `registered_by` / `registered_rank`, `ds_group_registrars`, `ds_push_bindings`): `PUT /ds/group/{hex}/{claim,reset,access,registrars,registrars-set,root,push-binding}` each take one signed `lakko-ds-proof` statement (Append only at `latest + 1`, a member at most one past the newest admin/root epoch; Replace only over strictly lower ranks, no root override; `registrars-set` root-only; a `root` naming the current root is a 204 probe); a stranger gets the uniform `401` before its nonce is spent, rank shortfalls are `403`, conflicts `409`; an epoch key opens the journal but registers nothing. A wake whose `push_gid` is not the publishing journal's binding (keyed-BLAKE3 under `LAKKO_DS_BINDING_PEPPER`, unique) is dropped; pepper unset -> binding `503 push-binding-disabled` and every wake dropped. `PUT /ds/mailbox/{hex}/access` requires the key the pseudonym derives from (`400` otherwise), idempotent. The pruner also sweeps knocks (7 d) and nonces (2 x window). Body handlers take collected `Bytes` so the proof covers the published bytes. `LAKKO_DS_TEST_DATABASE_URL`-gated route tests in `access.rs` and `ownership.rs` over the shared `test_support.rs` (skip silently without it) |
| `storage` | Ciphertext blob proxy — **backed by the local filesystem rooted at `LAKKO_BLOB_DIR`, not SeaweedFS**; SeaweedFS / S3 is a Phase-4+ migration the source names as future work, and this row asserted it as present for a long time. Two surfaces: the content-addressed blob store (`blob.rs` / `stream.rs`, the ADR-0023 attachment target) and the **backup blob** (`backup.rs` — `PUT` / `GET` / `DELETE /backup`, session-token gated, `MAX_BACKUP_BYTES`-capped), which is the storage half of [ADR-0037](docs/adr/0037-backup-head-rollback-commitment.md) and stays byte-opaque: the auth-signed head rides *inside* the blob, so no migration was needed here |
| `gif-proxy` | GIF search + media proxy in front of GIPHY — `lakko-svc-gif-proxy` ([ADR-0047](docs/adr/0047-media-messages-and-gif-proxy.md) §4). Stateless (no DB, no disk), `127.0.0.1:8091`, nginx `/api/gifs/` (web) + `api.lakko.me/gifs/` (native) with the usual prefix strip. Session-token auth identical to `storage`. `GET /gifs/trending?offset=` + `GET /gifs/search?q=&offset=` (fixed page of 24, `rating=pg-13`, q trimmed 1..=100 chars, offset <= 4999) return `{items: [{id, preview_url, full_url, width, height}], next_offset}` where both URLs are proxy paths `/gifs/media/{id}/{preview\|full}`, never GIPHY's; `GET /gifs/media/{id}/{variant}` takes an alphanumeric id only, resolves it through GIPHY's API (or a short-lived **per-account** rendition map filled by that account's own searches, so a preview does not cost an API call and a cache hit reveals nothing about other accounts), checks the rendition host (`https`, `giphy.com` / `*.giphy.com`, on every redirect hop too), and streams it with a size cap (preview 2 MiB, full 8 MiB) and a content-type allowlist (`image/gif`, `image/webp`, `video/mp4`). No client-supplied URL is ever fetched. No API key → every `/gifs` route `503 gif-provider-not-configured`; upstream failure → `502 gif-upstream-failed` (10 s timeout); per-account token buckets (search+trending, media) → `429 rate-limited` + `Retry-After`. Logs method, route template, status and latency only — never a query, id, account or GIPHY URL (the API key rides in it); nginx access/error logging is off on those locations for the same reason |
| `channels` | Phase 4 channel-broadcast backend. Publisher / subscriber / rotation paths complete. `ChannelStore` trait with two impls: `InMemoryChannelStore` (test default, dev fallback) + `SqlChannelStore` (MariaDB, production; migrations under `services/channels/migrations/000{1..4}_*.sql`). Toggled at boot via `LAKKO_CHANNELS_DATABASE_URL`. Background prune loop covers both posts (30-day TTL) + stale subscribers (90-day TTL via `last_active_at`, FK cascade drops envelope), hourly cadence; toggles: `LAKKO_CHANNELS_RETENTION_SECS` / `LAKKO_CHANNELS_SUBSCRIBER_RETENTION_SECS` / `LAKKO_CHANNELS_PRUNE_INTERVAL_SECS`. **Batched rotation ([ADR-0030](docs/adr/0030-batched-rotation-fanout.md)):** a two-phase staged prepare (`POST /rotate/stage`, chunked ≤5000, `channel_staged_envelopes` migration `0003`, never served) + atomic `POST /rotate/commit`, alongside the legacy single-shot `POST /rotate` — C-42 preserved as "the commit is atomic". **Multi-epoch envelope retention ([ADR-0031](docs/adr/0031-multi-epoch-envelope-retention.md)):** `channel_envelopes` keyed `(channel, subscriber, epoch)` (migration `0004`); each write path files at a concrete epoch + prunes to the highest N (`LAKKO_CHANNELS_ENVELOPE_RETENTION_EPOCHS`, default 4, floor 1); `fetch_envelope` returns the highest retained epoch (unchanged wire), the new `fetch_envelopes` (`GET .../subscribers/:pk/envelopes`) the whole retained `[(epoch, envelope)]` set — an offline subscriber catches up by opening each into a client-side `epoch → seed` map (`deliver_envelope` refuses a future epoch, so the read path never serves a pre-committed rotation). Server stays opaque — never decrypts `ChannelPost` bytes or HPKE seed envelopes; `lakko-channels` crate is `dev-dep` only. See [`docs/security/calls.md`](docs/security/calls.md) C-40..C-42 |
| `edge` | **A Phase-0 stub, not a service.** `main.rs` is 28 lines whose whole body logs "edge service starting (Phase 0 stub)"; there is no router, no cascade and no signaling, and it has not been touched since the Phase-0 foundations commit. The intended scope — broadcast cascade + audio-room signaling — is the *plan*, and the row read as if it had shipped |
| `discovery` | Username lookup via OPRF. Also fronts the identity-bundle store (the AccountIdentity pubkey + the ADR-0016 supersession proof + the ADR-0019 `leaf_auth`); on a key change it best-effort appends the new binding to `transparency` (reconciler sweep), threading `leaf_auth` so the log entry is client-authorised. **Genesis-attest sidecar (ADR-0036)** — migration `0016` adds an opaque `genesis_attest` column; discovery stores + forwards it to the transparency append (`genesis_attest_b64`) verbatim (never parsed, invariant #2), `COALESCE`-preserved across non-rotation re-uploads (a rotation carries none — its supersession proof already authorises it). **No proof of work since [ADR-0054](docs/adr/0054-anti-abuse-without-client-work.md) S3** (`/pow/challenge`, `LAKKO_DISCOVERY_POW_EFFORT_BITS`, `src/pow.rs` and `lakko-pow-server` removed; migration `0020` drops `pow_challenges` after `0019`, the byte-identical spent-set): `POST /discovery/oprf` spends one `discovery-lookup` anonymous token (`x-lakko-anon-token`, the shared gate in `lakko-anon-token-server`), `POST /discovery/channel/register` one `discovery-register`, the three ledger `.../consume` routes one `invite-consume`; `lookup` and `channel/lookup` carry no token (their enumeration step is the OPRF evaluation) and keep the per-IP limiter; the session-gated writes are counted per account by a separate write bucket (`rate_limit::write_middleware`, sized by the per-account lookup knobs). A contact-invite consume of an absent id now answers the documented `404` (it was a `500`: MariaDB's `INSERT IGNORE` downgrades the FK violation) |
| `transparency` | CONIKS-style KT log — `lakko-svc-transparency`. Persists the Merkle tree + operator-signed roots; serves `GET /transparency/{root,latest/:key,proof/:idx}` + `operator-key`. Append (`POST`, append-secret-gated) verifies the client `leaf_auth` and refuses an unauthorised new binding under `LAKKO_TRANSPARENCY_REQUIRE_LEAF_AUTH` (G-30 #2, ADR-0019). Witness integration (ADR-0020): **co-signs-on-read** — signs the head root fresh per `/root`, reuses a cached witness cosig keyed on `(tree_root, log_size)`, re-obtains it only on growth, and serves **no** cosig (never a stale one) when the witness is unreachable. **K-of-N witness quorum (ADR-0039, G-30 finding-1)** — `witness_client.rs` `list_from_env` fans out to a LIST of witnesses (`LAKKO_TRANSPARENCY_WITNESS_URLS` + `_COSIGN_SECRETS`, comma-separated 1:1 positional, empty-entry fail-closed; backward-compat singular `LAKKO_TRANSPARENCY_WITNESS_URL` / `LAKKO_TRANSPARENCY_WITNESS_COSIGN_SECRET` fallback — the shorthand `_SECRET` this row used to print names no real variable — plus `LAKKO_TRANSPARENCY_WITNESS_TIMEOUT_SECS`; nothing set → dormant); `root_with_cosig` best-effort collects a BUNDLE served as `witness_cosigs_b64: Vec<String>` (a down / refusing witness is absent, never blocking, never a stale per-witness cosig); migration `0005` widens the single-row `witnessed_root` cosig → a concatenated bundle AND adds a per-witness `witness_high_water` table (each witness handed a consistency proof from ITS OWN last-co-signed position, advanced only on its own success — anti-stranding auto-rejoin of a witness that was down during a partial-success growth). The **client** enforces the K-of-N threshold (`verify_witness_quorum`); the server is best-effort collection only. Operator Ed25519 key `load_or_init` (+ a dev/e2e `LAKKO_TRANSPARENCY_OPERATOR_SEED_HEX` fixed-seed override, env-gated, loud-warn, prod-inert). **Auth-attested genesis gate (ADR-0036, G-30 finding 2)** — migration `0004` adds an opaque `genesis_attest` sidecar (never in `canonical_bytes`, so it touches no root/proof); the genesis/reset arm of the append gate (`store.rs`) additionally runs `verify_genesis_fresh` (fail-closed) behind default-OFF `LAKKO_TRANSPARENCY_REQUIRE_GENESIS_ATTEST` + pinned `LAKKO_TRANSPARENCY_GENESIS_ATTEST_KEY` (`operator_key.rs`, loud startup diagnostics per flag×key combo), so a genesis leaf without a valid account-authenticated attestation is refused — turning first-entry squatting from detected to PREVENTED; the ADR-0019 self-sig gate stays as belt-and-suspenders |
| `witness` | Independent KT-log witness co-signer — `lakko-svc-witness` (ADR-0020, G-30 #1 split-view defence). A dedicated Ed25519 key (separate trust domain from the operator — a deploy property). `POST /witness/cosign` (cosign-secret-gated, constant-time): verifies the operator root sig + no-rollback + a Merkle consistency proof from its last-co-signed high-water, then co-signs + advances; **refuses a fork** (a same-size divergent root → 409 `NotConsistent`). `GET /witness/key` publishes the verifying key. Persists only `(root, log_size)` high-water — never accounts (invariant #5). Server stays opaque (invariant #2). **K-of-N (ADR-0039, G-30 finding-1): this service is UNCHANGED** — a witness quorum is N INDEPENDENT instances of this same binary (each its own Ed25519 key + DB + high-water); the K-of-N logic lives entirely in the transparency fan-out + the client-side `verify_witness_quorum` gate, so no witness-service code change was needed |
| `push-gw` | Fan-out to **four** providers, not three — `providers/{apns,fcm,unifiedpush,webpush}.rs`, with `ProviderKind::WebPush` a first-class variant (Web Push RFC 8291 framing lives here, deliberately not in `lakko-push`). Public routes: per-device `/push/register` / `/push/unregister` and group `/push/group/subscribe` / `/push/group/unsubscribe` over `push_group_subscriptions` (migration `0002`), each spending one `push-register` anonymous token (`x-lakko-anon-token`) since ADR-0054 S3 through `lakko-anon-token-server`'s gate (spent-set migration `0004`, `pow_challenges` dropped by `0005`; `/pow/challenge`, `LAKKO_PUSH_GW_POW_EFFORT_BITS` and `lakko-pow-server` removed). **The wake is not public since [ADR-0054](docs/adr/0054-anti-abuse-without-client-work.md) S1:** `/push/notify` is deleted (it had no caller) and `/push/notify-group` is served only on an internal listener (`LAKKO_PUSH_GW_INTERNAL_BIND`, default `127.0.0.1:8092`, `src/internal.rs`) behind `x-lakko-internal-secret` compared in constant time (`LAKKO_PUSH_GW_INTERNAL_SECRET`; unset -> the listener does not start), body `{push_gid (64 hex), envelope_pointer, kind}`, no PoW; the DS is its only caller. **The group id it keys on is an opaque `BINARY(32)` it never interprets** — which is exactly why [ADR-0042](docs/adr/0042-push-opaque-group-routing-id.md) / [0043](docs/adr/0043-push-routing-secret-rotation-on-removal.md) / [0044](docs/adr/0044-push-routing-secret-recency.md) could change that value's *meaning* from the DS journal key to a derived `push_gid` while leaving this service byte-identical unchanged; a `grep` here finds no `routing_id`, and that is the design, not a gap |
| `transport-rendezvous` | **A Phase-0 stub, not a service.** `main.rs` is 29 lines logging "transport-rendezvous service starting (Phase 0 stub)" — no signing, no config serving, no routes, untouched since Phase-0 foundations. The signed-bootstrap-config machinery that exists is all **client-side** (`BootstrapConfig::verify` + `DohFetcher` in `lakko-transport`, `fetch_bootstrap_config` on the UniFFI surface); there is no server counterpart yet, and the ADR-0026 Phase-5 deploy is where one would land |
| `obfs-proxy` | Obfs-terminating proxy — `lakko-svc-obfs-proxy` (lib+bin, ADR-0026 Phases 1-3). Runs the obfs server listeners — **REALITY + Shadowsocks-2022** (TCP) + **WebTransport** (QUIC, via the new `lakko_transport::WebTransportListener`) — terminates each tunnel into a framed `Connection`, and **byte-relays** it to a fixed internal HTTP target (`relay.rs`: full-duplex frame↔TCP via `select!`+mpsc). A dumb, stateless, **confined** relay — never parses the inner HTTP (MLS-E2EE), only reaches the one configured internal address (no open relay), holds no account state (invariant #5). `config.rs` env-parses the target + REALITY (bind/cover-SNI/allow-list) + SS-2022 (bind/PSK) + WebTransport (bind/cover-SNI); `main.rs` runs the accept loops + an axum `health`/`ready`/`version` control plane. The obfs ports are raw, not HTTP. `tests/loopback_e2e.rs` proves all three (REALITY + SS-2022 + WebTransport) end-to-end (client → listener → relay → fake internal HTTP origin → response). **MASQUE: `lakko_transport::MasqueListener` is built (quinn server + h3 extended-CONNECT-UDP accept + 200 + a datagram server `Connection`; handshake verified) but is NOT wired into the proxy** — a real finding: MASQUE `CONNECT-UDP` carries **unreliable, unordered QUIC datagrams**, so relaying a *reliable* internal HTTP service over it does not work (the response datagram is dropped on close → `BadResponse("no header terminator")`; QUIC datagrams are never retransmitted nor flushed ahead of `CONNECTION_CLOSE`). The MASQUE loopback e2e is `#[ignore]`'d documenting this; the three reliable-stream transports cover the use case (MASQUE is for tunnelling UDP, not TCP/HTTP — using it would need a reliability layer the proxy should not add). Native client routing (Phase 4) **landed** end-to-end on iOS + Android (FFI `fetch_bootstrap_config` + arming + the seam on every native REST client — see the ADR-0026 row). **WS-over-tunnel has landed too** — this row used to list it as following: `lakko_transport::tunneled_websocket` over a `ByteStreamConnection`, the UniFFI `open_tunneled_websocket`, both native `SfuSignalingClient`s consuming it, and this service's own non-ignored `reality_tunnel_carries_a_websocket_round_trip` proving the path. Only the Phase-5 deploy + live censored-net test remain. 12 tests (5 loopback e2e of which the MASQUE one is `#[ignore]`d, 4 config, 3 relay) |
| `sfu` | Minimal Rust SFU on `str0m` (stack chosen in `S-3-B-spike`). **Signaling half shipped** — Lakko-native typed wire protocol (welcome / peer-joined / peer-left / signal-relay / ping/pong / error), `lakko-call-jwt` admission, per-peer mpsc fanout, cross-call isolation. The frame definitions themselves moved out to `crates/lakko-sfu-proto` (`src/protocol.rs` is now a re-export shim) so server and both FFI clients parse one definition. 29 non-media unit tests + 9 in `tests/integration_signaling.rs`. **Media half** (`S-3-B-routing-media`): steps 1–5 landed — `str0m =0.19.0` pinned with `rust-crypto` feature (pure-Rust crypto provider, no openssl/aws-lc-rs); `services/sfu/src/media.rs` ships `MediaPeer` (wraps `str0m::Rtc`) + `MediaRouter` (per-call peer map) + `MediaCommand` + `MediaTaskHandle` (async/sync boundary, `oneshot` reply channels, `try_send` back-pressure with `Backpressure` / `TaskGone` / `Media(MediaError)` typed errors) + `spawn_media_task(bind_addr)` (real `tokio::net::UdpSocket` bind + tokio `select!` over inbound UDP, command channel, earliest-peer-timeout sleep; drains `Rtc::poll_output` → `UdpSocket::send_to` on `Output::Transmit`; prunes peers whose `Rtc::is_alive` flipped after DTLS/ICE failure). `Config::media_bind: Option<SocketAddr>` parses `LAKKO_SFU_MEDIA_BIND` (unset / empty → signaling-only; malformed → loud `Invalid`); `AppState::new_with_media` + `build_app_with_media` wire the handle through `Router::with_media`, `main.rs` spawns the task when configured. SDP / ICE wire frames (`ClientFrame::SdpOffer` + `IceCandidate`, `ServerFrame::SdpAnswer`, `ErrorCode::BadSdp` / `BadIceCandidate`) lazy-promote a media peer on first offer via `peer_or_insert`. RTP fanout (step 5): `drain_outputs` returns `DrainSummary { earliest_deadline, pending: Vec<PendingFanout> }`, each `Output::Event(Event::MediaData(_))` is captured tagged with originating `MediaPeerId`; `fanout_media_data(router, &pending)` walks non-origin peers and calls `MediaPeer::forward_media_data` (typed `MediaForward { Sent / NoWriter / NoCodecMatch / WriteFailed / WrongLayer }` outcome wrapping `Rtc::writer(mid).match_params(params).write(...)` — SFrame ciphertext stays opaque, the SFU forwards bytes without inspection; the fifth variant arrived with simulcast, below). `src/media.rs` now carries 44 unit tests (incl. async round-trip + real UDP-bind smoke + 7 fanout tests) — this row asserted "17" here and "30" a few sentences later, two counts for the same thing and both stale. **Track-routing half** (`S-3-B-tracks` steps 1–3b, complete): `TrackIn` / `TrackOutEntry` / `TrackOutState` bookkeeping + `Event::MediaAdded` capture; `propagate_new_tracks_in` opens a `ToOpen` on every non-origin peer; `negotiate_if_needed` + `drive_renegotiations(router, peers)` dispatch an SFU-initiated `ServerFrame::SdpOffer` back to each peer's outbound mpsc and `accept_renegotiation_answer` promotes the `TrackOutEntry` to `Open` so `forward_media_data` resolves the destination mid. Proven end-to-end in `services/sfu/tests/synthetic_client_e2e.rs`: a single-client test drives the production `spawn_media_task` through a full ICE+DTLS handshake against a real `str0m::Rtc`, and `two_clients_cross_fanout_forwards_media_verbatim` (step 3b) proves a publisher A's 80-byte payload surfaces **verbatim** as `Event::MediaData` at a subscriber B through the full renegotiation wire — the integration-side close of C-39. **Fanout fairness** (Jun 2026): `fanout_media_data` returns a `FanoutOutcome { sent / no_writer / no_codec_match / write_failed / wrong_layer }` tally (one increment per `frame × destination peer`) + emits `lakko_sfu_media_frames_forwarded_total` + `lakko_sfu_media_frames_dropped_total{reason}` Prometheus counters, so a starved subscriber (a `no_writer` spike) is observable rather than silently dropped; `fanout_visits_every_non_origin_peer_for_three_plus` (N ∈ {3,4,5}) + `fanout_tally_is_per_frame_times_non_origin_peers` pin that one origin frame is offered to every non-origin peer exactly once (origin never). **Simulcast-layer selection LANDED** — this row long carried it as the deferred follow-up "wire-touching, needs simulcast-capable test clients", and both halves are now built: `MediaPeer::select_layer` + a per-`TrackOutEntry` `chosen_rid` with a first-layer latch, per-`(Mid, Rid)` `LayerBitrateMeter`s, a PLI keyframe request on a switch, and the bandwidth policy `drive_layer_selection` driven from the live media loop, counted by a third Prometheus counter `lakko_sfu_simulcast_layer_switches_total{direction}`; the simulcast-capable client exists as `simulcast_publisher_negotiates_and_forwards_a_layer_over_the_live_sfu`. **Signaling liveness** (2026-09-19): the socket pings every peer on the WebSocket protocol layer (`LAKKO_SFU_SIGNALING_PING_SECS`, now 5) and drops one silent for `LAKKO_SFU_SIGNALING_IDLE_SECS` (now 15), announcing the leave — before this a vanished peer stayed registered until TCP or nginx gave up. **Call lifetime** (2026-09-20, `src/call_lifetime.rs`): the SFU decides when a call is over — a room that has stood empty for `CALL_EMPTY_GRACE` (5 s) is ended, a later socket for that call id gets `ErrorCode::CallEnded` in place of the Welcome and is closed, and `GET /v1/calls/{call_id}?token=<call jwt>` answers `unknown` / `live` / `closing` / `ended` for the call the token names (nginx routes it beside `/v1/connect`); the clients close the chat's own record on either signal, so a call whose last participant's app died stops being offered within seconds instead of the record's freshness window. See [`services/sfu/README.md`](services/sfu/README.md) + [`services/sfu/UPSTREAM.md`](services/sfu/UPSTREAM.md) + [ADR-0011](docs/adr/0011-rust-sfu.md) + [`docs/security/calls.md`](docs/security/calls.md) C-39 |

### Other

- `schemas/` — protobuf schemas; **single source of truth** for the wire format
- `apps/` — clients (iOS / iPadOS / macOS / watchOS / Android / desktop / web), arrive in Phase 1. **iOS WebRTC pod (G3-1)** shipped: `apps/ios/Lakko/Calls/WebRTCPeerConnection.swift` wraps `RTCPeerConnectionFactory` + `RTCPeerConnection` from `stasel/WebRTC` SPM (`from: 125.0.0`); `CallSession.swift` drives real SDP offer/answer + ICE through it. **Android WebRTC pod (G3-2)** shipped: `apps/android/app/src/main/kotlin/me/lakko/app/calls/WebRtcPeerConnection.kt` wraps `PeerConnectionFactory` + `PeerConnection` from `io.github.webrtc-sdk:android` (pinned `125.6422.07`); `CallSession.kt` drives real SDP offer/answer + ICE through it via `suspendCancellableCoroutine` around libwebrtc's `SdpObserver`; `RECORD_AUDIO` + `MODIFY_AUDIO_SETTINGS` permissions wired into AndroidManifest.xml. Both platforms: audio-only on first pass; video capture + TURN bootstrap deferred (G3-{1,2}-video / G3-{1,2}-turn) and now landed. Video capture surface: iOS `LocalCameraCapture.swift` wraps `RTCCameraVideoCapturer`; Android `CameraVideoCapture.kt` wraps `Camera2Enumerator` + `CameraVideoCapturer`; both target 720p / 30 fps front-camera by default. Session-side wire-up (G3-{1,2}-video-ui, Jun 2026): `CallSession` on both platforms exposes an observable local video track (`@Published RTCVideoTrack?` on iOS / `StateFlow<VideoTrack?>` on Android), constructs the capture pipeline pre-SDP-build on `dial(video=true)` + `acceptCall(withVideo=)`, tears down camera in the shared `teardownMediaPipeline()` helper on both end paths. Rendering `session.localVideoTrack` through `LocalVideoPreviewView` inside the in-call UI landed as G3-{1,2}-video-ui-screen (Jun 2026): `CallUiState` on both platforms exposes a `localVideoTrack` published / StateFlow surface + a `bind(to:)` / `bindTo(...)` helper that subscribes the matching `CallSession` channel through Combine (iOS) / a coroutine collector (Android). `CallScreen.{swift,kt}` render `LocalVideoPreviewView` inside `ParticipantTile`'s video surface when `participant.isLocal && camOn && localVideoTrack != nil`, with the mirror flag fed from `settings.mirrorLocal`; the prejoin preview gets the same dispatch. Remote-video reception + render landed as G3-{1,2}-remote-video (Jun 2026): the WebRTC pod `Delegate` gains `onRemoteVideoTrack`, fired from libwebrtc's Unified-Plan track-added callback (`didAdd rtpReceiver:streams:` on iOS / `onAddTrack` on Android, video tracks only — audio plays out automatically); `CallSession` exposes a `remoteVideoTrack` observable, `bind(to:)` / `bindTo(...)` bridge it alongside the local track, and `ParticipantTile` renders it **un-mirrored** for the sole remote participant (`soleRemoteParticipantId`, the 1×1 case — the single handle is never painted onto the wrong tile in a group call). G3-{1,2}-remote-video-nway (the per-participant track map keyed on the SFU MSID stream-id = source peer id) **has since landed on both natives** — `CallUiState.remoteVideoTracks: [String: RTCVideoTrack]` / `StateFlow<Map<String, VideoTrack>>` with `setRemoteVideoTrack(_:forParticipant:)`, keyed `"peer-<streamId>"` in each `SfuCallUi` and rendered per tile in each `CallScreen`; so the sole-remote handle described here is the 1×1 path, not the group one. Both `CallScreen.{swift,kt}` still seed `seedMockState()` for the mock design path; `bind(to:)` is the integration seam for the real `CallSession`. Session-bind adapter (G3-{1,2}-session-bind, Jun 2026): `CallUiState.driveFrom(session)` maps `CallSession.state` → `CallPhase` + synthesises the 1×1 roster (local always; sole remote at `connected`/track-arrival, `camOn` from track presence) + bridges both video tracks via a full-rebuild-on-every-emission collector; `CallScreen` accepts an optional `session` param and drives from it (mock fallback when nil), end-call routes to `session.hangUp()`. Call presentation (G3-{1,2}-call-present, Jun 2026): `ChatScreen` now presents the live `CallScreen(session:)` over the chat — iOS via a nested `CallHostView` observing `activeCall` + a `.fullScreenCover`; Android via a full-screen Compose `Dialog` — once the call is live (outgoing/connecting/connected/ending/done), with the `ringing` incoming-call prompt kept as a lightweight inline banner. `ChatViewModel` already owned `activeCall` + `startCall` (toolbar phone button) + accept/hangUp/clearFinished + the incoming-offer path, so the whole video/audio/SFrame adapter is now **runtime-active** on outgoing dial + accept-incoming; the in-call End button routes to `session.hangUp()`. Call controls (G3-{1,2}-call-controls, Jun 2026): the in-call mic / camera buttons drive the real WebRTC tracks — `CallSession` exposes observable `micEnabled` / `cameraEnabled` + `setMicrophoneEnabled` / `setCameraEnabled` (toggling `RTCAudio/VideoTrack.isEnabled` via the pod), `driveFrom` maps them onto the local participant tile, and the buttons route to the session. Call-key wiring (G3-{1,2}-call-key, Jun 2026) — **unblocks the call feature**: previously `setCallKey` was never called in the chat-call path, so `attachSFrame` always failed and every call refused per security invariant #4 (the SFrame keying plumbing was simply absent). Now `ChatRepository.deriveCallKey(groupIdHex, callId)` derives the per-call seed `group.export_secret(identity, "lakko/call/v1", callId, 32)` (audit-pack C-1 — deterministic per MLS epoch, so offerer + answerer derive identical bytes) + `own_leaf_index()`, and `ChatViewModel` arms `session.setCallKey(seed, localLeaf)` before dial/accept on both the outgoing and incoming paths (a failed derivation leaves the seed unset → the call refuses, fail-closed). E2EE pill (G3-{1,2}-e2ee-active, Jun 2026): `CallSession.e2eeActive` flips true when `setCallKey` arms the seed; `driveFrom` maps it onto `E2eeState(active:)` so the in-call pill shows the green lock only when SFrame keying is real (it stayed at the negotiating default before). Connection quality (G3-{1,2}-conn-quality iter1, Jun 2026): the in-call header shows a real quality signal sampled from WebRTC `getStats`. The pod's `sampleConnectionStats()` (iOS `async`, Android `suspend` over `getStats`) walks the stats report for the active ICE `candidate-pair` (`state == succeeded`, preferring `nominated`), reads `currentRoundTripTime` (s→ms) and flags `relayed` when either candidate's `candidateType == "relay"`; `CallSession` exposes `rttMs` (`@Published Int?` / `StateFlow<Int?>`) + `connectionRelayed` (`Bool`) driven by a 2 s background poll loop armed on the first connected transition and torn down with the media pipeline. `driveFrom`/`rebuildFromSession` map `rttMs` → the local participant's `QualityTier` (UX display bands, not measured SLOs: nil→good, <100→excellent, <200→good, <350→fair, <600→poor, else→bad) + republish a `ConnectionDetail(rttMs, downBps, upBps, connectionType=relay/direct/unknown)`. iter2 (Jun 2026): the popover's down/up lines show a live bitrate — `ConnectionStatsSnapshot` carries the candidate-pair's cumulative `bytesReceived`/`bytesSent`, `CallSession` keeps the previous (bytes, monotonic-time) sample and derives `downBps`/`upBps` in **bits per second** = `deltaBytes * 8 / dt` (dt from `ProcessInfo.systemUptime` / `System.nanoTime()` so poll jitter doesn't skew it; guarded `dt > 0.1 s`, deltas clamped `>= 0`), reset to nil in `teardownMediaPipeline`. Web parity (G3-3-conn-quality, Jun 2026): `apps/web/src/calls/call-session.ts` `CallSession` grows a `getStats()` poll loop (armed on the first `connected` transition, cleared in `dispose()`) that emits a typed `WebConnectionStats { rttMs, relayed, downBps, upBps }` via an optional `onConnectionStats` callback — same candidate-pair selection (succeeded + nominated) + same monotonic-clock byte-delta bitrate math; `Chat.tsx::buildCallSession` wires it into a `callConnStats` signal + the shared `callActions.setConnectionDetail` / `setQuality`. iter3 (per-remote-participant quality for group calls — needs the SFU multi-party wire) is deferred. Web session-bind (G3-3-web-session-bind, Jun 2026): the full `apps/web/src/routes/Call.tsx` in-call UI is now driven by the live `CallSession` (the web analog of iOS/Android session-bind + call-present). `Call` gains optional `embedded` / `onExit` props; in embedded mode it skips the mount-time `callActions.reset()` (the parent owns lifecycle), routes end-call + the ended-overlay dismissal through `onExit` (→ `CallSession.hangUp`) instead of navigating home, and hides the text E2EE pill (the 1×1 web path is P2P DTLS-SRTP, not SFrame — a "secured" text chip would mislead, and a text chip mid-call is poor UX). `Chat.tsx` maps the session's `WebCallState` → the shared store (`setPhase` + a synthesised 1×1 roster + `setE2ee` inactive) and presents `<Call embedded onExit>` as a full-screen overlay for live states (`outgoing`/`connecting`/`connected`/`ending`), keeping the lightweight inline banner only for `ringing` (Accept/Decline). The remote `<audio>` sink was lifted to a stable slot so it doesn't unmount when the banner gives way to the overlay. `tsc --noEmit` clean. Web call controls (G3-3-web-call-controls, Jun 2026): the in-call mic button now drives the real session — `CallSession.setMicrophoneEnabled(on)` flips the captured audio track's `enabled` flag (local-only gate, no renegotiation), and a `Chat.tsx` effect watching the store's local-participant `micOn` mirrors the `callActions.setMic` toggle onto the live session. Web 1×1 video shipped (later): the outbound caller's 📹 video button (`dial({audio,video})`) AND the incoming answerer's "Accept with video" (accept audio-only at capture, then camOn=true drives the shipped `addLocalVideo` mid-call camera-on via perfect-negotiation), local + remote video tiles — so the camera button drives a real local video track (the answerer captures audio-only until it opts in, fixing the old acquire-then-mute). `remoteWantsVideo` (from the offer's `mediaVideo`) gates the second accept button. Deferred web follow-ups: per-remote-participant quality (iter3) — still deferred; the roster's "Peer" placeholder is **not**, since the ADR-0021 resolution landed (`displayName`, else the chat label, else `"Peer"` as a last resort). Prejoin Join (G3-{1,2}-prejoin-join, Jun 2026): tapping the chat call button now PREPARES the session (builds it + arms the SFrame key) without dialing — the session sits `idle` (mapped to the prejoin phase), the in-call screen presents the prejoin mic/camera preview, and the Join button calls `CallSession.dial` carrying the chosen mic/camera; a Cancel button tears the prepped-but-undialed session down (iOS: `session.hangUp` + `onExit`; Android: `ChatViewModel.cancelActiveCall`). The presentation gate (`isLive` / `callIsLive`) now includes `idle` so the prejoin shows for an outgoing call before it dials. Incoming calls keep the ringing banner → `acceptCall` (no greenroom). Deferred follow-ups: live 2-device verification (local builds only here; seed-match asserted by construction so far), hand-raise/screen-share controls (no real backing — stay mock), the e2ee comparable-fingerprint string (G3-{1,2}-e2ee-fingerprint — **DONE** Jun 2026: `lakko_calls::safety_number::call_safety_number` = domain-separated BLAKE3 of the shared per-call seed → 4×4-digit code, golden-pinned + canonical, FFI `callSafetyNumber` (uniffi + wasm), rendered in the in-call E2EE pill on **all three** clients, the native 1×1 path included; `lakko-security` APPROVE-WITH-NOTES. **Web reached that state late and the reason is the lesson.** The number was derived and written into the store (`sfu-call-ui.ts`, `multi-joiner-call.ts` → `callActions.setE2ee({ shortFingerprint })`) but the only component rendering it, `Call.tsx`'s `E2eePill`, sat behind `<Show when={props.embedded !== true}>` while `Chat.tsx` always mounts `<Call embedded={true}>` — so it showed on **no live web call**, group calls included, surviving only on the standalone `/call` design route. The suppressing comment gave a **correct** reason for the wrong scope: the 1×1 web path really is P2P DTLS-SRTP with no SFrame and no number, but the same component also renders the SFU group call, which has both. **A layout flag was answering a cryptographic question**, and it drifted the moment a second call kind shared the component. Fixed by asking the question directly — `apps/web/src/calls/safety-pill.ts` `showsSafetyNumber(e2ee)` shows the pill exactly when there **is** a number, which the 1×1 path already denies it by publishing `{ active: false, shortFingerprint: null }`; so the misleading-chip hazard is closed by the data rather than by a second gate someone must remember, and a future path that arms SFrame gets the pill automatically. It is a **module, not a condition in the JSX**, because `vitest` runs with no jsdom and no Solid transform — nothing in `routes/Call.tsx` is reachable from a test, which is how a wrong condition survived there unnoticed) **SFrame keying done** via the G3-sframe-native arc (N-1..N-6, closes G-28): see [`docs/ROADMAP-PHASE3-CLOSEOUT.md`](docs/ROADMAP-PHASE3-CLOSEOUT.md) G3-1 / G3-2 rows + the new shared `apps/shared/{webrtc-cpp-headers,sframe-bridge}/` + `apps/android/lakko-sframe/` Gradle module + `apps/ios/Lakko/Calls/SFrameAttach.{h,mm}` Obj-C++ shim. Boot-time selector probe (`SFrameAvailability` on both platforms) enforces security invariant #4 — refuse-to-start if libwebrtc's setFrameEncryptor / setFrameDecryptor are not present. **Web SFrame worker (SFU-3-sframe-c, Jun 2026)** — the browser mirror of the native shim, built on the W3C Encoded Transform API: `apps/web/src/calls/sframe-transform.ts` is the main-thread attach controller (`attachSenderSFrame` / `attachReceiverSFrame` set an `RTCRtpScriptTransform` over one shared module worker; `isSFrameTransformSupported` gates it and the attach helpers THROW when `RTCRtpScriptTransform` is absent — invariant #4, no plaintext-media fallback) and `sframe-transform-worker.ts` is the off-thread engine (loads the wasm core via `loadWasm`, then per frame: encrypt → `ParticipantKey.sealFramed`; decrypt → peek the header KID via `unwrapSframeFrame`, lazily derive that sender's key into a per-leaf `Map`, then `openFramed` — any frame that fails to parse or authenticate is dropped, never forwarded as plaintext). AAD = the SFrame wire header per [ADR-0013](docs/adr/0013-sframe-cross-platform-aad.md) (no host RTP `additional_data`), so a frame sealed in this worker opens byte-for-byte under the native `sframe-bridge` shim and vice versa. `vite.config.ts` repeats `vite-plugin-wasm` under `worker.plugins` so the worker bundle resolves the wasm import; `tsc --noEmit` clean + `vite build` emits the worker chunk with the wasm glue inside it. Not wired into the 1×1 `CallSession` — that path is P2P DTLS-SRTP with no SFU and stays so — but **the SFU-multiparty wire-up this deferred to has landed**: `sfu-call-session.ts` and `multi-joiner-call.ts` both call `attachSenderSFrame` / `attachReceiverSFrame`, so the worker is live for every web group call. Deferred gate (cannot run headless): live cross-platform RTP verification of a web↔native frame over the SFU forwarding path. **Web multi-party session (SFU-3-web-b, Jun 2026)** — `apps/web/src/calls/sfu-call-session.ts` `SfuCallSession` composes the whole web SFU call: it opens the `SfuSignalingClient` (`lakko-sfu-v1` WS, JWT-admitted), builds one `RTCPeerConnection` to the SFU, `getUserMedia` + `addTrack` + `attachSenderSFrame` on every local track (publish offer is client-initiated, sent via `sendSdpOffer`; SFU replies `SdpAnswer`), answers SFU-initiated `SdpOffer`s through a serialized queue (subscribe renegotiation → `sendSdpAnswer`), and on `ontrack` runs `attachReceiverSFrame` + surfaces the remote `MediaStreamTrack` keyed by MSID stream id. Refuses at `connect()` (before any capture) if `RTCRtpScriptTransform` is absent (invariant #4). `tsc --noEmit` clean + `vite build` pulls the full signaling+SFrame+worker graph. **Chat-UI wire-up (SFU-3-web-{c,…}, Jun 2026)** — `apps/web/src/calls/sfu-call-ui.ts` `createSfuCallUi` binds the session callbacks onto the shared `callActions` store (phase + roster + E2EE) and exposes a reactive `remoteStreams` signal (MSID stream id → `MediaStream`). `apps/web/src/routes/Chat.tsx` routes the call button by chat size: a group chat (`group.memberCount > 2`) calls `startGroupCall` — derive the per-call SFrame seed via `group.exportSecret(identity, "lakko/call/v1", callId, 32)` + leaf via `group.ownLeafIndex` (audit-pack C-1, deterministic per MLS epoch), mint the call-admission JWT (`CallJwtClient`, bound to the live `auth.identitySecrets().sessionToken`), build `createSfuCallUi`, connect; 1×1 chats stay on the P2P `CallSession`. The existing store-driven `Call.tsx` overlay renders the SFU roster tiles automatically (no per-call-type branching in the view); remote audio plays through one hidden `<audio>` per `remoteStreams` entry (keyed by stream identity). SFU base URL from `VITE_LAKKO_SFU_URL` (`sfu-config.ts`); unset → the group-call button surfaces a clear error (1×1 unaffected). `tsc --noEmit` clean; `vite build` emits the worker chunk from the real entry graph (Chat.tsx → adapter → session → SFrame → worker). Per-tile remote VIDEO rendering (SFU-3-web-e, Jun 2026): the SFU already pins the downstream MSID stream-id to the source peer id (`add_media(.., Some(source_peer.to_string()), ..)` in `services/sfu/src/media.rs`; `MediaPeerId == PeerId`, the same string the signaling roster carries), so `ev.streams[0].id` maps straight to the roster tile. The adapter flips a remote participant's `camOn` on video-track arrival (new `callActions.setParticipantCamera`), `Call.tsx` `ParticipantTile` gains a `remoteStreamFor` prop and renders a real `<video muted playsinline>` (muted — audio plays through the hidden `<audio>` sinks, so no double audio) when a stream resolves, and `Chat.tsx` passes the `peer-<id>` → stream lookup; the mock + 1×1 paths pass no `remoteStreamFor` and keep the avatar/placeholder. **Client-initiated mid-call renegotiation on the web SFU path has since landed** (this row deferred it as "needs perfect-negotiation glare handling"): `sfu-call-session.ts` carries `publishVideo()`, a `negotiationReady` latch, an `onnegotiationneeded` handler and the canonical perfect-negotiation collision check — the file's own header comment still reads as if it were deferred, and CLAUDE.md inherited that. Still deferred: the live cross-platform RTP verification (no deployed SFU here). **Native SFU signaling clients (SFU-3-native-signaling, Jun 2026)** — the Swift + Kotlin mirrors of the web `sfu-signaling-client.ts`: `apps/ios/Lakko/Calls/SfuSignalingClient.swift` (over `URLSessionWebSocketTask`, subprotocol via `webSocketTask(with:protocols:)`) and `apps/android/app/src/main/kotlin/me/lakko/app/calls/SfuSignalingClient.kt` (over Ktor's WebSockets plugin on the OkHttp engine — `libs.ktor.client.websockets` promoted from androidTest to a production dep). Both connect to `{baseUrl}/v1/connect?token=<jwt>`, parse inbound frames through the UniFFI typed `parseSfuServerFrame` → `SfuServerFrame` enum (Swift cases / Kotlin sealed-class) dispatched to callbacks, and send via the `composeSfu*` helpers — transport + framing only. Both platforms compile green (`xcodebuild` + `:app:compileDebugKotlin` BUILD SUCCEEDED — the iOS green-up also surfaced that the `LakkoCore.xcframework` + `liblakko_sframe_native.a` must be recross-compiled via `just build-ios` + `just cross-build-sframe-native-ios` after any SFU / `seal_framed` change, since the Swift binding + the `SFrameAttach.mm` bridge reference symbols that only exist in a freshly-built static lib). **iOS native `SfuCallSession` (SFU-3-ios-session, Jun 2026)** — `apps/ios/Lakko/Calls/SfuCallSession.swift`, the native analog of the web `SfuCallSession`: `@MainActor` class composing `SfuSignalingClient` + `WebRTCPeerConnection`. `connect()` refuses if the SFrame shim is unavailable (invariant #4), opens signaling, builds the PC, `addLocalAudioTrack` + `armSFrameEncryptors(seed, localLeaf)` (fail-closed), `createOffer` → `sendSdpOffer`; `onSdpAnswer` → `applyRemoteAnswer`; SFU-initiated `onSdpOffer` → serialized-queue `createAnswer` → `sendSdpAnswer`; local ICE trickles up via `sendIceCandidate` (the SFU's own candidates ride in the SDP — no server→client ICE frame). The `WebRTCPeerConnection.Delegate` gained an `onRemoteReceiver(RTCRtpReceiver, streamId)` hook (fired for EVERY receiver, audio + video) so the session arms `armSFrameDecryptor(for:seed:)` on each incoming track (invariant #4). Audio publish on the first pass (video capture into the SFU session + the `CallUiState`/Chat wire-up are follow-ups). The **Android `SfuCallSession`** (SFU-3-android-session, Jun 2026) is the Kotlin twin — `apps/android/.../calls/SfuCallSession.kt`, same publish/subscribe/renegotiation/ICE/SFrame shape over `WebRtcPeerConnection` + `SfuSignalingClient`, taking a `Context` + a main-confined `CoroutineScope` (signaling callbacks run on the scope; the pod delegate fires on libwebrtc threads and hops back); `WebRtcPeerConnection.Delegate` gained the matching `onRemoteReceiver(RtpReceiver, streamId)` hook. Both compile green (`xcodebuild` BUILD SUCCEEDED + `:app:compileDebugKotlin` BUILD SUCCESSFUL). Remaining for both natives: video capture into the SFU session + the live SFU round-trip. **iOS chat-UI adapter (SFU-3-ios-callui, Jun 2026)** — `apps/ios/Lakko/Calls/SfuCallUi.swift`, the iOS analog of the web `createSfuCallUi`: a `@MainActor` class binding `SfuCallSession`'s callbacks onto a `CallUiState` (state→`CallPhase` + E2EE pill active on connect; welcome/peer-joined/peer-left→roster; remote video track→`setRemoteVideoTrack`, per-participant since the n-way map landed — this read "sole-remote" when the adapter still held one handle). Closures capture the `CallUiState` + name resolver directly (not `self`), avoiding the init-order/retain-cycle trap. `xcodebuild` BUILD SUCCEEDED. The Android twin `apps/android/.../calls/SfuCallUi.kt` (SFU-3-android-callui, Jun 2026) is the same binding over `CallUiState` (StateFlow), taking a `Context` + `CoroutineScope` for the underlying `SfuCallSession`; `:app:compileDebugKotlin` BUILD SUCCESSFUL. Both native `SfuCallUi` adapters now match the web `createSfuCallUi`. **iOS group-call trigger core (SFU-3-ios-trigger, Jun 2026)** — the trigger logic landed: `apps/ios/Lakko/Calls/SfuConfig.swift` reads the SFU URL from the `LakkoSfuUrl` Info.plist key (analog of web `VITE_LAKKO_SFU_URL`); `ChatRepository.memberCount(groupIdHex:)` wraps `group.memberCount()` for the group-vs-1×1 branch; `ChatViewModel.startGroupCall()` derives the per-call SFrame seed/leaf via the existing `repository.deriveCallKey` (MLS exporter), mints the JWT (`CallJwtClient` bound to a `sessionTokenProvider` closure — `LakkoApp` passes `{ authRepository.identitySecrets?.sessionToken }`), builds `SfuCallUi(config:, ui: sfuCallUiState)`, and connects (fail-closed: a missing SFU URL / token / seed NSLogs + refuses). `activeSfuCall` + `sfuCallUiState` are published. `xcodebuild` BUILD SUCCEEDED. **iOS chat-UI presentation (SFU-3-ios-present, Jun 2026)** — the iOS SFU group call is now wired end-to-end: `CallScreen` gained an `init(externalState: CallUiState, onExit:)` (injects the `SfuCallUi`-driven state via `StateObject(wrappedValue:)` + an `externallyDriven` flag that skips the prejoin branch — the SFU call is already connecting when presented), `ChatViewModel.isGroupChat` (via `ChatRepository.memberCount`) branches the `ChatScreen` call button (group → `startGroupCall`, 1×1 → `startCall`), and `ChatScreen` presents `CallScreen(externalState: sfuCallUiState)` in a `fullScreenCover` gated on `activeSfuCall` (dismiss → `hangUpGroupCall`). `xcodebuild` BUILD SUCCEEDED. **Android group-call trigger + presentation (SFU-3-android-trigger+present, Jun 2026)** — the Kotlin twin of the iOS trigger + present: `app/build.gradle.kts` adds a `LAKKO_SFU_URL` `buildConfigField` (sourced from the gradle property `lakko.sfu.url`, empty when unset); `apps/android/.../calls/SfuConfig.kt` reads `BuildConfig.LAKKO_SFU_URL` (trims trailing slashes, `null` when blank — analog of the iOS `SfuConfig`); `ChatRepository.memberCount(groupIdHex):Int?` wraps `group.memberCount()` for the group-vs-1×1 branch; `ChatViewModel` gains a `sessionTokenProvider: () -> String?` ctor param (wired from `MainActivity` to `authRepository.identitySecrets.value?.sessionToken`), `activeSfuCall: StateFlow<SfuCallUi?>`, a reused `sfuCallUiState: CallUiState`, `isGroupChat`, `startGroupCall()` (derive seed/leaf via `repo.deriveCallKey` MLS exporter → mint JWT via `CallJwtClient` → build `SfuCallUi(applicationContext, config, viewModelScope, ui = sfuCallUiState)` → `connect()`; fail-closed: missing SFU URL / token / seed logs + refuses per invariant #4; pre-sets the UI phase to Connecting so the externally-driven `CallScreen` never flashes the prejoin), and `hangUpGroupCall()`. `ChatScreen` branches the call button (`isGroupChat` → `startGroupCall`, else 1×1 `startCall`), disables it while either call kind is up, and presents the existing `CallScreen(onExit = hangUpGroupCall, state = sfuCallUiState)` (no `session` — the `SfuCallUi` adapter owns the state, so no Compose surgery needed unlike iOS's `externalState` injection) in a full-screen `Dialog` gated on `activeSfuCall`. `:app:compileDebugKotlin` BUILD SUCCESSFUL. The native SFU group-call client is now wired end-to-end on web + iOS + Android. **Native SFU video capture (SFU-3-native-video, Jun 2026)** — the native `SfuCallSession` (iOS `SfuCallSession.swift` + Android `SfuCallSession.kt`) now publishes the local camera when `config.video` is set: it builds the capture pipeline (`LocalCameraCapture` / `CameraVideoCapture`, front camera 720p/30 — the same pods the 1×1 path uses), adds the video track BEFORE arming SFrame + building the offer (so the offer advertises a video m-line and `armSFrameEncryptors` — which already walks `[audio, video]` senders — seals the video sender alongside audio; the SFU never sees a plaintext frame, invariant #2/#4), surfaces the local track through a new `onLocalVideoTrack` callback that the `SfuCallUi` adapter maps onto `CallUiState.setLocalVideoTrack` (self-preview tile), and tears the camera down in `hangUp` (iOS `stop()`; Android `stop()` + `dispose()`). Soft-fails to audio-only on no-camera / start failure (silent video sender keeps the m-line; receiver sees a black frame). Both adapters also gained a `setCameraEnabled` passthrough (local-only `isEnabled` gate — meaningful only on a `config.video` call, since the track is added at connect). `xcodebuild` BUILD SUCCEEDED + `:app:compileDebugKotlin` BUILD SUCCESSFUL. The receive path already renders remote video (sole-remote tile). **Native video-call entry point (SFU-3-native-video-entry, Jun 2026)** — the chat header now offers a second call button (iOS `video.fill` toolbar item; Android `🎥 Video` text button + a new `onStartVideoCall` on the `Header` composable) that starts the call AS a video call: `ChatViewModel.startGroupCall(video:)` threads the flag into `SfuCallSession.Config.video` (group chats publish SFrame-sealed camera through the SFU), and 1×1 chats route to `startCall(video: true)` (the prejoin still lets the user toggle the camera before joining). The audio button stays `video=false`. Both buttons disable while a call is up. `xcodebuild` BUILD SUCCEEDED + `:app:compileDebugKotlin` BUILD SUCCESSFUL. Remaining for the SFU arc: the live SFU round-trip (no deployed SFU yet) and the cross-platform RTP interop verification (G-26 deferred gate) — web SFU-group client-initiated mid-call renegotiation is no longer on this list, see the perfect-negotiation note above. **iOS silent-push NSE decrypt ([ADR-0041](docs/adr/0041-ios-silent-push-nse-decrypt.md), Phases 0-3 landed end-to-end)** — the `apps/ios/LakkoNotificationService` NSE renders the real decrypted message preview on a push wake via a read-only throwaway MLS decrypt (mirroring the web `148753b` + Android `ae9a087` siblings): `NsePushDecrypt.swift` + `NseDecryptDeps.swift` reconstruct a fresh throwaway `Identity.fromSeed` → `restoreStorage` → `loadGroup` from the App-Group-shared inputs (Phase 1), decrypt over the slim `LakkoCoreExtension.xcframework` (Phase 2, `lakko-ffi-extension`), read-only DS-fetch after the app cursor, `processIncomingVerifyingRoles`/`parseBodyEnvelope` for the newest preview, then DISCARD — never persisting, structurally no-write across three layers (C-59); fail-closed to the generic "New message" banner, and an anti-censorship-armed user short-circuits to generic rather than making a direct un-tunneled DS connection. `lakko-security` APPROVE-WITH-NOTES. **Scope note — this bullet stops at the Jun 2026 call arc and is not the whole of `apps/`.** Everything the client sprints of ADR-0029 (mid-call rekey), ADR-0032 (multi-joiner call links — `multi-joiner-call.ts` / `MultiJoinerCall.swift` / `MultiJoinerElection`), ADR-0035 (per-`(leaf, stream)` KID), ADR-0042/0043/0044 (push routing) and ADR-0045 (proposal-based membership, the beacon, ownership transfer, self-eviction, typed refusals, automatic re-request) built on the three clients is documented in **those ADR rows**, not repeated here — a reader who takes this bullet as an inventory of `apps/` will miss most of the current client code
- `infra/` — single-VPS Hetzner deployment via systemd + system-nginx + system-MariaDB; see `infra/README.md` (migration from lakko_me and daily ops). **SFU deploy (ADR-0011 Rust SFU, co-located on the main VPS)**: `infra/systemd/lakko-sfu.service` runs `lakko-svc-sfu` (env-var configured from `lakko.env`, in `lakko.target`); `infra/nginx/sites-available/sfu.conf` fronts `sfu.lakko.me` (`/v1/connect` WS + `/health` `/ready` `/version` + mTLS `/metrics` → `127.0.0.1:9641`); the UDP media plane (`LAKKO_SFU_MEDIA_BIND`, default `0.0.0.0:43210`) is a direct public port (not proxied), and **`LAKKO_SFU_MEDIA_ADVERTISE_IP` must name the public IP**: an unspecified bind cannot be an ICE host candidate, and until 2026-09 the SFU silently sent every SDP answer with no candidate at all, so no deployed media could connect — it now refuses to start instead. `just deploy` builds + installs the binary (deploy.sh `SERVICES` now includes `sfu` + `channels`); `just deploy-sfu` is the one-time setup (cert + vhost + UDP firewall); `just dev-sfu` / `restart-sfu` / `logs-sfu`. The stale LiveKit-fork infra (ADR-0008 — `livekit.yaml`, go build, separate CCX22) was retired. See `infra/README-calls.md`

`GOLDEN_WIRE_HEX` is the name it carries in `lakko-calls`, `lakko-sframe-native` and the web test), even with a non-empty host additional_data, so the native media path is byte-identical across stacks — G-26), wired via `just test-sframe-bridge` and `.github/workflows/sframe-bridge.yml` + channel rotation policy C-54: pure `RotationPolicy::should_rotate(self, now_ms, state)` in `crates/lakko-channels/src/rotation_policy.rs` (a method on the policy, not a three-argument free function) (24 h / 1 000 posts default, both independently disable-able, saturating arithmetic on the time delta tolerates host-clock jumps backward) + web / iOS / Android orchestration driving the fanout — since [ADR-0030](docs/adr/0030-batched-rotation-fanout.md) that is the two-phase `POST /rotate/stage` (bounded chunks) then one atomic `POST /rotate/commit`, not the single-shot `POST /v1/channels/:id/rotate` this bullet named, which still exists server-side but is no longer what the cron calls — `apps/web/src/channels/rotation-cron.ts` + `apps/ios/Lakko/Channels/RotationCron.swift` (Swift `actor`) + `apps/android/app/src/main/kotlin/me/lakko/app/channels/RotationCron.kt` (Kotlin coroutine driver), all reading the same UniFFI / wasm-bindgen policy decision so the cadence cannot drift between platforms) + SFU multi-party client SFrame coverage + cross-platform AAD C-55 (ADR-0013): all three SFU clients — web `RTCRtpScriptTransform` worker, native iOS/Android `SfuCallSession` — seal media end-to-end through the one `lakko_calls::sframe` `seal_framed` / `open_framed` core;
